# House History Dossier — 1822 Pine Street

Synthesized 27 August 2026 from a twelve-lens research pass (~1,160 source
lookups). Raw lens returns are in `research/deep-2026-08/`.

**Nothing here is publishable until re-verified against its cited source.**
Findings carry the researchers' own A/B/C grades. Where two lenses disagree,
the disagreement is preserved rather than resolved — publish the conflict or
publish nothing.

## Independently re-verified before any of this was written

Pulled byte-for-byte from Internet Archive OCR during the session, not taken
on trust:

```
1857 McElroy's:  Roset John, mer., 756 Pine
1858 McElroy's:  Roset John, nier., 1822 Pine     (nier. = mer., OCR artifact)
1859 McElroy's:  Broadhead John, 1822 Pine
1871 Gopsill's:  Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine
```

Establishing (a) the 756 → 1822 renumbering, proven with a single household as
its own control across consecutive volumes; (b) Howard Spencer as a real
resident in 1871, not a mis-transcription; (c) Roset gone from the house by
1859.

## Already applied to the site

`story-chapters.json` published "Graham Spencer died on June 28, 1896." That
date appears nowhere in `verified-facts.json`. It is now understood to be
**Howard Spencer's** death date — the right date on the wrong man. Both
instances were reduced to "he died in 1896" pending confirmation of the
November obituary.

---



# EXTRACT A — Archive map, newspaper sweep, families, block neighbours

1822 PINE STREET — CONSOLIDATED EXTRACT FROM LENSES 00, 03, 09, 10
Sources: 00_agent0.txt (archive map) · 03_agent3.txt (free newspaper sweep) · 09_agent9.txt (block-neighbour / city-directory lens) · 10_agent10.txt (people & genealogy lens)
All grades, URLs and quotes below are reproduced as the source files carry them. Nothing has been added, re-researched, or upgraded.

1. GRADE A FINDINGS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

── ERA: THE ROSET / ROZET FAMILY AND ORIGINS ──

• [A] John Roset, merchant, is documented at the house (as 756 Pine) from the 1855 directory volume — the first independent confirmation of a fact the site rests entirely on an unread, upside-down deed card. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1855amce/page/n495/mode/1up?q=%22756+Pine%22
  Quote: "Roset  John,  mer.,  756  Pine" — McElroy's 1855, leaf 495.

• [A] The same man, same trade, in the next volume after renumbering. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1858amce/page/n612/mode/1up?q=%22Roset+John%22
  Quote: "Roset  John,  nier.,  1822  Pine" — McElroy's 1858, leaf 612 ("nier." is OCR for "mer.").

• [GRADE A] John Roset's obituary — he did NOT die at 1822 Pine. Evening Telegraph (Philadelphia), Wed Aug 10, 1870, p.8. This is the archive's own [UNREAD] item `the-evening-telegraph-1870-08-10-page-8` (npcom 85300150). (03)
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1870-08-10/ed-1/seq-8/
  LOC: https://www.loc.gov/resource/sn83025925/1870-08-10/ed-1/?sp=8
  Image crop (verified by eye): https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/iiif/batch_pst_grange_ver01%2Fdata%2Fsn83025925%2F00280776294%2F1870081001%2F0703.jp2/1379,5839,652,380/1720,/0/default.jpg
  Quote: "John Roset, Esq., died yesterday at West Philadelphia, in the seventy-sixth year of his age. In his active days Mr. Roset was a prominent merchant."

• [GRADE A] The same obituary names the sons-in-law and the father's forename. (03)
  Same URLs as above.
  Quote: "Mr. Roset's daughters married several of our most prominent citizens, among whom are A. J. Drexel, Dr. J. L. Ludlow, and John A. Brodhead."
  Quote (father): "His father, Jacob Roset, having attracted the notice of the Emperor Joseph II, of Austria, was educated at the Imperial Military Academy at Pres[s]burg, Hungary."

• [A] The obituary has a known headline, from a City of Philadelphia nomination footnote — you know what the unread clipping says before opening it. (10)
  https://www.phila.gov/media/20220113090440/32-Manheim-St-reclassification.pdf (footnote 22)
  Quote: "'Death of an Estimable and Valuable Citizen,' The Evening Telegraph, 10 August 1870, 8."

• [A] Jacob Roset's origin, from an official City of Philadelphia historic-district nomination (Manheim Square, July 2020), p.25. (10)
  https://www.phila.gov/media/20220113090440/32-Manheim-St-reclassification.pdf
  Quote: "Jacob Roset was born as Jean Jacques Rozet to Simon Louis Marie Rozet, a régisseur or local Collector of the Royal Customs, and Elizabeth Cousset in July 1764 at Lyons, France."

• [A] Jacob Roset's marriage and issue, same nomination, p.25. (10)
  Quote: "Their union would produce at least four children: John Roset (1794–1870), who married Mary Ann Laning (1807–1880); Eliza Margaret Roset (b. 1797)… Catherine Roset (1802/3–1821); and Jacob Roset, Jr. (1807–1862)."
  Footnote 28, verbatim: "John Roset's daughter married Anthony J. Drexel."

• [A] John Roset's firm and trade, from his Evening Bulletin obituary as quoted in the nomination, p.30. (10)
  https://www.phila.gov/media/20220113090440/32-Manheim-St-reclassification.pdf
  Quote: "The successive houses of Jacob Roset, of Roset & Son, and finally Roset & Bicking, occupied for many years a conspicuous and honorable position among the large commission dry goods houses of this city."
  Cited there as: "Obituary," Philadelphia Evening Bulletin, 10 August 1870.

• [A] A Historical Society of Pennsylvania collection nobody in the repo knew about, its date range bracketing the 1854 purchase. (10)
  Quote: "Roset Family Papers (1794–1857), Collection 1897, Historical Society of Pennsylvania."

• [A] Rembrandt Peale portraits of the couple exist. Nomination p.37 figure captions. (10)
  https://www.phila.gov/media/20220113090440/32-Manheim-St-reclassification.pdf
  Quote: "Figure 44. Center Left: A portrait of John Jacob Roset in 1829 by Rembrandt Peale (1778–1860). Figure 45. Center right: A portrait of Mary Ann Lanning Roset in 1829 by Rembrandt Peale."

• [A] John Roset's full issue, giving dates to two daughters the archive had as "No dates known." (10)
  https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/132853253/john-roset
  Quote: "-- Elizabeth Virginia Rozet (1827–1915), m. John Brodhead -- Ellen Bicking Rozet (1829–1897), m. Anthony J. Drexel -- George Hollenback Rozet (1829–1900), m. Josephine Mandeville."
  Also establishes John Roset is buried at Woodlands Cemetery, not Laurel Hill.

• [A for family structure / B for the link to this house] Mary Rozet Smith is John Roset's granddaughter. (10)
  https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/228411953/sarah-emily-smith
  Quote: "Sarah Emily Rozet Smith / BIRTH 28 Jan 1839 … Parents / John Roset 1794–1870 … Children / Charles Frederic Mather-Smith 1863–1941 / Mary Rozet Smith 1868–1934 / Francis Drexel Smith 1874–1956."
  Corroboration: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mary_Rozet_Smith · https://www.wikitree.com/wiki/Rozet-1 · Jane Addams Digital Edition, https://digital.janeaddams.ramapo.edu/items/show/960 — "Francis Drexel Smith was the brother of Mary Rozet Smith."

── ERA: WHO ACTUALLY OCCUPIED THE HOUSE, 1855–1893 ──

• [A] The complete tenancy succession at 1822 Pine, volume by volume, from the McElroy's/Gopsill's directories. Bench item 20 is confirmed outright. (09)
  1855, 1856, 1857 — "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine"
  1858 — "Roset  John,  nier.,  1822  Pine"
  1859 — "Broadhead  John,  1822  Pine" — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1859amce/page/n105/mode/1up?q=%22Broadhead+John%22 (leaf 105)
  1860 — "M'Neill  Thomas  E.,  engineer,  414  Walnut,  h  1822  Pine" and "M'Neill  Virginia,  widow,  1822  Pine" (leaf 691)
  1862, 1864, 1865 — "Smith  S.  Decatur,  Kensington  glassworks,  35½ S  Front,  h  1822  Pine" (1862, leaf 673)
  1865 also — "Cuthbert  Mary,  1822  Pine"
  1867 — "Trimble  James,  grocer,  505  Market,  h  1822  Pine" — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1867amce/page/n932/mode/1up?q=%22Trimble+James%22 (leaf 932)
  1871 — "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine"

• [GRADE A] S. Decatur Smith at 1822 Pine in November 1866 — earliest confirmed occupant name found in any newspaper, and not a Roset. Evening Telegraph, Sat Nov 17, 1866, p.8 (Fourth Edition). (03)
  https://www.loc.gov/resource/sn83025925/1866-11-17/ed-1/?sp=8
  PSU: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1866-11-17/ed-1/seq-8/
  Image crop (verified by eye): https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/iiif/batch_pst_ewing_ver01%2Fdata%2Fsn83025925%2F00280776191%2F1866111701%2F0576.jp2/4536,1747,580,103/1720,/0/default.jpg
  Quote: "Smith, S. Decatur, No. 1822 Pine [street]"
  Context: a Fairbanks & Ewing full-page advertisement headed "REFERENCE. To some of the First Families in Philadelphia who have had the Willcox & Gibbs Machines in Use for Five Years and Upwards."

• [A] Howard Spencer documented at 1822 Pine in 1871 — Gopsill's Philadelphia City Directory for 1871, leaf 1354. Resolves the Spencer naming problem. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost/page/n1354/mode/1up?q=%22Spencer+Howard%22
  Quote: "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine"
  And four years earlier, same man, same office, different house: "Spencer  How.ird,  pres.  A.  K.  Co.,  233  S  3d,  h  Burlington,  X  J" — McElroy's 1867.

• [A, with a dating caveat] A second directory line placing Howard Spencer at the house, and giving his company. (10)
  https://archive.org/download/boydscopartnersh01boyd/boydscopartnersh01boyd_djvu.txt (line 138433)
  Quote: "Spencer  Howard,  i)res  Black  Creek  Improvement / Co,  h  1822  Pine"
  CAVEAT carried by the source: IA dates this volume 1858 and that is demonstrably wrong; internal evidence brackets it c. 1894–1900. Do not publish a year for this citation until the title page is read (imagecount 1682).

── ERA: THE SPENCER FAMILY, 1891–1921 ──

• [A] Graham Spencer's obituary, resolving the naming problem outright. Wilmington (Del.) Evening Journal, Tue Nov 24, 1896, p.2. (03)
  https://www.loc.gov/resource/sn85042354/1896-11-24/ed-1/?sp=2
  Image crop (verified by eye): https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:ndnp:deu:batch_deu_bear_ver01:data:sn85042354:00383343380:1896112401:0869/1900,5240,900,940/full/0/default.jpg
  Quote: "GRAHAM SPENCER DEAD. HE DEVELOPED THE KAOLIN MINES, AT HOCKESSIN… The funeral of Graham Spencer… took place from the residence of his relatives, at No. 1822 Pine street, Philadelphia, this morning, at 11 o'clock."
  Second quote from the same obituary: "Mr. Spencer was 44 years old and was the son of Howard Spencer, who was the pioneer in the kaolin and spar industry in this section."

• [A] The Spencer family structure, from Find a Grave. (10)
  https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/74603329/howard-spencer
  Quote: "Howard Spencer / BIRTH 26 Feb 1884 … DEATH 20 Apr 1891 (aged 7) … BURIAL Laurel Hill East Cemetery … PLOT Sec E, Plot 37-48 / Parents / Graham Spencer 1852–1896 / Agnes Mary Smith Spencer 1862–1921."
  Siblings listed verbatim: "Arthur Donaldson Spencer 1885–1921 / Graham Spencer 1889–1931 / Agnes Martha Spencer Dixon 1890–1975 / Margaret Howard Spencer Potter 1891–1960 / Anna Howard 'Ann' Spencer Shaw 1894–1969 / Christine Spencer Hunter 1896–1976."

• [A] Graham Spencer died 21 November 1896, aged 44; his father was Howard. (10)

• [A] The Spencer women had left 1822 Pine by September 1914, and there are two Agnes M. Spencers. Evening Public Ledger, Mon Sep 14, 1914, p.9. (03)

  Quote: "SPENCER.OnA 20, at 1822 Pine scarlet fever, Spencer, Jr., eldest of … Aged 7 years … Interment private."
  The child's given name is NOT legible in the OCR — only "Spencer, Jr."

── ERA: THE 1899 REMODEL ──

• [A] Duhring, Okie & Ziegler named as architects for the alterations. Philadelphia Inquirer, 1899-07-18, p.12 (npcom 168342170). (00)
  Quote: "…chitects Duhring, Okie & Ziegler. The same architects have finished plans for alterations and additions to Mrs. Graham Spencer's residence, at 1822 Pine street."

• [A] The contract award and the cost. Philadelphia Times, 1899-08-06, p.10 (npcom 53320902). (00)
  Quote: "…for the alterations and additions to the residence of Mrs. Agnes Spencer at 1822 Pine street to Contractor Rogers, of Stanwick, N.J. … Cost about $6,000."
  Firm order is printed in this notice as "Okie, Duhring & Ziegler."

── ERA: THE EDWARDIAN AND DAVIS HOUSE, 1901–1918 ──

• [A] Mrs. Graham Spencer's seasonal return. Philadelphia Times, 1901-01-11, p.6 (npcom 53400433). (00)
  Quote: "…try house, returned to 1822 Pine street for the winter." Column context identifies Mrs. Graham Spencer and her country house at Kaolin, Chester County.

• [A] The 1901 wedding breakfast — with the address digit in doubt. Philadelphia Inquirer, 1901-11-21, p.16 (npcom 168316181). (00)
  Quote: "…son Plumb. Mr. J. J. Sullivan and Mr. H. J. Sullivan. After the ceremony a breakfast was served at the residence of the bride's parents, IS22 Pine street."
  The address reads "IS22", not "1822".

• [A] The Davis household's first documented appearance. Philadelphia Inquirer, 1904-12-11, p.36 (npcom 169003334). (00)
  Quote: "At home—Mrs. Henry C. Davis and the Misses Davis, of 1822 Pine street."

• [A] The 1905 fireplace permit — taken out for an unexplained party. Philadelphia Inquirer, 1905-08-04, p.6 (npcom 168348476). (00)
  Quote: "J N Leng, fire place, 1822 Pine st. fer G C Purviss: cost $130."

• [A] Henry Corbit Davis died 7 February 1901 — and was Lucretia Mott's grandson. (10)

── ERA: THE HOUSE NEXT DOOR, 1820 PINE ──

• [GRADE A] Mrs. Lawrence Lewis at 1820 Pine funds the 1915 Philadelphia canvass. Evening Public Ledger, Fri Feb 19, 1915, p.2. (03)

• [GRADE A] The same woman on the picket line, and publicly disowned by the conservative wing. Evening Public Ledger, Thu Jun 21, 1917, p.2. (03)
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1917-06-21/ed-1/seq-2/
  Quote: "'Mrs. Lawrence Lewis is not a relative of mine,' said Mrs. Lawrence Lewi[s] Smith, of Strafford… 'and I do not wish to be confused with her because I highly disapprove the action of the Alice Paul faction.'"

• [GRADE A] Her message smuggled out of jail. Evening Public Ledger, Fri Nov 23, 1917, p.13. (03)
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1917-11-23/ed-1/seq-13/
  Quote: "MRS. LAWRENCE LEWIS EXPLAINS PICKETING. Message Justifying Militant Activities Smuggled Out of Washington Jail. … Weakened by hunger-striking and forcible feeding, Mrs. Lawrence Lewis, of Philadelphia, defended White House picketing during the war."

• [GRADE A] 1820 Pine converted to apartments by autumn 1919 — twenty-two years before the site's 1941 zoning stamp. Evening Public Ledger, Tue Sep 30, 1919, p.22 (reruns Oct 6, 7, 9, 1919). (03)
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1919-09-30/ed-1/seq-22/
  Quote: "HOUSEKEEPING APARTMENTS. FINEST IN PHILADELPHIA. 1820 PINE STREET, with all the latest conveniences. One large living room with a beautifully designed open fireplace to burn logs furnished by the owner."

• [GRADE A] The same building at $125/month two years later. Evening Public Ledger, Fri Feb 25, 1921, p.28. (03)
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1921-02-25/ed-1/seq-28/
  Quote: "1820 PINE STREET. Mo[de]rn apartment[s], cheerful and […] ideal for [sm]al[l] families and [ba]chelors; ever[y] conven[i]ence… $125 per month. ALBERT B. MILLS, WEST END TRUST BLDG."

• [GRADE A, minor] Two more block residents, April 1916. Evening Public Ledger, Fri Apr 14, 1916, p.17. (03)
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1916-04-14/ed-1/seq-17/
  Quote: "James Gay Gordon, Jr., 1820 Pine street, Philadelphia, is planning a trout fishing trip… Dr. John R. Jopson, 1824 Pine street, Phil[a]delphia, will go upstate in a few days to angle for trout."

── THE BLOCK NEIGHBOURS (all confirmed from directories that carry their own identification) ──

• [A] James Curtis Booth, 1813 Pine — melter and refiner of the U.S. Mint. Appears 1859, 1860, 1862, 1864; absent 1858 and earlier; gone by 1865. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1860amce/page/n120/mode/1up?q=%22melter+%26+refiner%22
  Quote: "Booth  James  C,  melter  &  refiner,  U.  S.  Mint,  h  1813  Pine" — McElroy's 1860, leaf 120.

  Quote: "B[ooth] (Professor of Chemistry in U. S. Mint), No. 1813 Pine street" — and on the same page, "H[az]lehurst, Isaac, No. 18[3]6 Pine street."

• [A] William Parker Foulke, 1827 Pine — living there in 1858, the year of the Hadrosaurus dig. Called by the source "the single strongest neighbor finding." (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1858amce/page/n248/mode/1up?q=%22FOULKE+WM.+PARKER%22

  Widow, 1871: https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost/page/n577/mode/1up?q=%22wid+William+P.%22 — "Foulde Julia, wid William P., h 1827 Pine."
  Corroboration [B]: https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/48622517/william-parker-foulke — "He discovered the first full dinosaur skeleton in North America (Hadrosaurus foulkii…) in Haddonfield, New Jersey in 1858."

• [A] Henry Coppée, 1826 Pine — two doors west, on the same side, 1857 through 1864, and the only candidate on 1822's side of the street. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1857amce/page/n161/mode/1up?q=%22760+Pine%22
  Quote (pre-renumbering): "Coppee  Henry,  prof.  Penn.  University,  760  Pine" — McElroy's 1857, leaf 161.
  Quote (post-renumbering): "Coppee  Hen.,  prof.  Penn.  University,  1826  Pine" — McElroy's 1859.

• [A for address; B for the Gettysburg identification] Chapman Biddle, 1831 Pine, continuously 1858–1871 at least. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1858amce/page/n72/mode/1up?q=%22BIDDLE+CHAPMAN%22
  Quote: "BIDDLE  CHAPMAN,  atty.  &  coun.  131  S  5th,  h  1831  Pine" — McElroy's 1858, leaf 72.
  Widow's death record: https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/102878937/mary-livingston-biddle — "Mary Livingston Cochran Biddle… died April 4th 1894. Address: 1831 Pine St, Philadelphia 7th Ward."

• [A] Joseph W. Drexel, 1829 Pine — arriving c. 1866–67, not in the 1850s/60s Roset era. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost/page/n495/mode/1up?q=%22Drexel+Joseph+W%22
  Quote: "Drexel Joseph W. (Drexel & Co.), h 1829 Pine" — Gopsill's 1871, leaf 495.
  Predecessors at 1829 Pine: "Quin Joseph P., 248 Chestnut, h 1829 Pine" (1858); "Ashmead Thomas E., merchant, from 1432 Chestnut to 1829 Pine" (1860).

• [A] Maj. Gen. George Gordon Meade, 1836 Delancey Place, one block north, c. 1864/65–1872. (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1865amce/page/n505/mode/1up?q=%22MEADE+GEORGE+G%22
  Quote: "MEADE  GEORGE  G.,  Major-Gen.  U.  S.  A.,  1836  De  Lancey  pi" — McElroy's 1865, leaf 505.
  https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost/page/n1029/mode/1up?q=%22maj.+gen%22 — "Meade George G., maj. gen. u.s.a., S Broad n Walnut, h 1836 De Lancey pl" (Gopsill's 1871, leaf 1029).

• [A] Charles Short, 1808 Pine, 1858–1862 — co-author of "Lewis and Short." Described by the source as "a genuinely new find and probably the most publishable of the extensions." (09)
  https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1858amce/page/n645/mode/1up?q=%22private+classical+school%22

  Identification [A — scholarly reference database]: https://dbcs.rutgers.edu/all-scholars/9118-short-charles-lancaster — "master of own school (Philadelphia), 1853-63; pres. & prof, mental & moral philos. Kenyon Coll., 1863-7; prof. Lat. Columbia Coll., 1867-86."

• [A] Commodore David Geisinger, USN, 742 Pine (= 1808 Pine), 1855–1857 — the same house Charles Short took in 1858. (09)

• [A] Archibald McElroy, 1806 Pine — the directory publisher renumbering his own house in his own book. (09)

  Quote: "Hart  John  S.,  principal  of  the  High  School,  Broad  &  Green,  h  1828  Pine" — McElroy's 1858.

── THE RENUMBERING ──

• [A] The +1066 renumbering is now proven from a single pair of directory volumes, with sixteen households converting in lockstep between the 1857 and 1858 volumes — better evidence than the eight-household table currently in the repo. (09)
  734 Duane → 1800 · 736 Hockley → 1802 · 738 Packard → 1804 · 740 McElroy → 1806 · 742 Geisinger → 1808 · 744 Siter → 1810 · 746 Fling → 1812 · 748 Rev. Jenkins → 1814 · 750 Kester → 1816 · 754 Hone → 1820 · 756 ROSET → 1822 · 758 Barnes → 1824 · 760 Coppée → 1826 · 766 Robeno → 1832 · 768 Taylor → 1834 · 770 Hazlehurst → 1836.

── THE MODERN CHAIN OF TITLE (all [A], from deed OCR) ──

• [A] 1979-10-17 deed: Serota → Goodwin. (00)
  Quote: "BEING the same premises which Mary G. Sharpe Wilkes, widow by Deed dated May 1, 1967 and recorded … in Deed Book C.A.D. #972 page 304 … granted and conveyed unto Benjamin Serota and Shirley W. Serota, his wife, in fee."
• [A] 1985-03-15 deed: Kosmin/Goodwin/Pine Associates → Kosmin and Stango Partnership, $73,000. Carries the legal description: south side of Pine, 230 feet west of Eighteenth, Eighth Ward, 22 ft front × 90 ft deep to Waverly. (00)
• [A] 1987-06-26 Indenture: Kosmin and Stango Partnership → grantee; recital deed 3/15/1985, recorded 3/18/1985, Deed Book FHS 100 page 326. (00)
• [A] 1999 deed: grantor Thomas Jay Bushyager, who took by Indenture dated June 26, 1987. Quote: "AND the said Thomas Jay Bushyager, departed this life on April 26, 1991 wherein John R.S. Bushyager…" (00)
• [A] 2002 deed: Stephen A. Mione and Tralan Muntean → ROLAND K. BULLARD, II and SALLY S. BULLARD, deed 08/08/2002, recorded 08/14/2002, Document No. 50510515. Trident Land Transfer Company. (00)
• [A, unexplained] "Woodhead" — recovered from the rotated 1893 deed card. Quote: "TRANSFER. PH. WOODHEAD." Appears nowhere else in the repo. (00)
• [A] The only legible text in the entire 1922–1952 deed-card run: a stamp reading "ZONING SEP 11 1941 REGISTERED" on 1941-deed-abstract-2. (00)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
2. CORRECTIONS — WHERE THE SITE OR REPO PUBLISHES SOMETHING WRONG
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

PEOPLE AND DATES

C-1. Graham Spencer's death date.
  Wrong (story-chapters.json): "Graham Spencer died June 28, 1896."
  Correct: 28 June 1896 is his FATHER Howard Spencer's death date (Atlantic City, aged 75). Graham died 21 November 1896 in Philadelphia, aged 44; the funeral was held at 1822 Pine on the morning of Nov 24.
  Evidence: Find a Grave 74953709 and 74953367 (10); Wilmington Evening Journal obituary, Nov 24, 1896, p.2, image-verified (03).

C-2. The Howard/Graham renaming instruction.
  Wrong (DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT, flagged as a "CRITICAL CORRECTION"): rename the patriarch Howard → Graham throughout.
  Correct: both men are real and they are father and son. Howard Spencer (1821–1896) is the kaolin/feldspar pioneer at Hockessin, Delaware and the earlier resident; Graham Spencer (1852–1896) is his son. CONTENT-OPPORTUNITIES #17 is right; the DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT instruction is wrong.
  Evidence: the Wilmington obituary — "was the son of Howard Spencer, who was the pioneer in the kaolin and spar industry" (03); Gopsill's 1871 "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine" (09); Find a Grave parent/child records (10). Corroborating detail: "Anne **Heward** Spencer" in 1921 (03).

C-3. The 1896 clipping assignments are inverted.
  Wrong (00, and the repo's own flag): 1896-06-29 Times p.5 is "very likely his [Graham's] obituary."
  Correct: `the-philadelphia-times-1896-06-29-page-5` (day after 28 June) is almost certainly HOWARD Sr.'s death notice; `the-philadelphia-times-1896-11-23-page-11` (two days after 21 Nov) is almost certainly GRAHAM's. Read both, June first (10). 03 independently calls 1896-11-23 "now the single highest-value unread clipping in the archive."

C-4. Henry C. Davis's death date.
  Wrong (00 Part 3): "Death date unknown; before May 1918."
  Correct: 7 February 1901. Which makes "Mrs. Henry C. Davis and the Misses Davis" (Dec 1904) a widow three years into her widowhood.
  Evidence: https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/66070050/henry_corbit-davis and the Fair Hill register line "Davis | Henry C. | 1901, 02/07" (10).

C-5. Ellen Rozet Drexel's birth year, and the "twin."
  Wrong: "Ellen Bicking Rozet Drexel (Feb 21, 1829 – Nov 27, 1891)… twin of George Hollenback Roset," with instruction to flag the 1829/1832 dispute in-line.
  Correct: publish 1832, footnote the 1829 variant, and DELETE "twin" — do not merely flag the year. "Feb 21, 1829" is George's exact birth date to the day; on the best data George is 1829 and Ellen is 1832, so they are not twins.
  Evidence: Find a Grave 13162046 (structured header "BIRTH 1832"), Find a Grave 132795075 (George, "BIRTH 21 Feb 1829"), Drexel University exhibit https://drexelexhibits.omeka.net/exhibits/show/drexel-family-2023/item/182 — "Ellen Rozet Drexel (1832-1891)". The 1829 date traces to WikiTree Rozet-1 and Find a Grave free-text bio prose (10).

C-6. Elizabeth Virginia Brodhead's dates.
  Wrong: "No dates known."
  Correct: Elizabeth Virginia Rozet (1827–1915), m. John Brodhead. Evidence: https://www.findagrave.com/memorial/132853253/john-roset (10). The 1870 obituary independently names "John A. Brodhead" as a son-in-law (03).

C-7. Jacob Roset described as "French-Hungarian."
  Wrong: "Jean Jacques Rozet (1764/65–1850), French-Hungarian dry-goods merchant."
  Correct per the source: "French-born at Lyons, a refugee to Vienna, educated in Hungary, in Philadelphia from c. 1792." Also, the 1870 obituary names him as "Jacob Roset," which should be quoted as printed alongside the genealogical form.
  Evidence: phila.gov Manheim Square nomination p.25 (10); Evening Telegraph obituary (03).

C-8. John Roset's place and date of death.
  Wrong (site framing): implied death at the family home; repo date Aug 8, 1870.
  Correct: he "died yesterday at West Philadelphia" per the Aug 10 paper — i.e. Aug 9, 1870, not Aug 8. The source instructs: flag the one-day discrepancy.
  Evidence: Evening Telegraph, Aug 10, 1870, p.8, image-verified (03).

C-9. "Roset family home, 1854–1893."
  Wrong: the site's framing for the whole 39-year span.
  Correct: John Roset appears at the house for four volumes only (1855–1858) and is gone by 1859, eleven years before his death. The framing "is wrong for roughly thirty of its thirty-nine years." Occupancy was a tenancy succession: Roset → Broadhead → the McNeills → S. Decatur Smith → Mary Cuthbert → James Trimble → Howard Spencer.
  Evidence: the volume-by-volume directory run (09, Part 5d); S. Decatur Smith at 1822 Pine in Nov 1866 (03); John Roset dying in West Philadelphia (03).

C-10. Joseph W. Drexel's dates at 1829 Pine.
  Wrong (00 implies a Roset-era adjacency): listed as a [LEAD] neighbour without dates.
  Correct: he arrives c. 1866–67, NOT in the 1850s/60s. Before him: Joseph P. Quin (1858), Thomas E. Ashmead (1860 through 1865). Evidence: McElroy's 1867 and Gopsill's 1871 (09).

C-11. S. Decatur Smith's occupation — CONFLICT BETWEEN TWO LENSES, DO NOT SILENTLY PICK ONE.
  Repo/bench list: "S. Decatur Smith the glassware manufacturer."
  03 says: unsupported; contemporary papers make him an iron founder ("SMITH & ELLIS… Union Iron Foundry, Kensington," Evening Telegraph May 11, 1867, p.5, https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1867-05-11/ed-1/seq-5/) and an amateur composer ("the music by S. Decatur Smith, who is a promising composer," The Press, May 22, 1865, p.2, https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn84026296/1865-05-22/ed-1/?sp=2). Graded B, "linking the man at 1822 Pine (Nov 1866) to the foundry partner (May 1867) is an inference." Instruction: "'glassware manufacturer' should come off the bench list until sourced."
  09 says [A]: the directory itself prints "Smith  S.  Decatur,  Kensington  glassworks,  35½ S  Front,  h  1822  Pine" (McElroy's 1862, leaf 673).
  Both are Kensington. The lenses do not reconcile this; it must be presented as printed in each source.

C-12. Naomi Lawton Davis's burial.
  Not previously known: her ashes were interred 16 January 1945 — twenty-six and a half years after her death on 21 May 1918. Fair Hill register: "Davis | Naomi Lawton | 1945, 01/16 | | 75 | Ashes 4'. Cremated. Died 5/21/18." (10)

C-13. Agnes Mary Smith Spencer's death.
  The repo carries "(1862–1921)". The Oct 29, 1921 Evening Public Ledger speaks of "Mrs. Graham Spencer, of this city and Bermuda" as living (03). Resolved by 10: she died 24 Dec 1921 in Somerset, Sandys Parish, Bermuda.

C-14. Do not cite PAB for the architects.
  Wrong practice: attributing Duhring/Okie/Ziegler on the strength of the Athenaeum's database.
  Correct: "PAB's record lists **no architect at all** — the Duhring/Okie/Ziegler attribution rests entirely on the two 1899 newspaper notices already in the archive, not on the Athenaeum." (10)

C-15. Okie's Betsy Ross House / Pennsbury Manor framing.
  Wrong (site): "Betsy Ross House architects" applied to Duhring, Okie & Ziegler.
  Correct: Betsy Ross House 1936–37 and Pennsbury Manor 1936–40 "were OKIE ALONE, after the firm dissolved in 1918." (00)

C-16. The 1899 contractor's name.
  As printed in the Philadelphia Times: "Contractor Rogers, of Stanwick, N.J."
  CONTENT-OPPORTUNITIES corrects to J. S. RODGERS, awarded 2 August 1899. (00)

C-17. The 1899 renovation cost.
  Wrong (site and older material, in places): $25,000, which "has no source."
  Correct: $6,000, per the Philadelphia Times, 1899-08-06 — "Cost about $6,000." (00)

C-18. The 1867-04-08 Evening Telegraph clipping does not concern this house — cross it off.
  It is a medical-electricity advertisement; the line reads "M. Errickson, No. **1322** Pine street." The Apr 17 rerun OCRs as "No. 1S22 Pine street," which is what a keyword sweep flags. Image-verified.
  https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/iiif/batch_pst_ewing_ver01%2Fdata%2Fsn83025925%2F00280776208%2F1867041701%2F0731.jp2/2907,3747,608,102/1720,/0/default.jpg
  Page: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1867-04-17/ed-1/seq-5/ (03)

C-19. Fabricated archive synopses.
  Wrong (document-archive.json, 1913-02-09 and three sibling suffrage-era entries): "Suffrage movement gains momentum with meetings held in Rittenhouse Square homes, including 1822 Pine Street"; also synopses asserting meetings "in the parlors of prominent Philadelphia homes near Rittenhouse Square."
  Correct: these are generated prose, not derived from any document, and they violate the repo's own do-not-publish rule. Also wrong on the same grounds: the 1824-03-26 synopsis ("Pine Street neighborhood development near Rittenhouse Square") and the 1906/1907/1908 "Holiday entertaining at the Spencer residence" synopses, which are additionally chronologically doubtful since the Davises are the documented household by 1904. (00)

C-20. The 1891-11-28 clipping has three mutually incompatible descriptions in the repo.
  document-archive.json: "The Spencer family era begins at 1822 Pine Street." timeline.json: the death of Ellen Rozet Drexel. research/unverified-claims.md: the scarlet-fever death report. "All three are guesses. Neither can be true of the Spencer purchase, which the deed places in 1893." (00)

DATA AND STRUCTURE

C-21. Four modern deeds are filed under the wrong date (extracted_date taken from the "BEING the same premises" recital, i.e. the PREVIOUS conveyance). This mis-sorts /history/timeline and the archive index. (00)
  "1822 Pine - 1979 Deed.pdf" filed 1967-05-01 → true date Oct 17, 1979.
  "1822 Pine - 1985 Deed.pdf" filed 1979-10-17 → true date Mar 15, 1985.
  "1822 Pine - Deed 1987.pdf" filed 1985-03-15 → true date June 26, 1987.
  "1822 Pine - 1999 Deed 1.pdf" filed 1991-04-26 → true date 1999.
  The 1922/1947/1949/1952 "-01-01" dates are year-only placeholders; only 1941-09-11 is a real day, from the zoning stamp.

C-22. Four broken document citations in story-chapters.json — chapters point at slugs that do not exist. (00)
  "before-the-threshold", "architecture-and-fabric", "provenance-dossier" → `1854-deed-abstract` (real slug: `1854-deed-abstract-2`)
  "events-and-filming" → `the-philadelphia-inquirer-1901-11-21` (real slug: …-page-16)
  "the-seasonal-house" → `the-philadelphia-times-1901-01-11` (real slug: …-page-6)
  The 1854 deed is cited three times and links nowhere in all three.

C-23. The 2013 deed supplies six of the nineteen facts on /history/provenance but is not in document-archive.json, so those six citations render as plain text with a dead link. Either add it or move the facts. File: raw_documents/1822 Pine - 2013 Deed.pdf. (00)

C-24. Duplicate archive entries. `society-tea` and `the-philadelphia-times-1883-12-09-page-4` are the same Newspapers.com page (image ID 52185424) entered twice; the ten "-2"-suffixed floor-plan/deed pairs are the same drawing in two formats. (00)

C-25. Architectural style.
  Wrong (public/llms.txt, homepage, /history metadata): "Victorian mansion."
  Correct: the City's own 1995 Rittenhouse-Fitler inventory describes the row as "Nineteen, 4-story, 2 bay, brick, late Greek Revival townhouses." Listed as a blocker before any architecture page publishes. (00)

C-26. "Goosetown."
  Wrong (DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT): Rittenhouse Square's early character as "Goosetown."
  Correct: no checked source corroborates it; the sourced working-class name west of the Square is "Ramcat." (00)

C-27. Historic district registration number.
  Wrong (DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT): National Register listing April 27, 1992, #92001878.
  Correct: that number belongs to RittenhouseTown in Germantown, not this district, and is on the do-not-publish list. The Philadelphia Register date of February 8, 1995 is the one to use. (00) — PAB independently prints "Philadelphia Register of Historic Places -- 2/8/1995" for 1822 Pine (10).

C-28. Tax-resistance year.
  Wrong (older project material, and RP-Alt-Index.md still carries it): 1911.
  Corrected by DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT to December 1913 — but see GUARDRAIL G-3 below: the 1913 quote itself is on the do-not-publish list while /history/suffrage currently renders it as a full pull quote. This is "a live, shipped contradiction between the repo's own rule and its own page." (00)

C-29. The "Apartment House Years" opening.
  Wrong: the chapter begins with a 1941 zoning stamp.
  Correct: the block's apartment wave is documented next door at 1820 Pine twenty-two years earlier, September 1919. (03)

C-30. The renumbering table.
  Wrong/weak: the repo's eight-household conversion table.
  Better: a sixteen-household lockstep conversion between the 1857 and 1858 volumes, with John Roset as the proof line. "That is a publishable table, and it is better evidence than the eight-household table currently in the repo." (09)

C-31. Room and bath count. Site says 8 bedrooms / 6 baths, sleeps 16–18; OPA's record says 6BR/6BA. Unreconciled. (00)

C-32. Rosenbach dates (a Rittenhouse-area context page correction). (09)
  Corrected phrasing given verbatim by the source: "Philip and A.S.W. Rosenbach lived at 2006 Delancey Place from 1926, moved to 2010 Delancey Place in 1949, and the museum opened in that house in 1954; 2008 Delancey was added later." Also note this is the 2000 block of Delancey — two blocks west of 1822 Pine, not the 1800 block.
  Evidence: https://hiddencityphila.org/2017/06/with-bloomsday-near-remembering-the-rosenbach-brothers/ and https://www.rosenbach.org/about/

C-33. Do not publish "General Birney lived on the 1800 block of Delancey." Gopsill's 1871 reads "Birney D. B., wid David B., h 1810 Delancey pl" — that is his WIDOW, who moved there after his death in October 1864. The directories place his own household at 1920 Race throughout. (09)

C-34. The John Page Nicholson marker at 1805 Pine. The Wikipedia table flags its status as "Missing" — do not send a reader to look at it without checking. And Nicholson is commemorated there because the museum he founded was housed at 1805 Pine, NOT because he lived there. (09)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
3. RESOLVED QUESTIONS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

R-1. OPEN QUESTION 1 — THE SPENCER NAMING PROBLEM. RESOLVED, THREE WAYS.
  Howard and Graham Spencer are father and son, not one man mis-transcribed.
  Evidence: (a) Wilmington Evening Journal, Nov 24, 1896 — "was the son of Howard Spencer, who was the pioneer in the kaolin and spar industry," image-verified (03); (b) Gopsill's 1871 leaf 1354 — "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine," with McElroy's 1867 showing the same man at 233 S 3d living in Burlington, N.J. (09); (c) Find a Grave parent/child structured records for Howard 1821–1896, Graham 1852–1896, Agnes 1862–1921 (10); (d) the surname "Anne **Heward** Spencer" carried forward in 1921 (03).
  Consequence: the two 1896 clippings swap owners (see C-3).

R-2. OPEN QUESTION 3 — THE RENUMBERING. PROVEN, AND STRENGTHENED.
  1822 Pine was 756 Pine. Proven from one pair of consecutive directory volumes with sixteen households converting at a constant +1066, and John Roset himself as the proof line: "Roset  John,  mer.,  756  Pine" (1855) → "Roset  John,  nier.,  1822  Pine" (1858). Independent internal proof: the directory's own publisher moved 740 → 1806 in his own book. (09)

R-3. BENCH ITEM 20 — WAS 1859–1893 A TENANCY SUCCESSION? CONFIRMED OUTRIGHT.
  The volume-by-volume occupant list (09, Part 5d) plus two newspaper documents: S. Decatur Smith at 1822 Pine in Nov 1866 (03), and John Roset dying "at West Philadelphia" in Aug 1870 (03). 03 adds a corroborating negative: "Any Roset or Rozet within five words of 'Pine' in any Philadelphia paper 1857–1871 — zero hits… Meaningful, given the same corpus yields Smith at 1822 Pine and a full Roset obituary."

R-4. OPEN QUESTION 2 — THE BUILD DATE. RESOLVED IN FAVOUR OF 1854, INDEPENDENTLY. [A for the data; B for the inference]
  The 1853 volume's name section contains no Pine Street number between 709 and 817 — nothing in the 730–779 range at all. The 1854 volume shows 734, 736, 738, 740, 746, 748, 750, 752, 760 filling in from the 18th Street corner. The 1855 volume shows the block essentially full, with 756 (Roset) present. "1822 Pine (as 756) is first occupied in the volume for 1855. This is consistent with an April 1854 deed and inconsistent with 'c. 1845.'" Caveat carried by the source: negative OCR evidence over a 3 MB text; a spot-check of the 1853 volume's actual Pine Street pages would harden it. (09)

R-5. GAP 3'S CENTREPIECE — NAOMI DAVIS'S DEATH AT THE HOUSE. RESOLVED WITH THREE DOCUMENTS.
  The archive previously held NO document for an event the site narrates in two chapters. Now: Evening Public Ledger, May 22, 1918, p.18 (image-verified); New-York Tribune, May 23, 1918, p.7 (independent, out-of-town); and the Fair Hill register giving "Died 5/21/18" independently of any newspaper. First primary confirmation of her full name, that she died at this address, that Henry C. Davis predeceased her, and of "suddenly" plus the private funeral. (03, 10)

R-6. OPEN QUESTION 13 — ELLEN ROZET'S BIRTH YEAR. RESOLVED AS 1832, AND THE "TWIN" CLAIM DISSOLVED. See C-5. (10)

R-7. THE BLOCK-NEIGHBOUR IDENTIFICATIONS — ALL FIVE CANDIDATES CONFIRMED, AND THE REPO'S CAUTION ABOUT PUBLISHING DIRECTORY IDENTIFICATIONS IS PARTLY LIFTED.
  The repo warned: "the directory LINES are quotable verbatim; the IDENTIFICATIONS are not." 09 supplies the resolution: a directory line that carries its own occupational identifier ("melter & refiner, U. S. Mint"; "prof. Penn. University"; "maj. gen. u.s.a."; "(Drexel & Co.)") IS the identification, printed by the source. Booth [A], Foulke [A], Coppée [A], Biddle [A address / B Gettysburg], Joseph W. Drexel [A]. 03 additionally supplies a free, republishable non-directory source for Booth at 1813 Pine (the 1866 Willcox & Gibbs page).

R-8. WHICH SIDE OF THE STREET. Odd numbers are the north side, even the south — derived from the 1985 deed already in the archive ("south side of Pine, 230 feet west of Eighteenth"). Booth, Foulke, Drexel and Biddle were all ACROSS the street from 1822; Coppée was on the same side, two doors west. [B inference, "a safe one."] (09)

R-9. OPEN QUESTION 5 — DID SUFFRAGISTS MEET IN THIS HOUSE? REINFORCED AS "NO," AND NOW WITH THE TRAP IDENTIFIED.
  The single contemporary document reporting a suffrage meeting at Miss Martha Davis's residence prints **1823** Pine Street — the odd/north side, across the street — eleven months after her mother died at 1822 Pine. Read straight, Martha Davis had moved off the south side of the block by April 1919. Image-verified; the OCR reads "1123." (03)

R-10. OPEN QUESTION 14 — WHICH DAVIS WOMAN DID WHAT. PARTLY RESOLVED.
  Resolved: Henry C. Davis = Henry Corbit Davis, d. 7 Feb 1901; Naomi Lawton Davis = his second wife, d. 21 May 1918; his first wife was Martha Mellor Davis (1843–1874, m. 1862, d. at Nice). Lucy Davis's presence at the address and her Quakerism are independently confirmed. Still open: Lucy's and Martha's parentage (see S-6).

R-11. THE ARCHIVE'S 1870-08-10 CLIPPING IS IDENTIFIED BEFORE IT IS OPENED.
  Two lenses converge: 03 read the page image and transcribed it; 10 found the nomination footnote giving its headline — "'Death of an Estimable and Valuable Citizen,' The Evening Telegraph, 10 August 1870, 8." Two obituaries ran that day: this one and the Evening Bulletin's.

R-12. THE 1867-04-08 CLIPPING IS DISPOSED OF. Not about this house; the address is 1322 Pine. Image-verified. (03) — See C-18.

R-13. THE ATHENAEUM/PAB QUESTION IS ANSWERED. 1822 Pine IS in PAB as "Spencer Residence," client Agnes Spencer, dwelling — but with no architect on the record. (10)

R-14. WHY THE 1913 SUFFRAGE COVERAGE CANNOT BE FOUND FREE. Structural, not a search failure: the Evening Public Ledger's digitized run begins 1914-09-14, ten months too late, and no other free Philadelphia daily covers 1913. "Open Question #4 cannot be closed with this lens." (03)

R-15. WHY "756 PINE" RETURNS NOTHING IN NEWSPAPERS. Zero hits in the entire Penn State PA archive and all of Chronicling America. Structural: there is no free full-text Philadelphia daily for 1802–1856. "Do not run this search again." The renumbering key has to be worked in directories, deeds and tax ledgers. Confirmed independently by 10: "Boyd's directory searched for '756 Pine' — zero hits, as expected for a post-renumbering volume." (03, 10)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
4. STILL OPEN — AND THE SPECIFIC RECORD THAT WOULD SETTLE EACH
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

S-1. WHO OWNED THE HOUSE 1922–1967, AND WHO SIGNED THE 1854 DEED.
  Settled by: rotating the ten Philadelphia Bureau of Engineering deed-registry card JPGs 180° and re-OCRing them. The handwritten owner names, dates and deed-book references "are physically present in the scans and have simply never been read." Files in raw_documents/. Named by 00 as the single cheapest high-value task available. Would close 1922–1952 in one pass and give the 1854 McCrea→Roset conveyance a readable source.

S-2. DID AGNES SPENCER OWN THE HOUSE CONTINUOUSLY 1899–1921, MAKING THE DAVISES HER TENANTS?
  [B — labelled inference by 10.] Agnes took title 3 Apr 1899 and died 24 Dec 1921; the 1922 card carries "Spencer," a husband-and-wife pair, and a date fragment. "The obvious reading is an estate conveyance following her death." If true it also dissolves the G. C. Purviss problem into an ordinary owner-of-record-vs-occupant situation.
  Settled by: reading the rotated `1922-deed-abstract-1` card. "Do not publish this as fact until the rotated 1922 card is read — but it is the hypothesis to test first."

S-3. WHO IS G. C. PURVISS? Still unexplained. The 1905 fireplace — the site's most-repeated physical detail, invoked in six chapters — was permitted for him, not for any Davis, Spencer or Roset.
  Ruled out as a route: free newspapers. "'G. C. Purviss' — all zero in Pennsylvania papers… Purviss returns only unrelated 1845/1857/1889 items. The 1905 fireplace permit's mystery party will not be solved from free newspapers." (03)
  Settled by: S-2's 1922 card, or the Philadelphia building-permit series.

S-4. THE DECEMBER 1913 TAX-RESISTANCE QUOTE. Cannot be closed with free newspapers (see R-14). Settled by: Newspapers.com, or the NAWSA / Caroline Katzenstein papers. (03)

S-5. THE LOAD-BEARING JOIN: IS THE HENRY C. DAVIS OF 1822 PINE THE SAME AS HENRY CORBIT DAVIS, LUCRETIA MOTT'S GRANDSON?
  Stated honestly by 10: no document names *this* Henry C. Davis at 1822 Pine. The join is three circumstantial strands, and the load-bearing one (Naomi's 21 May 1918 death) leans on a repo date CONTENT-OPPORTUNITIES itself flags as poorly sourced — "so the argument is partly circular."
  Settled by, in one step: **Philadelphia Inquirer, 22 May 1918, page 17** — the exact citation from Naomi Davis's Find a Grave memorial. Same paper, same Newspapers.com workflow as the rest of the archive. It should also name the surviving children and settle Open Question 14 outright.

S-6. LUCY'S AND MARTHA'S PARENTAGE. [C — explicitly labelled speculation.] Neither appears in the Fair Hill register (all 3,573 rows searched; 26 Davis burials, none of them), in Woman's Who's Who beyond Lucy's own entry, or as children on either of Henry's Find a Grave marriages. The testable inference: Henry married Martha Mellor in 1862 and she died 1874; a Lucy who took a Vassar A.B. in 1885 was born c. 1863–65, inside the first marriage — which would make Naomi Lawton Davis their STEPmother.
  Settled by: the 22 May 1918 Inquirer obituary, or a census.

S-7. THE DATE OF THE BOYD'S VOLUME PLACING HOWARD SPENCER AT 1822 PINE. IA dates it 1858; internal evidence brackets it c. 1894–1900.
  Settled by: reading the title page from the page images (imagecount 1682) of `boydscopartnersh01boyd`. Do not publish a year for that citation until then. (10) — Note: 09's Gopsill's 1871 line independently supplies a firmly dated Howard Spencer occupancy, and 10 records that it "found NO support anywhere for that item's 'in 1871' date." The two lenses disagree on whether "1871" was sourced; 09's leaf-1354 link is the record that decides it.

S-8. "IS22" vs "1822" — THE 1901 ROLIN-PLUMB WEDDING. Until the digits are confirmed against the page image, the wedding is not proven to have happened at this address. 03's own methods note reinforces this: three of nine strongest OCR leads changed meaning at the image-crop step.
  Settled by: cropping the Newspapers.com page image, npcom 168316181.

S-9. THE 1900 SECOND CONSTRUCTION CAMPAIGN and the $1,200 Jacob Myres & Son permit. The archive holds an unread Inquirer clipping dated 1900-10-03 (npcom 168086532) sitting exactly on that window.
  Settled by: transcribing that clipping, and reading the Real Estate Record and Builders' Guide page image for 2 Aug 1899 p.489 (ten minutes, free) which may also carry the settled 1899 cost in the unread remainder after "A new story."

S-10. IS THE 1891-11-28 CLIPPING ANYTHING? Three incompatible repo descriptions, all guesses. Settled by: reading npcom 168413629.

S-11. THE 1871-01-14 CLIPPING. Tempting as Annie McQueen Graham Spencer's death notice — but it is dated the day BEFORE her recorded death of 15 Jan 1871. "Either Find a Grave's day is off, or the clipping is unrelated. Read it before assuming either." Settled by: reading npcom 169198203. (10)

S-12. ARE THE FIVE FLOOR PLANS HISTORIC OR MODERN MARKETING DOCUMENTS? "A five-minute question that nobody has asked." No survey date, draughtsman or provenance recorded. (00)

S-13. THE PORTRAIT SITTER. The nomination's caption pairs "John Jacob Roset" with "Mary Ann Lanning Roset" — "the sitter is either John Roset or his father Jacob." Needs one check. (10)

S-14. "ROSET & BICKING" as a firm. Appears nowhere on the open web except the phila.gov nomination quoting the 1870 obituary. Settled by: HSP's Roset Family Papers (Collection 1897) or period directories. The [B] inference that Ellen **Bicking** Rozet's middle name is the partner's name is not independently corroborated. (10)

S-15. THE ROSENBACH 2008 DELANCEY DATE. A search summary of the Hidden City piece said the foundation purchased 2008 Delancey in January 1993; rosenbach.org says "since 2003." "I could not reconcile these; do not publish either date without checking." (09)

S-16. DEATH DAYS THAT MUST NOT BE PUBLISHED WITHOUT CHECKING: Chapman Biddle (December 9 vs December 29, 1880) and George Gordon Meade (November 6 vs November 7, 1872). Neither could be resolved. (09)

S-17. THE 1868–1893 DIRECTORY GAP. Gopsill's and Boyd's for years other than 1871 are NOT on the Internet Archive (checked with the advancedsearch API). The free IA run is McElroy's 1837–1867 plus Gopsill's 1871 only. This covers the whole Howard Spencer occupancy and all of Gap 5.
  Settled by: Ancestry, FamilySearch, or the Free Library of Philadelphia's digital collections. "That is the next place to dig." (09)

S-18. DEATH NOTICES STILL SITTING FREE AND UNREAD in Chronicling America (09 was rate-limited out of LOC entirely): Foulke (June 1865), Meade (November 1872), Chapman Biddle (December 1880), all likely carrying street addresses. Called "the highest-value unblocked lead I am leaving behind." Note 03 successfully reached both LOC and PSU and should be the model for retrying.

S-19. THE 24 UNREAD CLIPPINGS. All retrievable by Newspapers.com image ID (listed in 00 Part 1). Highest-value single item, per 03: **1896-11-23 Philadelphia Times p.11, npcom 53387473** — almost certainly Graham Spencer's death notice.

S-20. HMDB MARKER INSCRIPTIONS. hmdb.org returned Cloudflare 403 to every approach; no marker inscription could be read directly. "Someone should open the marker pages in a browser and transcribe them." (09)

S-21. WHY THE 1824-03-26 CLIPPING IS IN THE ARCHIVE AT ALL. Predates the house by ~30 years; content entirely unknown; "why it was clipped is unrecorded." (00)

S-22. DUPLICATE DOMAINS. 1822pine.com and rittenhouseresidence.com both indexed with near-identical titles; canonical unresolved. (00)

S-23. THE EVENTS POLICY CONTRADICTION. The "Events & Filming" story chapter is a full micro-wedding and film-shoot playbook; site policy in llms.txt and the FAQ is no parties/events. Flagged as needing reconciliation. (00)

S-24. Martha Mellor Davis is listed by Find a Grave as buried at Fair Hill but does not appear in the Fair Hill register — though her parents do ("Mellor | Martha | 1880, 02/23 | 67 | Sec E | Lot 123" and "Mellor | Thomas | 1882, 03/28 | 74 | Sec E | Lot 123"). Discrepancy logged, unresolved. (10)

S-25. UNPURSUED LEAD: a published book on this family. "Grace Mather-Smith and Kelley, Russell P., III, *My Darlings: A Memoir* (Rowman & Littlefield, 2018), 144," cited by the phila.gov nomination for the Rozets' Vienna years. "An obvious next stop and would likely settle several of the above." (10)

── 5D. RETRIEVAL KEYS AND METHOD NOTES FOR BUILDING THE ARCHIVE ──

IIIF crop patterns:
  PSU: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/iiif/<batch-path>.jp2/<x>,<y>,<w>,<h>/1720,/0/default.jpg
  LOC: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/<service-id>/<x>,<y>,<w>,<h>/full/0/default.jpg — request size `full`, not an upscale, or you get a blurred low-res tile.
Page OCR: `resource.fulltext_file` in the LOC JSON; `/lccn/…/seq-N/ocr.txt` on PSU.
Word bounding boxes: `/lccn/…/seq-N/coordinates/` on PSU (gzipped JSON, coordinate space is 4× the JP2); LOC `word-coordinates-service` with `format=alto_xml` and no `full_text` flag.
Working search endpoints: `https://www.loc.gov/collections/chronicling-america/?q=…&fo=json` (LOC) and `https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/search/pages/results/?…&format=json` (PSU). The legacy `chroniclingamerica.loc.gov/search/pages/results/?format=json` API is dead — it 308s to a www.loc.gov 404.
Run BOTH search engines: LOC's search is exact-token against raw OCR and "a single garbled character kills the hit"; PSU's `proxtext=…&proxdistance=N` is far more forgiving and found three EPL pages LOC missed entirely.

NOT REPUBLISHABLE — noted so they are not mistaken for archive assets: all Find a Grave memorials (cited above as evidence only), Wikipedia and WikiTree pages, the Hidden City Philadelphia article, rosenbach.org, and every Newspapers.com clipping (the 24 unread ones are retrievable only by image ID from behind that paywall — 03 confirms the Philadelphia Inquirer and Philadelphia Times "are not in any free full-text archive").

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
6. GUARDRAILS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

G-1. NO SUFFRAGE MEETINGS IN THIS HOUSE. The record shows tickets SOLD from the address and tax resistance BY a resident. Four unread archive synopses assert meetings here; the unverified-claims file records the marketing lines "Elegant double parlors … where Philadelphia suffragettes held meetings in 1915" and "Suffragette movement headquarters." "Those must not propagate." (00)

G-2. THE APRIL 1919 MEETING NOTICE IS ADDRESSED 1823 PINE. "**This document must not be used to claim a suffrage meeting inside 1822 Pine.**" It is nevertheless the strongest thing yet found on Martha Davis's own political life "and it belongs on the site with the address printed exactly as it appears." (03)

G-3. THE DECEMBER 1913 TAX-RESISTANCE QUOTE IS ON THE DO-NOT-PUBLISH LIST. CONTENT-OPPORTUNITIES: "the cited secondary page does not contain it; attribute by name and mark unverified, or hold it." /history/suffrage currently renders it as a full pull quote attributed to "Mrs. Henry C. Davis, of 1822 Pine Street · December 1913." This is a live, shipped contradiction between the repo's own rule and its own page. (00)

G-4. LUCY DAVIS IS NOT A SUFFRAGIST. Her Woman's Who's Who entry does NOT carry the volume's "Favors woman suffrage" tag. "She is a Quaker reformer at this address, not a suffragist." Independently re-verified by 10, which checked every other DAVIS entry on that page and the next: several carry the tag; Lucy Davis does not. (00, 10)

G-5. NEVER "DREXEL LIVED HERE." Required phrasing per the repo: "documented family connection." Anthony J. Drexel never lived at 1822 Pine; in 1853–56 the newlyweds were at 1816 S. Rittenhouse Square. 10 confirms: "Nothing I found places Anthony J. Drexel at this address, and the phila.gov nomination's footnote… is exactly the right altitude." 09 supplies the truthful alternative: Joseph W. Drexel of Drexel & Co. at 1829 Pine from c. 1867, directly across the street. (00, 09, 10)

G-6. QUOTE THE GRAHAM SPENCER OBITUARY AS PRINTED. "Careful: the phrase is 'the residence of his relatives,' not 'his residence.' Quote it as printed; do not upgrade it to 'his home.'" (03)

G-7. DO NOT CONVERT THE 1866 SEWING-MACHINE TESTIMONIAL INTO A TENANCY DATE. "'in Use for Five Years and Upwards' describes the machine, not the residence. Do not convert it into 'Smith lived here since 1861.'" (03)

G-8. DO NOT LET "GUY BRYAN SCHOTT" INTO THE NARRATIVE. The OCR appears to place him at 1822 Pine Jan 1868–Aug 1870, including a funeral held in the house. Both page images read **1522** Pine. "Recording this here so the next researcher does not re-find it and publish it. It is the strongest argument in this whole report for the rule that no address digit goes on the site without someone looking at the page image." (03)

G-9. NO ADDRESS DIGIT GOES ON THE SITE WITHOUT SOMEONE LOOKING AT THE PAGE IMAGE. "Three of my nine strongest OCR leads changed meaning at this step (C1, C2, C3), and two of those three would have been published as facts about this house." (03)

G-10. DO NOT PUBLISH "LUCRETIA MOTT'S GRANDSON LIVED AT 1822 PINE." The join is circumstantial and partly circular. "Until it is read, publish this as 'strongly indicated, not yet confirmed,' and never as 'Lucretia Mott's grandson lived at 1822 Pine.'" The record that breaks the circle in one step: Philadelphia Inquirer, 22 May 1918, p.17. (10)

G-11. DO NOT PUBLISH THE AGNES-SPENCER-OWNED-IT-TO-1921 HYPOTHESIS AS FACT. "Do not publish this as fact until the rotated 1922 card is read — but it is the hypothesis to test first." (10)

G-12. DO NOT PUBLISH A YEAR FOR THE BOYD'S HOWARD SPENCER CITATION until the title page is read from the page images. (10)

G-13. DO NOT PUBLISH A DAY FOR CHAPMAN BIDDLE'S DEATH (Dec 9 vs Dec 29, 1880) OR GEORGE GORDON MEADE'S (Nov 6 vs Nov 7, 1872). (09)

G-14. DO NOT PUBLISH EITHER ROSENBACH 2008-DELANCEY DATE (Jan 1993 vs "since 2003") WITHOUT CHECKING. (09)

G-15. DO NOT PUBLISH "GENERAL BIRNEY LIVED ON THE 1800 BLOCK OF DELANCEY." Explicitly refuted; the 1871 listing is his widow. (09)

G-16. ELLEN ROZET: PUBLISH 1832, FOOTNOTE 1829, AND DELETE "TWIN." The instruction is explicit that flagging the year in-line is NOT sufficient — the twin claim is itself the artifact of the conflation. (10)

G-17. TRUST FIND A GRAVE'S STRUCTURED FIELDS, NOT ITS BIO PROSE. "Find a Grave's free-text bios contradict its own structured fields on this family… Trust the structured fields; treat the bio prose as a lead only." (10)

G-18. DO NOT CITE PAB FOR THE ARCHITECTS. PAB's record lists no architect; the Duhring/Okie/Ziegler attribution rests entirely on the two 1899 newspaper notices. (10)

G-19. DO-NOT-CONFUSE LIST. "Spencer Ervin" of Bala Cynwyd (PAB holding 75343) is a man named Spencer Ervin, nothing to do with this house. The Boyd's directory contains two Henry C. Davises, neither at 1822 Pine — "Davis Henry C (John Davis & Sons), h Devon Pa" and "Davis Henry C, pres Barclay RR Co… h Hotel Meiropole"; the anthracite-railroad one is tempting and "I am not asserting it. Treat as an open lead only." Mary W. [wife of —], Anthony Tarter and Margaret Thackray appear in the 1891 clipping only because they are adjacent death notices in the same column — they are not residents. (00, 10)

G-20. THE 1913-02-09 SYNOPSIS IS AN INVENTION. "Do not cite it, do not repeat it, and do not treat [it] as a finding — it is an invention, and it also violates the repo's own do-not-publish rule." The same applies to the one-line synopsis on every one of the 41 never-read documents: "It is generated prose." (00)

G-21. THE 1905 FIREPLACE ATTRIBUTION IS UNSAFE AS PUBLISHED. "The site attributes this fireplace to the Davis era without accounting for Purviss at all." (00)

G-22. BLOCK-NEIGHBOUR DIRECTORY LINES vs IDENTIFICATIONS. The repo's standing rule: "the directory LINES are quotable verbatim; the IDENTIFICATIONS are not, and must not be published without an independent source." 09 refines rather than overrides this — a line carrying its own occupational identifier IS the identification. Where the directory gives only a name, 09 grades lower and says so, and marks S. Austin Allibone and William Duane as "probable, not proven." (00, 09)

G-23. BUILD-DATE FIGURES ON THE DO-NOT-PUBLISH LIST: the OPA "1800" estimate and the Redfin "circa 1845" figure are both barred by the repo's own rule. (00)

G-24. THE 2022 PROPERTY CERTIFICATE (raw_documents/1822 Pine - Property Cert.pdf, PC-2022-009391, owner PRICE ROBERTSON) is intentionally excluded from the public archive, and the site says so. (00)

G-25. FLAG DISCREPANCIES IN-LINE RATHER THAN SILENTLY PICKING ONE: the EFS headquarters address prints as "33 South 9th street" in the Jan 13, 1915 notice and "35 South 8th" in the Jan 8 piece — "worth flagging in-line rather than silently picking one." Likewise the one-day discrepancy on John Roset's death (Aug 8 vs "died yesterday" Aug 9, 1870). (03)

# EXTRACT B — Pine Street, Waverly, physical building, census & directories

GRADE A FINDINGS, CORRECTIONS, RESOLVED QUESTIONS, OPEN QUESTIONS, ASSETS AND GUARDRAILS
Extracted from lenses 01 (Pine Street identity), 04 (Waverly Street), 07 (physical building), 08 (census/directory).
Source-file provenance is marked as [01-F#], [04-F#], [07-§#], [08-F#].

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
1. GRADE A FINDINGS
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

──── ERA: PENN'S GRID AND THE STREET NAME (1683) ────

• [01-F1][A] Penn's own 1683 pamphlet describes Pine Street's geometry but does NOT name it — or any tree street. The street Pine occupies was laid out fifty feet wide as one of Penn's eight east-west streets.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/aletterfromwill00penngoog
  Raw OCR: https://archive.org/download/aletterfromwill00penngoog/aletterfromwill00penngoog_djvu.txt
  Verbatim (long-s rendered "f"): "and eight Streets, (befides the High-ftreet, that run from Front to Front, and twenty Streets, (befides the Broad-flreet) that run crofs the City, from fide to fide ; all thefe Streets are of fifty Foot breadth."
  Hard negative in the same document: the word "Pine" does not occur anywhere in the text.

──── ERA: THE ROW IS BUILT AND OCCUPIED (1854–1858) ────

• [08-F4][A] Hard directory evidence for the 1854 build date. Count of distinct occupied house numbers in the 734–775 Pine range (the future 1800 block, south side): 1845=0, 1847=0, 1849=0, 1850=0, 1851=0, 1852=0, 1853=0, 1854=9, 1855=19, 1856=18, 1857=17. 756 Pine is NOT among the nine occupied in 1854; the 1855 volume shows a continuous occupied row.
  URLs: https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1854amce/mcelroysphiladel1854amce_djvu.txt and https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1855amce/mcelroysphiladel1855amce_djvu.txt

• [08-F4][A] / [04-F21][A] John Roset's own movements track the build date exactly.
  Verbatim: 1845 "Roset John, mer., 221 High, h 130 Pine" · 1847 "Roset John, mer., 120 High, h Spruce ab Sch 8th" · 1850 "Roset John, mer., 120 High, h Walnut W Sch 7th" · 1852 & 1853 "Roset John, mer., 180 High, h 730 Poplar" · 1854 "Roset John, mer., 730 Poplar" · 1855 "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine"
  He moves in between the 1854 and 1855 canvasses. 1845 is untenable; c.1854 with occupancy by the 1855 directory is documented.

• [04-F1][A] Waverly Street already bore that name behind 1822 Pine in 1855. It is NOT a product of the 1897 consolidation.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1855amce (text: https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1855amce/mcelroysphiladel1855amce_djvu.txt)
  Verbatim: "Waverly st. fr W to 17th, E to 19th bet Lombard &c / Pine"
  Repeated verbatim 1856, 1857; 1858 with new numbers: "Waverly st. fr W to 416 S 17th, E to 19th bet Lom-bard k Pine" (https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1858amce). 1866 gives both endpoints: "Waverly st. W fr 419 S 17th to 411 S 19th" (https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1866amce).

• [04-F2][A] Waverly Street is ABSENT from the 1854 street index — it appears between the 1854 and 1855 volumes, the same moment the Pine Street row is first occupied.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1854amce
  Verbatim (the full alphabetical run where "Waverly st." would sit): "Watson's St. fr 5tli to .\r.ibella / Watson St. W fr .\"ith bel Washington / Waverly pi. fr ^V to Noble N Front / Wayne St. fr Washington to Wallace, bel HUh (S G)"
  Grade note carried in the source: [A] for the absence itself; [B] for the inference that the street was opened/named c.1854–55.

──── ERA: THE RENUMBERING (1856–1858) ────

• [08-F1][A] / [04-F20][A] The renumbering is PROVEN, not inferred: 756 Pine = 1822 Pine, same man, consecutive directory years.
  1857: https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1857amce/mcelroysphiladel1857amce_djvu.txt (line 106850) — "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine"
  1858: https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1858amce/mcelroysphiladel1858amce_djvu.txt (line 105797) — "Roset John, nier., 1822 Pine" ("nier." is OCR for "mer.")
  "1822 Pine" occurs exactly once in the entire 1858 volume, and this is it. Both files md5-verified byte-for-byte against fresh archive.org downloads.

• [08-F2][A] An empirical 1857→1858 conversion table: nine unambiguous same-name pairs land on a constant +1066, all in the 1800 block — 740→1806 (M'Elroy Danl. R.), 744→1810 (Siter Adam), 746→1812 (Fling Wm.), 748→1814 (Jenkins John, Rev.), 750→1816 (Kester John W.), 754→1820 (Hone Isaac, Mrs.), 756→1822 (Roset John), 758→1824 (Barnes Jonathan), 766→1832 (Robeno Andrew).
  Flanking pairs verbatim: "Hone Isaac, Mrs., 754 Pine" → "Hone Isaac, Mrs., 1820 Pine"; "Barnes Jonathan, 758 Pine" → "Barnes Jonathan, 1824 Pine"
  THE CORRECTION inside this finding: +1066 is the offset for the 1800 block ONLY. In the 1700 block the same matching yields +1000 to +1006: "Hall Charles, Mrs., 708 Pine" → "Hall Charles, Mrs., 1714 Pine" (+1006); "Fithian Lemuel S. … h 727 Pine" → "…h 1727 Pine" (+1000); "Harbert Mrs. 703 Pine" → "Harbert Mrs. 1705 Pine" (+1002).

──── ERA: WHO ACTUALLY LIVED IN THE HOUSE, 1855–1871 ────

• [08-F3][A] Complete occupancy of 1822 Pine, 1845–1871, from directories (each volume searched on the correct number for its year; all files md5-verified):

  1855 — "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine"
  1856 — "Roset John, mer^ 756 Pine"
  1857 — "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine"

• [04-F22][A] The house was tenanted, not a continuous Roset home. McElroy's 1866 shows S. Decatur Smith, glass manufacturer, at 1822 Pine; the 1866 volume contains no Roset or Rozet entry anywhere.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1866amce
  Verbatim: "Smith S. Decatur, glassworks, 36^ S Front, h 1822 Pine"

• [04-F23][A] / [08-F3][A] Howard Spencer is documented at 1822 Pine in 1871, with occupation and business address.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost
  Verbatim: "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine"
  "pres" = president (of a company at 233 South Third Street — not identified; the source says do NOT guess). There is no "Spencer Graham" or "Spencer G." anywhere in the 1871 Gopsill volume.

• [08-F5][A] 740 Pine (= 1806 Pine) is the McElroy directory's own office/home — the publisher of the book lived four doors from Roset. Previously a [LEAD] in the repo; now Grade A with the line itself naming the directory.

• [08-F5][A] 754 Pine (= 1820 Pine), the Isaac Hone house, is the adjoining house that Alice Paul's Congressional Union used as its Pennsylvania headquarters in 1914–15. Eliza Hone, Isaac's widow, is still there in 1871.
  Verbatim (Gopsill's 1871): "Hone Eliza, wid Isaac, h 1820 Pine"

• [08-F5][A] Later block-neighbour confirmations, all Grade A directory lines:

• [04-F24][A] Three block-neighbour identifications upgraded from [LEAD] to primary, from McElroy's 1857 (https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1857amce):
