  "Coppee Henry, prof. Penn. University, 760 Pine" → 1826 Pine
  "M'Elroy Danl. R., 740 Pine st W 18th" and "M'Elroy Thomas E., 155 Walnut, h 740 Pine" → 1806 Pine
  Rest of the 1857 row: "Siter Adam, mer., 744 Pine" (1810) · "Fling Wm., mer., 133 Market, h 746 Pine" (1812) · "Jenkins John Rev., 748 Pine" (1814) · "Kester John W., mer., 99 Market, h 750 Pine" (1816) · "Fhng Wm. B., office r 95^ Walnut, h 752 Pine" (1818) · "Hone Isaac, Mrs., 754 Pine" (1820) · "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine" (1822) · "Barnes Jonathan, 758 Pine" (1824) · "Lea Robeson, com. mer. 76 S Del av, h 764 Pine" (1830) · "Robeno Andrew, mercliant, 766 Pine" (1832)
  Gopsill's 1871 confirms: "Biddle Chapman, lawyer, 131 S 5th, h 1831 Pine" and "Drexel Joseph W. (Drexel & Co.), h 1829 Pine"

──── ERA: THE SEVENTH WARD, DU BOIS AND THE SERVICE STREETS (1871–1899) ────

• [04-F9][A] 1822 Pine sits inside the Seventh Ward — the exact ward W.E.B. Du Bois surveyed house by house in 1896–97 for The Philadelphia Negro.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/philadelphianegr001901mbp
  Verbatim: "The work commenced with a house-to-house canvass of the Seventh Ward. This long narrow ward, extending from South Seventh street to the Schuylkill River and from Spruce street to South street, is an historic centre of Negro population, and contains to-day a fifth of all the Negroes in this city."
  Ward corroborated independently: Gopsill's 1871 street index heads both the Waverly and Addison entries "7th Ward," and the 1950 federal census still assigns the block to Ward 7.

• [04-F10][A] Du Bois's block-by-block tour of the ward reaches 18th Street.
  Same URL. Verbatim: "From Sixteenth to Eighteenth, intermingled with some estimable families, is a dangerous criminal class. They are not the low, open idlers of Seventh and Lombard, but rather the graduates of that school: shrewd and sleek politicians, gamblers and confidence men…"
  And immediately after: "North of Lombard, above Seventeenth, including Lombard street itself, above Eighteenth, is one of the best Negro residence sections of the city, centring about Addison street… The Negro population practically stops at Twenty-second street."

• [04-F11][A] The 1871 directory shows exactly who the 1800-block Addison households were, by occupation, twenty-eight years before Du Bois. Verbatim entries (1800 block of Addison only), from https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost:
  "Clower Andrew, waiter, h 1817 Addison" · "Demby George, waiter, h 1837 Addison" · "Holland John W., waiter, h 1832 Addison" · "Lucas William H., waiter, h 1807 Addison" · "Parker Solomon, waiter, h 1821 Addison" · "Sewell William, waiter, h 1821 Addison" · "Thompson Jacob, waiter, h 1837 Addison" · "Wheeler Richard, waiter, h 1823 Addison" · "Press George D., waiter, h 1815 Addison" · "Bivens John L., barber, h 1815 Addison" · "Wilson Jocob B., barber, h 1825 Addison" · "Brown Henry, porter, h 1807 Addison" · "Grosset William J., porter, h 1834 Addison" · "Russell James, steward, h 1819 Addison" · "White Ishmael, driver, h 1808 Addison" · "Harrison Isaiah, driver, h 1808 Addison" · "Taswell Emanuel, driver, h 1818 Addison" · "Carter John, driver, h 1816 Addison" · "Gray James, driver, h 1807 Addison" · "Brown Jeffry, driver, h 1807 Addison" · "McIntyre Andrew, driver, h 1836 Addison" · "Henderson William, teacher, h 1807 Addison" · "Haviland John D., coachmaker, h 1822 Addison" · "Boyd Mary L., sewing, h 1825 Addison" · "Bailey Mary, wid Linton, h 1816 Addison"
  Ten waiters, seven drivers, two barbers, two porters, a steward, a coachmaker.
  [A] modern fabric: OPA shows 32 parcels in the 1800 block of Addison, uniformly "ROW 3 STY MASONRY," 14–16 ft frontage, 40–42 ft depth.

• [04-F12][A] Isabel Eaton's canvass for the same study walked the residence streets of this ward, including Pine, and counted servants house by house. Source: Isabel Eaton, "Special Report on Negro Domestic Service in the Seventh Ward Philadelphia," appended to The Philadelphia Negro (same volume/URL).
  Verbatim: "This schedule was used throughout the residence streets of the Seventh Ward, and elsewhere in the ward limits wherever colored domestics were employed."
  Verbatim: "Altogether 677 men have been recorded and 1612 women, making a total of 2289 domestics, male and female, either working or living in the Seventh Ward."
  Eaton's Table IX splits Pine Street at Broad: seven blocks east of Broad, "99 domestics, or 58 per cent of all colored domestic servants on Pine Street"; seven blocks west of Broad — the stretch containing 1822 — "71 domestics, or 42 per cent of all colored domestic servants on Pine Street."
  Verbatim, Rittenhouse Square named: "A white butler on Rittenhouse Square sums up the situation from what might be called the impersonal point of view: 'You see they (the employers) go to Europe and bring home Englishmen, and that knocks out the Negro.'"

• [04-F13][A] Du Bois's diagram of how Philadelphia back-alley housing formed. Same URL.
  Verbatim: "the houses of older Philadelphia were built like this: A HOME / B OUTHOUSE / C YARD / O PASSAGE TO STREET. When, however, certain districts like the Seventh Ward became crowded and given over to tenants, the thirst for money-getting led landlords in large numbers of cases to build up their back yards like this: A FRONT TENEMENT FACING ST. / C BACK TENEMENT FACING ALLEY / D ALLEY / R COMMON OUTHOUSE FOR 3 TENANTS."
  Also verbatim: "Houses and stables are mixed promiscuously."
  And on Waverly further east: "The saloon is under a residence, three stories, corner of Waverly and Eleventh streets. Waverly street has a Negro population which fairly swarms — good position for Negro trade." (This is the 1100 block, NOT the 1800 block — say so explicitly if used.)

• [04-F19][A] Nearest documented commercial stable to 1822 Pine in 1871: "Dann James, livery, 320 S 19th" (Gopsill's 1871) — one block west and one block north, between Spruce and Pine.
  [A] modern OPA note for the 18th/Waverly corner: 410 S 18TH ST is classified "ROW W/GAR 3 STY MASONRY"; 412 S 18TH ST is 40 ft wide by only 18 ft deep. Suggestive, NOT proof; [C] as a stable identification.

──── ERA: WAVERLY STREET, 1871–TODAY ────

• [04-F3][A] In 1897 the City folded ~21 separately named little streets into one continuous "Waverly," and the 17th–19th stretch behind 1822 Pine is one of only two Center City blocks that needed NO renaming — because it was already Waverly.
  URL: https://www.phillyhistory.org/HistoricStreets/ (ASP.NET form; enter "Waverly" in Street Name and click Find)
  Verbatim provenance note: "This index was compiled from the original road records, docket books, jury decisions, and surveys held by the Philadelphia City Archives… Changes to the names of certain streets, alleys, and courts were first effected by an ordinance dated September 1, 1858… By an ordinance of February 23, 1897 names of intermediate streets were indexed by old name, location and new name."
  Verbatim rows (all "Year Recorded 1897", all "s. of Pine St."): "Helmuth | Waverly | 1897 | 16th to 17th Sts., s. of Pine St." · "Ringgold | Waverly | 1897 | 19th to Uber Sts., s. of Pine St." · "Watts | Waverly | 1897 | Uber to 20th Sts., s. of Pine St." · "Stone | Waverly | 1897 | 15th to 16th Sts., s. of Pine St." · "Dorsey | Waverly | 1897 | 13th to Juniper Sts., s. of Pine St." · "Barley | Waverly | 1897 | 10th to Marvine Sts., s. of Pine St." · plus "Kemble," "Knease," "Ohio," "Souder," "Aspen," "Harmony," "Mansion," "Mulberry," "Osage," "Ashburton" (x3), "King," "Harrison."
  The gap is decisive: there is NO 1897 entry for 17th-to-18th or 18th-to-19th.

• [04-F5][A] The 1800 block of Waverly has never had front doors. In three separate 19th-century directories it carries NO numbered household at all.
  URL: https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost
  Verbatim: "WAVERLY, W fr 416 § 17th / 7th Ward. / Left. Rt. / —_ — 17th / — — 18th / — — 19th"
  Compare, same index, one block south: "ADDISON, W fr.S 17th to 19th / 7th Ward. / Left. Rt. / 1802 1807 S 18th / 1836 1841S 19th"
  Exhaustive text search: the entire 8.9 MB 1871 directory contains exactly six occurrences of "Waverly," all in the street index and cross-street tables. Zero residents. Against 173 "Addison" hits, 103 "Helmuth" hits, 49 "Ringgold" hits in the same volume.

• [04-F6][A] The only households ever attached to this block sit at the 18th Street corner, in 1855 and again in 2026.
  1855 (https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1855amce): "Knowles Wm. C, 18th and Waverly" and "Maule Henry, lumber mer., 17th bel Spruce, h 18th 8c Waverly" (Maule repeats at 18th & Waverly in 1856 and 1857).
  2026, City of Philadelphia AIS — exactly eight address points for the whole 1800 block, all at 1800 and 1802: "1800 WAVERLY ST", "1800 WAVERLY ST APT 1–5", "1800 WAVERLY ST APT 4B", "1802 WAVERLY ST". Nothing from 1804 to 1898.
  Query: https://services.arcgis.com/fLeGjb7u4uXqeF9q/ArcGIS/rest/services/AIS_Address_Points/FeatureServer/0/query?where=street_name%3D%27WAVERLY%27+AND+address+BETWEEN+1800+AND+1899&outFields=street_address&f=json

• [04-F7][A] No separately assessed property fronts the 1800 block of Waverly Street. Not one.
  Query: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20location%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20location%20LIKE%20%2718__%20WAVERLY%20ST%27 → 0 rows.
  Waverly citywide has 255 assessed properties. By block: 1000 (22), 1100 (19), 1200 (8), 1300 (2), 1500 (5), 1600 (26), 1900 (32), 2000 (39), 2300 (21), 2400 (27), 2500 (24). Blocks 1400, 1700, 1800, 2100, 2200: zero. The two AIS addresses at 1800/1802 Waverly return opa_account_num = null.

• [04-F8][A] 1822 Pine's lot runs the full 90 feet to Waverly with no separate rear parcel, then or now. There is no coach-house parcel at 1822; any rear structure would have been an appurtenance of the same lot.
  OPA record: parcel 081079100, depth 90, frontage 22, total_area 1980, "ROW 4 STY MASONRY," zoning RM1, year_built "1800" with year_built_estimate = "Y". Neighbours 1826 Pine and 1825 Pine also 90 and 100 ft deep.
  Query: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20*%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20location%3D%271822%20PINE%20ST%27

• [01-F10][A] The rear of the lot, described by the City. 1995 inventory, entry heading "1800 block Waverly Street": "Houses on both sides of the street face onto Pine or Addison Streets. Street blacktopped, granite curbs, concrete sidewalks."
  URL: https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213131150/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler.pdf
  There is no separate 1800-block-of-Waverly building inventory because there are no buildings fronting it.

──── ERA: WHAT THE TWO GOVERNMENTS SAY ABOUT THE ROW ────

• [01-F5][A] / [07-§1][A] The City's 1995 inventory entry for the south side, the row containing 1822 — PDF page 88 (printed page 54).
  URL: https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213131150/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler.pdf
  Verbatim (07's complete transcription): "1800-1836 (1836 aka 401-409 S. 19th Street) Nineteen, 4-story, 2 bay, brick, late Greek Revival townhouses, marble trim and surrounds; 1808, reduced one story, metal cornice; 1810, altered c. 1880 cornice and door. --- Contributing" and "Street blacktopped, concrete sidewalks, granite curbs."
  NOTE: "1808" and "1810" here are ADDRESSES on the block, not years. THE IMPORTANT PART: this entry carries NO construction date.
  Transcription caveat from 01: the PDF's text layer interleaves the two printed columns; 01 reordered the words and every word is present verbatim. Confirmed independently by the 1983 NR entry below.

• [01-F6][A] The City's 1995 inventory on the north side, opposite: "(1835 aka 345 S. 19th Street) Seventeen, [4]-story, 2 bay, brick, marble trimmed, Greek Revival rowhouses c. 1850, bracketed and panelled cornices, pedimented paired marble door surrounds; 1827-1831 altered c. 1950 for hospital. 1833, Colonial Revival base — Contributing"
  So: the City dates the NORTH side c.1850 and gives the SOUTH side no date at all.

• [01-F7][A] The 1983 federal nomination, same row, independent wording. National Register of Historic Places, Rittenhouse Historic District, NRIS 83002277, listed 8/25/1983, prepared by "George E. Thomas, Ph.D. / Center City Foundation, Clio Group, Inc., March 1983."
  Asset page: https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/83002277 · PDF: https://npgallery.nps.gov/GetAsset/7ed7c1d1-8cdd-425a-a5b1-c47897c79c6e
  Verbatim: "1800-36 4-story brick, late Greek Revival townhouses, marble trim and surrounds; 1808, reduced one story, metal cornice; 1810, altered c.1880 cornice and door. Contributing."
  North side verbatim: "1803-35 4-story brick, marble trimmed townhouse row, bracketed and panelled cornices, pedimented paired marble door surrounds; 1827-3[1] altered c.1950 for hospital Intrusion. 1833, Colonial Revival base. Contributing."

• [01-F8][A] The c.1845 the site publishes belongs, in the City's own inventory, to the 1700 BLOCK — not this one.
  1995 City inventory, "1700 block Pine Street": "(1700 aka 462 S. 17th Street; 1738 aka 401-407 S. 18th Street) Twenty, 4-story, 2 bay, late Greek Revival townhouses, c. 1845, marble trim and door surrounds, modillioned wood cornice, paired doorways and stoops; 1708 windows reduced. 1730, 1732, 1736, 1738 ground floor alterations c. 1910 — Contributing"
  1983 NR agrees: "1700-38 4-story, late Greek Revival townhouses, marble trim and door surrounds, modillioned wood cornice, c.1845. Contributing."
  Real architectural distinction recorded: the 1700 block has "paired doorways and stoops" and a "modillioned wood cornice"; the 1800-1836 entry says only "marble trim and surrounds."

• [01-F9][A] / [07-§1][A] Nineteen houses — arithmetically confirmed. 1800, 1802 … 1836 even = exactly 19 addresses; the City's assessment file returns exactly nineteen even-numbered addresses 1800–1836 on the block.

• [01-F11][A] The City lists 1822 Pine Street by name on the Philadelphia Register.
  URL: https://www.phila.gov/media/20220725152039/Historic-Register-OPA-addresses.pdf ("Philadelphia Register of Historic Places (OPA-compliant addresses) As of January 23, 2024")
  Verbatim row: "1822 PINE ST	Rittenhouse Fitler	2/8/1995"
  Every even address 1800 through 1836 appears, and only those — 19 rows, matching "Nineteen."
  Two incidental observations: the trailing "Date" column is populated for only three addresses on the block (1800 PINE ST → 1840, 1834 PINE ST → 1845, 1836 PINE ST → 1845); 1822 PINE ST has no date. [C] — do not publish these as construction dates. And 1827, 1829, 1831 Pine are ABSENT from the OPA list, consistent with "altered c. 1950 for hospital."

• [01-F14][A] The correct federal citation for this district: Rittenhouse Historic District, NRIS reference number 83002277, listed 8/25/1983, period of significance 1800-1924, 141 acres, "Roughly bounded by Waverly, 15th, Sansom, Ludlow, 23rd and 25th Streets."
  Boundary language from the nomination: "Along Pine Street from 25th both sides to the middle of the 1900 block, south to Waverly (Ringgold) east both sides of Addison to 17th, north t[o] Pine Street, east on Pine both sides to the middle of the 1400 block at Carlisle Street..."
  ([B] inference, clearly labelled in source: 1822 Pine sits inside the 1983 federal district boundary as described; the address is not enumerated in the federal document, so state it as "within the boundary as described," not as "federally listed.")

• [01-F37][A] District designation dates, both: Philadelphia Register 8 February 1995 (stated on the City's own inventory cover page and on every OPA row); National Register 25 August 1983, as "Rittenhouse Historic District," NRIS 83002277.

──── ERA: PINE STREET'S PLACE IN THE CITY, FROM THE 1983 FEDERAL NOMINATION ────
All from https://npgallery.nps.gov/GetAsset/7ed7c1d1-8cdd-425a-a5b1-c47897c79c6e

• [01-F15][A] The hierarchy of streets, stated explicitly: "the principal houses tended to be erected on the major east-west streets, i.e. Walnut, Spruce, Locust and Pine, while the secondary east-west streets, with the notable exception of certain blocks of Delancey Street, tended to be the site of workingmen's housing, stables, and the rear buildings of the mansions."

• [01-F16][A] Pine as the archetype: "Within its confines are the unified red brick blocks of houses along Pine, Delancey and Spruce streets that are the stereotype of the Quaker City."

• [01-F17][A] The district's shape: "The resulting district generally includes the blocks from Walnut to Pine and from 15th to 24th streets, with extensions to the north to include the institutional center of the Swedenborgian, Lutheran and Unitarian churches."

• [01-F18][A] John McCrea on Pine Street: "Evidence of this can be seen in the 1700 blocks of Pine and Addison Streets, which were among the early projects of builder John McCrea."
  And on developer rows generally: "These were the investment projects of John McCrea and family, Lemuel Coffin, trading as Coffin and Altemus, among others. It was their bold initiative that created the handsome red brick unified block fronts that characterize the district. Especially noteworthy are the McCrea developments lining both sides of the 1800 and 2000 blocks of Delancey."

• [01-F19][A] Pine Street's other named architect commissions: "The earliest important designers include John Haviland on Rittenhouse Square and on Chestnut Street between 15th and 16th Streets; Thomas U. Walter on the south side of the 1500 block of Pine Street..." and "Similar designs by Frank Miles Day (Yarnall House, 17th and Locust; Wood House, 245-47 S. 17th Street, and a small development on the 1900 block of Pine Street)..."

• [01-F20][A] The 1920s remaking: "Rather than building new rows, the old houses were renovated, with tell-tale casement windows and Spanish stucco detail on Panama, Rittenhouse and the western blocks of Pine Street. Architect William Koelle specialized in such adaptations for the cream of old Philadelphia society."

• [01-F21][A] Pine Street forms the south edge of Fitler Square. 1983 NR inventory, "2300 block Pine Street": "North side, Fitler Square." (1995 City inventory designates the square itself: "The Square with all sidewalks, fences, fountains, benches and green areas is designated.")

──── ERA: 1822 PINE'S OWN BUILDING RECORD, 1899–1900 ────

──── ERA: THE PHYSICAL FABRIC, PLATE BY PLATE ────

• [07-§2][A] The GeoHistory address search returns exactly these property-level plates for 1822 Pine (verbatim from the results table): "Plate F | 1875 | City Atlas of Philadelphia, vol. 6"; "Plate Q | 1885 | Atlas of the City of Philadelphia, 1885"; "Plate 2 | 1910 | Atlas of the City of Philadelphia"; "Plate 3 | 1922 | Atlas of the City of Philadelphia (Central)"; "Plate 9 | 1930 (circa) | Aerial Survey of Philadelphia, PA"; "Plate 3A-4 | 1942 | Philadelphia Land Use Map, 1942"; "Plate 3A-4 | 1962 | Philadelphia Land Use Map, 1962".
  Search URL: https://www.philageohistory.org/rdic-images/search-address.cfm?Address=1822+Pine+St%2C+Philadelphia%2C+PA&Search=Search

• [07-§2][A] 1885 Bromley Plate Q metadata verbatim: "5th, 6th, 7th, 8th, 9th, & 10th Wards, Scale 80 feet to an inch. From Actual Surveys and Office Plans of the Survey Department."

• [07-§2][A] 1895 Bromley Plate 2 header verbatim: "PART of WARDS 7, 8 & 9 … Scale 200 feet to the inch … PLATE 2"

• [07-§2][A] 1922 Bromley Central Plate 3 header verbatim: "PART OF WARDS 7 & 8 … Scale 150 Feet to the Inch … PLATE 3"; record subtitle "South Street to Lehigh Ave. Wards 5 to 20, 28, 29, 31, 32, 37 & 47".

• [07-§3][A] 1858–1860, Hexamer & Locher. 1822 Pine is drawn as a FOUR-STORY front block (numeral "4." on the main mass) with a THREE-STORY back building behind it (numerals "3."), a small stepped rear appendage, then OPEN GROUND between the back building and the Waverly Street line. The whole south row 1804–1836 is drawn identically — a uniform, purpose-built speculative row of 4-story fronts with 3-story back buildings. House numbers on the plate are already the post-renumbering 1800-series, so this atlas post-dates the 1856–57 ordinance. Earliest building-level depiction of the house reached.
  Caveat [B]: Hexamer & Locher also print small paired dots in each lot whose meaning was not confirmed against the legend — do not publish an interpretation of the dots. The storey numerals are unambiguous.
  Registration verified by the map's own printed numbers: 1858–60 H&L prints "1822." inside the box; 1875 Hopkins prints "22" inside 1822's box and "24"/"20"/"18" in the neighbours; 1910 Bromley prints "1824" and "1818" inside theirs.

• [07-§3][A] 1885, Bromley, Plate Q — the sharpest single sheet. Reading west to east from the printed anchors 1824 and 1820, the lot between them is labelled "22" — 1822 Pine, 22 feet front, 90 feet deep. The building (pink = brick) fills essentially the whole 22 × 90 lot, with a narrow white light court cut into its WESTERN party line running from roughly 40% to roughly 72% of the depth; the rear of the lot is built, not yard. Neighbours 1826/1828 show large open rear yards; 1820 shows a much larger rear court.

• [07-§3][A] 1885 frontages match the City's assessment file today, house for house. Bromley 1885, west to east: 1836 = 24; 1834–1826 = 20; 1824 = 20; 1822 = 22; 1820–1812 = 22; 1810–1802 = 20; 1800 = 20. Today's OPA frontages are identical (1836 = 24, 1834–1824 = 20, 1822–1812 = 22, 1810–1804 = 20); every lot on the row is 90 feet deep in both. No lot on this block face has been subdivided or merged in 141 years.
  Modern-side query: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20location,year_built,year_built_estimate,frontage,depth,total_livable_area,building_code_description,category_code_description%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20street_name=%27PINE%27%20AND%20house_number::int%20BETWEEN%201800%20AND%201836%20AND%20mod(house_number::int,2)=0%20ORDER%20BY%20house_number::int&format=csv

• [07-§3][A] 1895 Bromley Plate 2 and 1910 Bromley Plate 2 both show 1822 as brick, filling the lot to near the Waverly line, with the same narrow light slot on the west party line. The rear-built condition therefore predates the 1899 Duhring, Okie & Ziegler alterations by at least fourteen years.

• [07-§3][A] 1922 Bromley Central Plate 3 annotates 1822 Pine with frontage "22" and the row with depth "90" and storey count "4"; the building is brick and built out to near the rear line. Anchors: "1828" and "1818" printed four lots apart, with "20" in 1824 and "22" in 1822.

• [07-§1][A] "1808, reduced one story" is independently corroborated today: OPA records 1808 Pine as 3 stories while every other survivor on the row is 4.

──── ERA: THE APARTMENT YEARS (1941–1962) ────

• [07-§4][A] The 1942 Philadelphia Land Use Map legend, verbatim from the Index and Legend sheet (https://www.philageohistory.org/rdic-images/view-image.cfm/LUM1942.Index), "LEGEND OF LAND USE SYMBOLS / SYMBOLS FOR RESIDENTIAL USES":
  plain lot = "DWELLING USE - SINGLE & TWO FAMILY - ATTACHED"
  "A" only = the same "- ATTACHED - WITH ACCESSORY USE"
  numeral over "M" = "MULTIPLE FAMILY - ATTACHED - No. OF STORIES"
  "A" over numeral over "M" = "- ATTACHED - … - WITH ACCESSORY USE"
  Sheet note: "NUMERALS IN BEDS OF STREETS INDICATE HOUSE NUMBERS."
  Sheet imprint: "INDEX / PHILADELPHIA / LAND USE MAP … BUREAU OF ENGINEERING SURVEYS & ZONING / 1942", subtitle "Land-Use Zoning Project No. 18313".

• [07-§4][A] On Plate 3A-4 (1942), registration anchored by the "1836", "1800" and "20" house numerals printed in the street beds:
  1826 Pine = M / 4 · 1824 Pine = A · 1822 PINE = M / 4 / A (MULTIPLE FAMILY, ATTACHED, FOUR STORIES, WITH ACCESSORY USE) · 1820 Pine = M / 4 plus commercial stippling · 1818 Pine = M / 4 / A

• [07-§4][A, with a registration note] Plate 3A-4 of the 1962 Land Use Map carries the identical coding for the same five lots, including M / 4 / A at 1822. The 1962 sheet is georeferenced about half a lot off from the 1942 sheet in GeoHistory's mosaic; re-rendered with a +0.000043° longitude correction the lot lines land on the strip edges and the codes read cleanly.
  These are dated, plate-specific City records saying in the City's own notation that 1822 Pine was a multiple-family dwelling in 1942 and still one in 1962; they bracket the "ZONING SEP 11 1941 REGISTERED" stamp the archive already holds, and they show 1824 next door was NOT converted.

• [01-F24][A] The house next door went to apartments in 1941-42, with an architect and a surviving drawings collection.
  PAB project 1260412 — https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display.cfm/1260412
  Verbatim: Project Status "Built"; Building Type "apartment house, & Offices"; Client "Slavin, Jacob"; Location "1818 PINE ST"; project name "Slavin Office & Apartment Building"
  Chronology: "1941-1942 ADDITIONS/ALTERATIONS"
  Architects: "Magaziner, Louis (1878-1956) (Architect)"
  Holdings: "Athenaeum of Philadelphia - Magaziner Collection / Holding Name: Slavin, Jacob / Slavin Office & Apartment Building / Quantity: 1 folder / Call Number: 78-JF-011-041"

• [04-F14][A] In 1950 the house and the north side of its Waverly block were still in Ward 7, in census enumeration district 51-158.
  URL: https://1950census.archives.gov/search/?county=Philadelphia&state=PA&ed=51-158
  Verbatim ED description: "Philadelphia city - That part of Ward 7 (Tract 7-C-part) Bounded by Spruce; 18th; Waverly, 19th, Waverly; 20th" — 62 sheets, free, images online.
  The south side of the same Waverly block is ED 51-164: "That part of Ward 7 (Tract 7-D-part) Bounded by Waverly, 19th, Waverly; 18th; South; 19th, Lombard, 20th" — 41 sheets.

──── ERA: TODAY ────

• [07-§4][A] Today's condition, City assessment record, parcel 081079100: location 1822 PINE ST; frontage 22; depth 90; total_area 1980; total_livable_area 5923; building_code_description "ROW 4 STY MASONRY"; category "SINGLE FAMILY"; zoning RM1; year_built "1800" with year_built_estimate "Y".
  Query: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20*%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20location=%271822%20PINE%20ST%27
  The 22 × 90 = 1,980 sq ft lot matches the 1985 deed's legal description and the 1885/1922 Bromley annotations exactly.
  Of the nineteen houses: 1822 and 1824 are the only two still "SINGLE FAMILY"; 1804 and 1806 are "HOTEL/RM HSE 4 STY MASON"; 1800 and 1802 are condominiums; the other thirteen are "APTS 5-50 UNITS MASONRY".

• [07-§5][A] City permit record — four permits exist for the address in the City's open permit dataset.
  Query: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20permitnumber,permitissuedate,permitdescription,typeofwork,approvedscopeofwork,contractorname%20FROM%20permits%20WHERE%20address=%271822%20PINE%20ST%27%20ORDER%20BY%20permitissuedate
  443144, 2012-12-12, PLUMBING PERMIT / EZPLUM — "REPLACE FULL WATER SERVICE METER INCREASE FROM 5/8\" TO 1\""
  994255, 2019-08-06, ALTERATION PERMIT / MAJOR — "INSTALL STAR BOLTS PER ENGINEERING.", contractor "WM.PROUD MASONRY RESTORATION"
  PP-2021-016844, 2021-09-17, Plumbing Permit / Alterations — "Replace curb trap, main drain, fai[…]", contractor ROBERT GOODMAN INC.
  ZP-2022-004846, 2022-06-27, Zoning Permit / Change of Use — "Limited Lodging", applicant "Pritzker Law Group, LLC"
  The star anchor plates visible on the façade are a 2019 masonry-restoration intervention, not historic ironwork.

• [01-F36][A] The bike lanes, from the City.
  URL: https://www.phila.gov/programs/complete-streets/projects/spruce-pine-traffic-safety-project/
  Verbatim: existing facilities run on "Spruce and Pine streets from Front to 22nd streets" and are "painted bike lanes with a painted buffer and flexible delineator posts at intersections." The project will "Upgrade the painted bike lanes to separated bike lanes" using "concrete and planter separation."
  First phase installation announced June 4, 2025: https://www.phila.gov/2025-06-04-city-ppa-to-install-first-phase-of-spruce-and-pine-bike-lane-safety-upgrades-project/
  2009 pilot origin: https://nacto.org/case-study/buffered-bike-lanes-on-pine-and-spruce-streets-philadelphia-pa/

──── OTHER BUILDINGS ON AND NEAR THE BLOCK (all [A]) ────

• [01-F25][A] Other documented buildings on the 1800 block:
  1827-1831 Pine — PAB project 75644, Building Type "dwelling, hospital", chronology "c. 1850 BUILT source: PHMC"; City inventory "altered c. 1950 for hospital." A hospital operated directly across the street from 1822 Pine in the 1950s; neither PAB nor the inventory names it.
  1835 Pine / 345 S 19th — PAB project 81571, "George Henry Lea House", Client "Lea, Alice Van A.", Architect "Autenrieth, Charles M., Jr. (1856-1908)", chronology "1900 EARLIEST DOCUMENTED DATE", 7 images at the Athenaeum including "Interior-5 (1900)", Local ID "P-395".
  1826 Pine — PAB project 12756, "Walsh Residence", Client "Walsh, Stevenson H."
  1801 Pine — City inventory: "(aka 300 S. 19th Street) 4-story, 2 bay brick, c. 1870 Italianate rowhouse, windows, door, base altered c. 1950, air conditioner holes cut."

• [01-F27][A] Christ Church Chapel, 1915-1923 Pine Street.
  1995 City inventory: "Christ Church Chapel, brick terra cotta trimmed, 1876-1877, James P. Sims, architect; altered by Frank Furness, c. 1900. Sand blasted — Significant"
  1983 NR: "1915-23 Christ Church Chapel, brick terra cotta trimmed 1870s chapel, J.P. Sims, architect; altered by Frank Furness, c.1900. Significant."
  PAB: https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display.cfm/36761 — "1915-1923 PINE ST", "Philadelphia Register of Historic Places -- 2/8/1995"
  Modern chapter, Inquirer (https://www.inquirer.com/philly/hp/news_update/20130929_Church_at_19th_and_Pine_to_become_private_home.html): built in the 1870s, "designed by Philadelphia architect James Peacock Sims, with later alterations by architect Frank Furness"; First Church of Christ, Scientist "acquired it in 1930"; purchased August 2013 by Robert Lane and Randi Zemsky for $1.61 million to "adapt it very sensitively to a single-family home," with "a 2,400-square-foot private garden."

• [01-F28][A] Other named Pine Street houses within a five-minute walk, all from the 1995 City inventory:
  1711 Pine — "George W. Pepper House, 3-story, 2 bay Venetian Gothic rowhouse, 1890, tan brick, pressed metal trim, A. J. Boyden, architect."
  1739 Pine (aka 345 S. 18th) — "3 1/2 story, 3 bay, red brick, Colonial Revival mansion. Flemish bond with darkened headers, limestone trim, gambrel roof with dormers. Constructed 1903, architects Sturgis and Barton for P. E. Spaulding, president of Pennsylvania Railroad. Significant."
  1920-1930 Pine — "J. R. Kates Row, six, 3 1/2-story, 2 bay, brick, brownstone trimmed Queen Anne rowhouses, 1888-1889, F. M. Day, architect, all in good condition. Significant."
  1900 block Waverly aka Ringgold Place — "Thirteen, 3-story, 2 bay, Greek Revival, brick rowhouses, paired entryways reached by tall transverse marble stoops, raised basements, marble trim, c. 1862. Walter Allison, builder. Significant."

──── THE NATIONAL REGISTER NUMBER ERROR ────

• [01-F12][A] / [07-§6][A] 92001878 is RittenhouseTown in Germantown, not Rittenhouse-Fitler, confirmed at NPS.
  URL: https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/92001878
  Verbatim: Resource Name "RittenhouseTown Historic District"; Location "Philadelphia ; 206--210 Lincoln Dr."; Listing Date "4/27/1992".
  07 downloaded the NPS file itself: https://npgallery.nps.gov/NRHP/GetAsset/NRHP/92001878_text — 46 pages, "NATIONAL HISTORIC LANDMARK NOMINATION", page 1: "historic name RittenhouseTown Historic District", "street & number 206 - 210 Lincoln Drive".

• [01-F13][A] / [07-§6][A] The error originates in the Athenaeum's own database, not in the repo.
  URL: https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display.cfm/73459
  That page prints, under Historic Registrations and Surveys: "National Register: 92001878 (4/27/1992)".

──── RIGHTS ────

• [07-§7][A] Every GeoHistory plate page carries the footer: "Greater Philadelphia GeoHistory Network / PhilaGeoHistory.org — A project of the Athenaeum of Philadelphia / Permission is required to duplicate or publish materials on this site." Underlying scans belong to the Free Library of Philadelphia Map Collection (Hexamer & Locher, Smedley, both Land Use Maps, the Dallin aerials) and the Athenaeum (the Bromleys).

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
2. CORRECTIONS — WHERE THE SITE OR REPO CURRENTLY PUBLISHES SOMETHING WRONG
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

C1. "VICTORIAN MANSION" → "LATE GREEK REVIVAL TOWNHOUSE"
  Wrong text (site): the "Victorian mansion" framing.
  Corrected text: one of "Nineteen, 4-story, 2 bay, brick, late Greek Revival townhouses, marble trim and surrounds."
  Evidence: [01-F7][A], [07-§1][A]. Both the 1983 federal nomination (https://npgallery.nps.gov/GetAsset/7ed7c1d1-8cdd-425a-a5b1-c47897c79c6e) and the 1995 City inventory (https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213131150/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler.pdf) call the row "late Greek Revival townhouses." Neither uses the word Victorian, mansion, or Italianate.

C2. THE "c. 1845" DATE IS MISATTRIBUTED — IT BELONGS TO THE 1700 BLOCK
  Wrong text: "c. 1845" attached to 1822 Pine / the 1800 block, attributed to the City inventory.
  Corrected text: the City's inventory assigns the 1800–1836 row NO construction date at all. "c. 1845" is the City's date for the 1700 block (1700–1738 Pine), the block to the east.
  Evidence: [01-F8][A], [07-§1(b)][A]. 1995 inventory "1700 block Pine Street" entry reads "Twenty, 4-story, 2 bay, late Greek Revival townhouses, c. 1845…"; 1983 NR agrees, "1700-38 … c.1845." The City dates the north side c.1850 and 1801 Pine c.1870 but gives 1800–1836 nothing.
  Secondary source of the c.1845: [01-F22][B] — PAB project 277174 ("1808-1836 PINE ST"), chronology tab https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display_alldates.cfm/277174 reads verbatim "c. 1845 BUILT source: PHMC". Publish the conflict, do not silently pick a winner.

C3. THE BUILD DATE — 1845 IS UNTENABLE; c.1854 WITH OCCUPANCY BY 1855
  Wrong text: "circa 1845" / OPA's "1800".
  Corrected text: no household occupies 734–775 Pine in any directory 1845–1853; nine appear in 1854; nineteen in 1855; 756 Pine (=1822) first appears occupied in the 1855 volume.
  Evidence: [08-F4][A] directory counts; [04-F21][A] Roset's move from 730 Poplar (1854) to 756 Pine (1855). Also [07-§4][A]: OPA's "1800" is flagged by the City itself as year_built_estimate = "Y".

C4. "THE ROSET FAMILY HOME, 1854–1893" IS WRONG FOR ~35 OF THOSE 39 YEARS
  Wrong text: the site's "Roset family home, 1854–1893" framing.
  Corrected text: 1822 Pine was occupied by a succession of tenants — Broadhead (1859), Thomas E. and Virginia McNeill (1860), S. Decatur Smith of the Kensington Glass Works (1861–1866), James Trimble the grocer (1867), Howard Spencer (1871) — while John Roset (d. 1870) and his estate held title. Roset himself appears at the house only 1855–1858.
  Evidence: [08-F3][A] full verbatim directory table above; [04-F22][A] (no Roset or Rozet entry anywhere in the 1866 volume; no Roset at any Pine Street address after 1858 in any volume through 1871).

C5. THE "RENAME HOWARD → GRAHAM THROUGHOUT" INSTRUCTION IS WRONG AND SHOULD BE REVERTED
  Wrong text: DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT's blanket instruction to rename Howard Spencer → Graham Spencer.
  Corrected text: two generations, not one man. Howard Spencer is the documented 1871 head of household at 1822 Pine; Agnes / Mrs. Graham Spencer commissions the 1899–1900 remodel.
  Evidence: [08-F3][A] and [04-F23][A] — "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine" (Gopsill's 1871); there is no "Spencer Graham" or "Spencer G." anywhere in the 1871 Gopsill volume. Confirms CONTENT-OPPORTUNITIES #17 against DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT.

C6. THE +1066 RENUMBERING OFFSET MUST BE SCOPED TO THE 1800 BLOCK ONLY
  Wrong text: a blanket +1066 conversion for "this stretch of Pine."
  Corrected text: +1066 is the offset for the 1800 block only. The 1700 block runs +1000 to +1006.
  Evidence: [08-F2][A] — "Hall Charles, Mrs., 708 Pine"→"1714 Pine" (+1006); "Fithian Lemuel S. … h 727 Pine"→"…h 1727 Pine" (+1000); "Harbert Mrs. 703 Pine"→"Harbert Mrs. 1705 Pine" (+1002). The repo's re-tagging task (#5 in its cheapest-tasks list) should be scoped to the 1800 block.

C7. THE NATIONAL REGISTER NUMBER IS WRONG
  Wrong text: National Register 92001878 for the Rittenhouse-Fitler district.
  Corrected text: 92001878 is RittenhouseTown Historic District, 206–210 Lincoln Drive, listed 4/27/1992. The correct federal citation for this district is Rittenhouse Historic District, NRIS 83002277, listed 8/25/1983.
  Evidence: [01-F12][A] https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/92001878 ; [07-§6][A] https://npgallery.nps.gov/NRHP/GetAsset/NRHP/92001878_text ; [01-F14][A] https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/83002277
  Source of the error: [01-F13][A] / [07-§6][A] — PAB's own district page https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display.cfm/73459 prints "National Register: 92001878 (4/27/1992)".

C8. THE "MCELROYS AT 1806 PINE" FLOURISH NAMES THE WRONG MEN
  Wrong text: identifying Archibald McElroy, publisher of the directory, as the resident at 740/1806 Pine.
  Corrected text: the 1857 volume names Daniel R. and Thomas E. McElroy at 740 Pine, NOT Archibald. Separately, the 1854/55 volume prints the directory's own office at that address: "PHILADELPHIA CITY DIRECTORY, OFFICE, 7 HART'S BUILDINGS, (2d story), h 740 Pine".
  Evidence: [04-F24][A] "M'Elroy Danl. R., 740 Pine st W 18th" and "M'Elroy Thomas E., 155 Walnut, h 740 Pine"; [08-F5][A] the directory-office line. Correct the site accordingly, or drop the "publisher of the directory itself" flourish until Archibald is found there.

C9. THE 1899 CONTRACTOR'S NAME IS RODGERS, NOT ROGERS
  Wrong text: "Rogers".
  Corrected text: "J. S. Rodgers, of Stanwick, N. J."
  Evidence: [08-F7][A] Builders' Guide v.14, 1899, p.489 (8/2/1899).

C10. THE DEEP REAR BUILDING PREDATES THE 1899 REMODEL — DO NOT CREDIT DUHRING, OKIE & ZIEGLER WITH IT
  Wrong text (implied by narrative): the deep rear building as part of the 1899 Spencer remodel.
  Corrected text: 1822 Pine grew rearward between the 1858–60 Hexamer & Locher survey (front block + 3-story back building + open rear yard) and the 1885 Bromley survey (built to the rear lot line). That change belongs to the Roset ownership. The rear-built condition also appears on 1895 and 1910 Bromley.
  Evidence: [07-§3][B, but well founded] plus [07-§3][A] for the 1885/1895/1910 plates. Graded B only because Hopkins 1875 is too generalised to bracket the change more tightly than 1860–1885.

C11. "THE HOUSE IS IN PAB AS THE SPENCER RESIDENCE" — CITED FROM THE WRONG RECORD
  Wrong text: repo Open Question 6's PAB claim resting on https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/ho_display.cfm/75343, "Ervin, Spencer, Residence Alterations."
  Corrected text: that record reads "Client Name: Ervin, Spencer", "Item dates: 1925-1927", "Collection: George B. Roberts Papers", "Institution: Historical Society of Pennsylvania", "Location: NW corner of Belmont Ave and Llanberris Rd." — a man named Spencer Ervin in Lower Merion, not Agnes Graham Spencer at 1822 Pine.
  Evidence: [07-§6][A]. 07 also read the complete PAB project lists for Duhring, Okie & Ziegler (ar_display_projects.cfm/22640) and Duhring (22482), Okie (91390), Ziegler (23435): none contains "Spencer" or "1822 Pine". Do not publish the claim without a specific pj_display.cfm project URL. (01-F23 supplies one: pj_display.cfm/14707 — the two lenses disagree on whether the record is reachable; see STILL OPEN.)

C12. "GOOSETOWN — COULD NOT CORROBORATE" CAN BE RETIRED, WITH A CORRECTION OF SCOPE
  Wrong text: the repo's "could not corroborate" note on Goosetown; and any use of Goosetown for the whole neighbourhood.
  Corrected text: Goosetown attaches to Manning Street (then Ann Street), not to the whole neighbourhood.
  Evidence: [04-F17][B] John Kopp, PhillyVoice, June 12, 2017, quoting Vincent Fraley of HSP — https://www.phillyvoice.com/rittenhouse-square-phillys-prestigious-park/ — verbatim: "A tight cluster of houses that developed along Manning Street – then known as Ann Street – was dubbed 'Goosetown Village' due to the geese that flocked to the water-filled clay pits in the area."

C13. "STABLES ON WAVERLY BEHIND THE HOUSE" IS NOT SUPPORTED
  Wrong text: the site's copy that stables once stood on Waverly behind the house.
  Corrected text: the record does not show it. 1822's lot has never had a separate rear parcel; the 1800 block of Waverly has no assessed property and no numbered household in any 19th-century directory; the nearest documented livery in 1871 was James Dann at 320 S 19th; the surviving Rittenhouse stable row is on the 2000 block of Rittenhouse Square Street.
  Evidence: [04-F5][A], [04-F7][A], [04-F8][A], [04-F19][A].

C14. THE "EIGHTH WARD" IN THE DEEDS IS NOT THE CENSUS WARD
  Wrong text: searching Ward 8 on the strength of the 1985/2013 deeds' legal description.
  Corrected text: 1822 Pine is in the census Seventh Ward (Du Bois 1896–97; Gopsill's 1871 street index; 1950 tract 7-C / ED 51-158). The "Eighth Ward" in the deeds is a 20th-century ward number.
  Evidence: [04-F9][A], [08-F6] closing note. 04 adds: flag the discrepancy in-line, do not silently harmonise it.

C15. STAR BOLTS ARE NOT HISTORIC IRONWORK
  Corrected text: the star anchor plates on the façade are a 2019 masonry-restoration intervention.
  Evidence: [07-§5][A] L&I permit 994255, 2019-08-06, "INSTALL STAR BOLTS PER ENGINEERING.", contractor WM.PROUD MASONRY RESTORATION.

C16. IF THE SITE SAYS ANTIQUE ROW IS A "DISTRICT," IT SHOULD SAY "INFORMALLY KNOWN AS"
  Evidence: [01-F32][C] / [01-N7] — no Philadelphia Historical Commission designation, no National Register listing, and no City ordinance creating or bounding "Antique Row." It is an informal commercial corridor.

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
3. RESOLVED QUESTIONS
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

R1. Open Question #1 — the Spencer naming problem. RESOLVED in favour of CONTENT-OPPORTUNITIES #17 and against DEEP-RESEARCH-REPORT. Howard Spencer is real and documented at 1822 Pine in 1871 ("Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine", Gopsill's 1871); no "Spencer Graham" or "Spencer G." exists anywhere in that volume. Two generations, not one man. The blanket rename should be reverted. [08-F3][A], [04-F23][A]

R2. Open Question #2 / the build date. RESOLVED as far as the directories can take it: zero occupied houses in the 734–775 Pine range 1845–1853; nine in 1854 (756 not among them); nineteen in 1855 with 756 occupied by John Roset, who was at 730 Poplar in the 1854 volume. Corroborates an April 1854 conveyance and kills c.1845 and OPA's 1800. [08-F4][A], [04-F21][A]. The competing c.1845 is separately traced to PAB/PHMC, not to the City inventory. [01-F8][A], [01-F22][B], [07-§1(b)][A]

R3. Open Question #3 — the renumbering. RESOLVED and PROVEN: John Roset, "mer.", at 756 Pine in the 1857 McElroy's and at 1822 Pine in the 1858 McElroy's; flanked by Hone 754→1820 and Barnes 758→1824; nine same-name pairs on a constant +1066. The ordinance itself now has a published citable source: the 1856 "Decimal" or "Philadelphia System" introduced by councilman John Mascher, implemented from 1857. [08-F1][A], [08-F2][A], [04-F20][A], [01-F4][B]

R4. Open Question #10 — is the row Victorian? RESOLVED: no. Two government documents call it "late Greek Revival townhouses." [01-F7][A], [07-§1][A]

R5. Open Question #12 — the National Register number. RESOLVED: 92001878 is RittenhouseTown; the correct number is 83002277 (8/25/1983). The bad number was inherited from PAB's own district page. [01-F12][A], [01-F13][A], [07-§6][A]

R6. Bench item 20 — was the house tenanted rather than a continuous Roset home? CONFIRMED, and stronger than claimed: a full tenant succession 1859–1871 with verbatim directory lines for each. [08-F3][A], [04-F22][A]

R7. Two of the repo's unsourced guesses are now specific named people: "the McNeills" = Thomas E. McNeill, engineer, office 414 Walnut, and Virginia McNeill, widow (1860); "S. Decatur Smith the glassware manufacturer" = confirmed, firm named as the Kensington Glass Works, 35½ S. Front St. [08-F3][A]

R8. The Waverly Street pre-1855 name question. RESOLVED as a negative: there was none. The DOR Historic Street Index records no 1858 and no 1897 change for 17th-to-18th or 18th-to-19th; the street is absent from the 1854 McElroy street index and present in 1855. Stop hunting for a former name. [04-F1][A], [04-F2][A], [04-F3][A]

R9. Was the coach house / rear parcel ever separate? RESOLVED as a negative: no separately assessed property has ever fronted the 1800 block of Waverly, and 1822's lot runs the full 90 feet with no separate rear parcel. [04-F7][A], [04-F8][A]

R10. Repo Open Question 6's PAB claim. RESOLVED as a false match: ho_display.cfm/75343 is Spencer Ervin of Lower Merion, 1925–1927, George B. Roberts Papers at HSP. [07-§6][A]

R11. The "Apartment House Years" chapter now has documents. The 1942 and 1962 Philadelphia Land Use Maps both code 1822 Pine "M / 4 / A" — multiple family, attached, four stories, with accessory use — bracketing the "ZONING SEP 11 1941 REGISTERED" stamp; 1824 next door is coded "A" (single & two family) in 1942. [07-§4][A]

R12. When did the rear of the lot get built out? RESOLVED to a bracket: between the 1858–60 Hexamer & Locher survey and the 1885 Bromley survey — during the Roset ownership, not the 1899 remodel. [07-§3][B, well founded, resting on [A] plate readings]

R13. Has the lot ever been subdivided or merged? RESOLVED as a negative: 1885 Bromley frontages match today's OPA frontages house for house across the whole row, and every lot is 90 feet deep in both. No change in 141 years. [07-§3][A]

R14. The 1899 contract details. RESOLVED and corrected: contractor J. S. Rodgers of Stanwick, N.J.; a second campaign in September 1900; a $1,200 permit to Jacob Myres & Son. And a hard negative: nothing for 1822 Pine in the Builders' Guide 1901–1940. [08-F7][A]

R15. The Goosetown "could not corroborate" note. CLOSED with a scope correction — it attaches to Manning Street (formerly Ann Street), sourced to HSP's Vincent Fraley via PhillyVoice 2017. [04-F17][B]

R16. Is 1822 Pine on the Philadelphia Register by name? RESOLVED: yes — "1822 PINE ST	Rittenhouse Fitler	2/8/1995" on the City's own OPA-compliant address list. [01-F11][A]

R17. Is 1822 Pine in the 1983 federal district? RESOLVED only as "within the boundary as described" [B] — the address is not enumerated in the federal document. Do not write "federally listed." [01-F14]

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
4. STILL OPEN — AND THE SPECIFIC RECORD THAT WOULD SETTLE EACH
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

O1. Who built the 1800 block of Pine? Open. PAB attributes NO builder and NO architect to the three block-level records (1800-1806 = 277172, 1808-1836 = 277174, 1803-1825 = 277170); all are "(untitled)" with a PHMC date and nothing else, and there is no McCrea attribution anywhere on the 1800 block of Pine in PAB. [01-F22 negative, 01-N6]
  What would settle it: the deed chain behind the April 1854 conveyance, or the City Archives / Athenaeum pre-1889 building records. The 1983 NR places McCrea only on the 1700 blocks of Pine and Addison and the 1800/2000 blocks of Delancey.

O2. Does a photograph of 1822 Pine or the 1800 block exist at PhillyHistory.org? Open — the archive is JavaScript-rendered and could not be scraped.
  What would settle it: ninety seconds in a browser at https://www.phillyhistory.org/PhotoArchive/Search.aspx?action=link&type=address&address=1822+PINE+ST [01-N4, 04 negatives]

O3. What was the hospital at 1827-1831 Pine? Open. Both inventories say "altered c. 1950 for hospital" and PAB types the record "dwelling, hospital," but no source names the institution.
  What would settle it: 1950s Philadelphia city directories, L&I records, or the Philadelphia Inquirer. [01-N5]

O4. Which Sanborn volume/sheet covers Pine between 18th and 19th, and does a free one exist? Open — loc.gov was unreachable (403 / Cloudflare 429).
  What would settle it: retry loc.gov from a browser, look for the sanborn07905 series and its index map/street index. Penn's guide states the Philadelphia Sanborns are microfilm with online access only through subscription products (ProQuest Sanborn Maps Geo Edition; Digital Sanborn Maps 1867–1970 Pennsylvania) — https://guides.library.upenn.edu/sanborn [07 negatives]

O5. Which Hexamer & Locher plate number is this block? Open. Layer HXL1860 is georeferenced but GeoHistory returns no metadata record for the id, and the address search does not return an H&L plate. The atlas is Volume 3 (1858), plates 30–42.
  What would settle it: the Free Library digital collections (item IDs appear to run 11737 + plate number: 11770 = Plate 33, 11771 = Plate 34, 11775 = Plate 38) and the "Index (vol. 3) and Legend" at https://libwww.freelibrary.org/digital/item/11766 — needs a browser. The same visit should capture the H&L legend so the paired-dot symbol can be explained. [07 negatives]

O6. What do the paired dots in each Hexamer & Locher lot mean? Open — not confirmed against the atlas legend. Do not publish an interpretation. Same Free Library legend item as O5. [07-§3 caveat]

O7. What grade does the 1934 Appraisal Map (layer JMB1934.Phila) give the Pine–Waverly block between 18th and 19th? Open — the layer's maxZoom is 17 versus 19–20 for other layers and the scan disintegrates at the needed magnification.
  What would settle it: the source scan in hand. [07 negatives]

O8. Can the c.1930 Dallin aerial (Plate 9, 16,800 × 10,829) be positively fixed on this block? Open — rows, rear yards and party walls are visible but individual houses cannot be separated and there is no landmark in the frame.
  What would settle it: georeferencing the plate against Rittenhouse Square first. [07 negatives]

O9. Who occupied 1822 Pine between 1872 and 1935? Open — there is no free full-text Philadelphia city directory for any year after 1871 on Internet Archive; the run is McElroy's 1837–1867 plus Gopsill's 1871.
  What would settle it: HathiTrust's Full View Boyd's Philadelphia combined city and business directories for 1887 (hvd.hx6aaz), 1893 (hvd.hx6aax), 1894 (hvd.hx6ab9), 1895 (hvd.hx6aaj), 1900 (hvd.hwrff5) — blocked by Cloudflare to tooling, readable by a human in a browser in minutes. Named in 08 as the highest-value single unblocked task remaining. [08 negatives]

O10. Who was in the house in the 1880 census (the first with addresses)? Open, but bounded. The 1800 block of Pine is somewhere between reel 1170 page n648 and page n700.
  Reels (free, no login): https://archive.org/details/populationsc18801170unit (Wards 6–7, ED 107 sheet 5 – ED 135 sheet 10, 764 pages) and https://archive.org/details/populationsc18801171unit (Wards 7–9, ED 135 sheet 11 – ED 164 sheet 16, 763 pages)
  Page images: https://archive.org/download/<id>/page/n<N>_w2000.jpg
  ED↔page map already established on reel 1170: n409 = ED 122, n454 = ED 124, n499 = ED 126, n544 = ED 127, n589 = ED 129, n634 = ED 130, n679 = ED 132. On reel 1171: n4 = ED 135, n54 = ED 137, n104 = ED 139, n154 = ED 141, n204 = ED 142, n254 = ED 144, n304 = ED 146, n354 = ED 148.
  Streets positively identified: ED 129 (~n600) = Delancey Place 1700–1800 blocks; ~n655 = Lombard 1900 block; ~n675 = Naudain St; ~n710 = Pine St 2000 block and Spruce 2100 block; reel 1171 n42–n62 = house numbers 2200–2400. [08 roadmap]

O11. Who was at 1822 Pine in 1950? Open — the enumerator wrote "no one home" at house 1,822 on sheet 38 of ED 51-158, with a partly legible cross-reference "See S[heet] __ Line __", and the call-back entry was not located among the other 61 sheets. [08-F6, Grade B]
  What would settle it: reading the remaining sheets of ED 51-158 (62 sheets) at https://1950census.archives.gov/search/?ed=51-158

O12. Did Waverly between 18th and 19th ever acquire residents after 1871? Open — no free directory between 1872 and 1930 was found.
  What would settle it, cheapest route: the 1950 census EDs 51-158 (north side) and 51-164 (south side). [04 negatives]

O13. Is there a coach house at 1822 on the 1875 / 1885 / 1895 atlas plates? 07 answers the fabric question (built to the rear line by 1885); 04 records this as still unclosed on the GeoHistory image tiles and as an open target at RP-Alt-Index.md line 131. Treat 07's 1885 Bromley Plate Q reading as the answer of record and reconcile the two lenses.

O14. The Builders' Guide volume number for the 8/2/1899 notice. Two lenses disagree: PAB's citation reads "v. 24, n. 31, p. 489, 8/2/1899" [01-F23]; 08's own full-text search reads "v.14, 1899, p.489 (8/2/1899)" and links https://www.philageohistory.org/rdic-images/view-book-uv.cfm/PhilaBuildersGuide_v14_1899 [08-F7]. Settle from the volume title page before citing.

O15. Does PAB project 14707 ("Spencer Residence," 1822 PINE ST) exist and resolve? 01 reached it and quotes its architects and citations tabs [01-F23][A]; 07 read the DOZ firm project lists and found no "Spencer" or "1822 Pine" and warns against publishing the claim without a specific pj_display.cfm URL [07-§6][A]. Re-check https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display.cfm/14707 directly before publishing.

O16. Was Mary Cuthbert (1865, "Cuthbert Mary, 1822 Pine") a lodger, a relative, or a directory error? Open [C]. Do not publish her as a second household without more. [08-F3]

O17. What company was Howard Spencer president of, at 233 S. 3d? Open — the directory says only "pres". Do NOT guess. [04-F23][A]

O18. Is Henry Coppée at 1826 Pine the same man as Lehigh's first president? Open — the directory only says "prof. Penn. University." Needs an independent source. [04-F24]

O19. The 1863 McElroy's has no 1822 Pine entry. The volume is ~25% smaller than its neighbours — treat as a volume/OCR gap, not evidence of vacancy. [08 negatives]

O20. Pine Street's one-way direction. Open — not stated on any City page reached; search summaries asserted "eastbound on Pine" but no City source confirmed it. Do not publish a direction without checking the Streets Department. [01-F36 caution, 01-N9]

O21. The 1995 City nomination's narrative sections (pages 1-33) are image-only with no text layer. If the City's statement of significance or boundary justification is wanted in words, it needs OCR or a manual read. The 1983 federal nomination supplies the equivalent narrative in searchable text. [01-N10]

O22. The Rittenhouse-Fitler Manual PDF (https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213124021/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler-Manual.pdf) is 56 pages of pure scan — 775–831 characters of extractable text across the whole file. Not worth fetching again without OCR. [01-N11, 04 negatives]

O23. Historic building permits (1889 onward) are not online. The City's open permits dataset begins in the 2000s. Anyone chasing the 1899 alteration permit, the 1900 $1,200 Jacob Myres & Son permit, or the 1905 J. N. Long fireplace permit has to go in person to the City Archives or through the Athenaeum. [07 negatives]

O24. Dennis Clark's essay "Ramcat and Rittenhouse Square," in The Divided Metropolis: Social and Spatial Dimensions of Philadelphia, 1800–1975 — the root of everything published about Ramcat — was reached only through Ken Finkel's summaries. Someone with library access should get Clark's text itself. [04 negatives]

O25. Whether "Goosetown" or "Ramcat" appears in any 19th-century source. Open as a negative: zero occurrences of "goose"/"Goosetown" in Charles J. Cohen, Rittenhouse Square, Past and Present (1922), https://archive.org/details/rittenhousesquar00cohe ; zero occurrences of "Goosetown" or "Ramcat" in the entire 8.9 MB Gopsill's 1871. Treat as [B] with the HSP attribution; do not claim a period source. [04 negatives]

O26. Unresolved directory lines, listed so the next researcher does not re-chase them: McElroy's 1857 "FRENCH WM. H., plasterer & plaster paris ornament manuf., 18th and Chestnut, h 192 Waverly" (the number 192 fits neither the old nor new numbering on 17th-to-19th Waverly); McElroy's 1857 "Smith Russel, landsc. painter, Waverly" (no number; tempting to connect to the painter Russell Smith, NOT confirmed); McElroy's 1866 "Moore Marmaduke, 319 Walnut, h Waverly" (no number). [04 negatives]

O27. The Burke book — "Burke, Bobbye et al. Historic Rittenhouse: A Philadelphia Neighborhood. Philadelphia: University of Pennsylvania Press, 1985., p. 44-45" — discusses this house or its immediate row and nobody on the project has looked at it. [01-F23]

O28. What is the source of the trailing "Date" column on the City's OPA-compliant Register address list (1800 PINE ST → 1840, 1834 → 1845, 1836 → 1845)? Unresolved [C]. Do not publish these as construction dates. [01-F11]

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
5. FREELY REPUBLISHABLE ASSETS — PRE-1930 PAGES AND PUBLIC-DOCUMENT SOURCES WITH DIRECT URLS
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Read the GUARDRAILS section before hosting anything: the GeoHistory map plates (§5.D) are explicitly NOT free to republish.

──── 5.A  PRE-1930 PRINTED BOOKS — PUBLIC DOMAIN, FULL TEXT AND SCANS ON INTERNET ARCHIVE ────
All of these are downloadable in full (page images and OCR text) and are the backbone of the archive.

• William Penn, "A Letter from William Penn… to the Committee of the Free Society of Traders," London: A. Sowle, 1683
  Item: https://archive.org/details/aletterfromwill00penngoog
  Full OCR text: https://archive.org/download/aletterfromwill00penngoog/aletterfromwill00penngoog_djvu.txt
  (99,655 chars; contains the "fifty Foot breadth" passage; contains zero occurrences of "Pine")

• McElroy's Philadelphia City Directory — the full free run is 1837–1867. Item and text URL pattern:
  Item: https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel<YEAR>amce
  Text: https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel<YEAR>amce/mcelroysphiladel<YEAR>amce_djvu.txt
  Volumes named and used across the lenses, each with quoted lines above:
    1854 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1854amce (Waverly absent from street index; nine occupied houses in the 734–775 range; Roset at 730 Poplar)
    1855 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1855amce (Waverly street-index entry; nineteen occupied; Roset first at 756 Pine; Knowles and Maule at 18th and Waverly)
    1856 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1856amce
    1857 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1857amce ; text https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1857amce/mcelroysphiladel1857amce_djvu.txt (line 106850 = "Roset John, mer., 756 Pine"; the full pre-renumbering block roster)
    1858 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1858amce ; text https://archive.org/download/mcelroysphiladel1858amce/mcelroysphiladel1858amce_djvu.txt (line 105797 = "Roset John, nier., 1822 Pine"; the only occurrence of "1822 Pine" in the volume)
    1859, 1860, 1861, 1862, 1863, 1864, 1865 — same pattern
    1866 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1866amce (S. Decatur Smith at 1822 Pine; Waverly endpoints)
    1867 — https://archive.org/details/mcelroysphiladel1867amce
  These are the source of the "756 Becomes 1822" article: two directory lines a year apart, both scans publishable.

• Gopsill's Philadelphia City Directory for 1871
  Item: https://archive.org/details/gopsillsphiladel00cost
  Full text: https://archive.org/download/gopsillsphiladel00cost/gopsillsphiladel00cost_djvu.txt
  Contains: the Waverly street-index entry with dashes, the Addison entry with numbers, the whole 1800-block Addison occupational roster, "Spencer Howard, pres, 233 S 3d, h 1822 Pine", "Hone Eliza, wid Isaac, h 1820 Pine", "Drexel Joseph W. (Drexel & Co.), h 1829 Pine", "Biddle Chapman, lawyer, 131 S 5th, h 1831 Pine", "Dann James, livery, 320 S 19th".

• W.E.B. Du Bois, The Philadelphia Negro (1899), including Isabel Eaton's "Special Report on Negro Domestic Service in the Seventh Ward Philadelphia"
  Item: https://archive.org/details/philadelphianegr001901mbp
  Public domain. Source of the ward-boundary passage, the 16th-to-18th and Addison Street passages, Eaton's Table IX Pine Street split, the butler quotation, and the back-tenement diagram.

• Charles J. Cohen, Rittenhouse Square, Past and Present (1922)
  Item: https://archive.org/details/rittenhousesquar00cohe
  Public domain. Contains the brickyard material ("this whole section was brickyards," "The ground originally was a brickyard"); contains zero occurrences of "goose"/"Goosetown"/"Ramcat".

• Retrieval note for all Internet Archive items: item-level djvu.txt downloads work reliably —
  https://archive.org/download/<identifier>/<identifier>_djvu.txt
  Page images fetch directly as https://archive.org/download/<id>/page/n<N>_w2000.jpg (follow redirects).
  The full-text search API https://ia-pub-fts-api.archive.org/search/v1/scrape returned nothing; do not use it.

──── 5.B  PRE-1930 TRADE PRESS — PHILADELPHIA REAL ESTATE RECORD AND BUILDERS' GUIDE ────
Free full-text search of the whole 1886–1940 run, hosted by GeoHistory:
  Search: http://philageohistory.org/BuildersGuide/search.cfm?q=%221822+Pine%22 (4 results, the complete set)
  Volume viewers:
    v.14, 1899 — https://www.philageohistory.org/rdic-images/view-book-uv.cfm/PhilaBuildersGuide_v14_1899 (p.461, 7/19/1899 notice; p.489, 8/2/1899 notice)
    v.15, 1900 — https://www.philageohistory.org/rdic-images/view-book-uv.cfm/PhilaBuildersGuide_v15_1900 (p.585, 9/12/1900; p.611, 9/19/1900)
  These are pre-1930 trade-press pages naming 1822 Pine, Duhring Okie & Ziegler, Agnes / Mrs. Graham Spencer, J. S. Rodgers of Stanwick N.J., and Jacob Myres & Son with a $1,200 cost. Note the rights caveat in GUARDRAILS about GeoHistory-hosted images generally; the search results and the four quoted passages are quotation, not image republication.

──── 5.C  FEDERAL AND MUNICIPAL PUBLIC RECORDS — QUOTABLE AND REPRODUCIBLE ────
07 states plainly: "the phila.gov nomination PDF, the OPA/L&I/PWD data, and the NPS nomination are public records you can quote and reproduce."

• National Register of Historic Places, Rittenhouse Historic District, NRIS 83002277 (George E. Thomas / Clio Group, March 1983), 253 pages, WITH an OCR text layer
  Asset detail: https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/83002277
  PDF: https://npgallery.nps.gov/GetAsset/7ed7c1d1-8cdd-425a-a5b1-c47897c79c6e
  Source of: the 1800-36 and 1803-35 inventory entries, the 1700-38 entry, the street-hierarchy passage, the "stereotype of the Quaker City" passage, the district-shape passage, the McCrea passages, the Haviland/Walter/Day passages, the Koelle passage, the "2300 block Pine Street / North side, Fitler Square" entry, the Christ Church Chapel entry, and the boundary description.

• NPS record for RittenhouseTown Historic District (the wrongly cited number), NRIS 92001878
  Asset detail: https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/92001878
  Full nomination text PDF (46 pp): https://npgallery.nps.gov/NRHP/GetAsset/NRHP/92001878_text

• Philadelphia Historical Commission, Rittenhouse Fitler Residential Historic District inventory, added 8 February 1995 to the Philadelphia Register
  PDF: https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213131150/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler.pdf
  176 pages; inventory pages ~34–176 have an extractable text layer (two-column interleave — read carefully); narrative pages 1–33 are image-only. The 1822-governing entry is at PDF page 88 / printed page 54.
  Source of: the 1800-1836 entry, the 1803-1835 entry, the 1700 block entry, the 1800 block Waverly Street entry, 1801 Pine, 1711 Pine, 1739 Pine, 1920-1930 Pine, 2009 Pine, the 1900 block Waverly / Ringgold Place entry, Christ Church Chapel, and the Fitler Square designation sentence.

• Philadelphia Historical Commission, "Philadelphia Register of Historic Places (OPA-compliant addresses) As of January 23, 2024," 292 pages
  PDF: https://www.phila.gov/media/20220725152039/Historic-Register-OPA-addresses.pdf
  Contains the row "1822 PINE ST	Rittenhouse Fitler	2/8/1995" and all nineteen block addresses.

• Rittenhouse-Fitler Manual (image-only scan, 56 pp, ~775–831 extractable characters — listed for completeness, not worth hosting without OCR)
  PDF: https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213124021/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler-Manual.pdf

• 1950 U.S. Census, NARA — free, open, images online
  ED 51-158 (north side of the Waverly block, contains 1822 Pine): https://1950census.archives.gov/search/?county=Philadelphia&state=PA&ed=51-158 — 62 sheets
  API form: https://1950census.archives.gov/api/search/?ed=51-158&state=PA&county=Philadelphia
  ED 51-164 (south side of the same Waverly block): 41 sheets, same search interface
  DIRECT IIIF IMAGE URL for the sheet containing 1822 Pine (sheet 38 of 62):
    https://1950census.archives.gov/iiif/2/1950census%2F43290879-Pennsylvania%2F43290879-Pennsylvania-057476%2F43290879-Pennsylvania-057476-0038.jpg/full/full/0/default.jpg
  (The IIIF path pattern — .../43290879-Pennsylvania-057476-00NN.jpg/full/full/0/default.jpg — generalises to the other sheets of this ED by changing the trailing sheet number.)

• 1880 U.S. Census population schedules, free on Internet Archive, no login
  Philadelphia Wards 6–7, ED 107 sheet 5 – ED 135 sheet 10 (764 pp): https://archive.org/details/populationsc18801170unit
  Philadelphia Wards 7–9, ED 135 sheet 11 – ED 164 sheet 16 (763 pp): https://archive.org/details/populationsc18801171unit
  1870 Philadelphia Ward 7 (no address column): https://archive.org/details/populationschedu1392unit
  Page images: https://archive.org/download/<id>/page/n<N>_w2000.jpg

• Wikimedia Commons holds the 1940 ED descriptions and maps for Philadelphia County free (~1,066 files, e.g. "File:1940 Census Enumeration District Descriptions - Pennsylvania - Philadelphia County - ED 51-159 - NARA - 5858784.jpg") — but NOT the population schedules.

• City of Philadelphia Department of Records, Historic Street Name Index (the 1897 Waverly consolidation table)
  https://www.phillyhistory.org/HistoricStreets/ — ASP.NET form, queryable by POST; enter "Waverly" in Street Name and click Find. 04 recommends publishing the returned table verbatim as a document page.

• City of Philadelphia open data — all public, all quotable, all directly linkable:
  OPA record for 1822 Pine: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20*%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20location=%271822%20PINE%20ST%27
  OPA row, whole block 1800–1836 even: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20location,year_built,year_built_estimate,frontage,depth,total_livable_area,building_code_description,category_code_description%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20street_name=%27PINE%27%20AND%20house_number::int%20BETWEEN%201800%20AND%201836%20AND%20mod(house_number::int,2)=0%20ORDER%20BY%20house_number::int&format=csv
  OPA, 1800 block Waverly (returns 0 rows): https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20location%20FROM%20opa_properties_public%20WHERE%20location%20LIKE%20%2718__%20WAVERLY%20ST%27
  L&I permits for 1822 Pine: https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql?q=SELECT%20permitnumber,permitissuedate,permitdescription,typeofwork,approvedscopeofwork,contractorname%20FROM%20permits%20WHERE%20address=%271822%20PINE%20ST%27%20ORDER%20BY%20permitissuedate
  AIS address points, 1800 block Waverly: https://services.arcgis.com/fLeGjb7u4uXqeF9q/ArcGIS/rest/services/AIS_Address_Points/FeatureServer/0/query?where=street_name%3D%27WAVERLY%27+AND+address+BETWEEN+1800+AND+1899&outFields=street_address&f=json
  PWD parcel polygons, 1818–1826 Pine (GeoJSON): https://services.arcgis.com/fLeGjb7u4uXqeF9q/ArcGIS/rest/services/PWD_PARCELS/FeatureServer/0/query?where=ADDRESS%20IN%20('1818%20PINE%20ST','1820%20PINE%20ST','1822%20PINE%20ST','1824%20PINE%20ST','1826%20PINE%20ST')&outFields=ADDRESS,PARCELID&outSR=4326&f=geojson

Layers that do NOT help: 1933 Zoning Map (PRD1933.Phila) returns a pure white image for this block — the layer does not cover 1822 Pine. The "HSF" items in address results (HSF.D2D19, HSF.D2H1, HSF.D2H7A, HSF.D2H12) are Historical Society of Frankford copies of state and city maps — nothing property-level.
