  NOTE: the maps1822 images are derivatives of GeoHistory plates and inherit the G7 rights restriction.

──── 5.F  RETRIEVAL TECHNIQUES THE LENSES ESTABLISHED (reusable, not assets) ────
• phila.gov, npgallery.nps.gov and philadelphiabuildings.org all serve fine to curl with a Chrome User-Agent but reject WebFetch or bare curl. philageohistory.org via WebFetch returns 403; it works fine via curl with a normal browser User-Agent — don't conclude the site is down.
• pypdf is installed, so any nomination PDF with an OCR layer can be turned into a greppable text file in one command.
• PAB has a browse-by-block view: the 1800 block of Pine is https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/view_street.cfm/64820?PJ_GRID_BLOCKNUMBER=18 — "39 records displayed."
• PAB location search (https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/search_location.cfm) is behind a login; project pages are public once you have an id; Pine Street ids cluster around 75400–75900; pj_search_results.cfm no longer exists.

════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
6. GUARDRAILS — WHAT MUST NOT BE PUBLISHED, OR MUST BE WORDED A SPECIFIC WAY
════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

G1. DO NOT SAY "Penn named it Pine Street in 1683" AND CITE THE 1683 LETTER. The word "Pine" does not occur anywhere in Penn's pamphlet; it describes only the grid's geometry. The naming attribution is secondary-source only. [01-F1, 01-N1]

G2. THE "UN-QUAKERLY RENAMING" ANECDOTE MUST BE TOLD AS A STORY ABOUT THE STREET GRID, NOT AS A SOURCED STATEMENT ABOUT PINE. The PHMC marker text lists "Chestnut, Cranberry, Locust, Mulberry, Strawberry, 'Wallnut,' and Vine" — Pine is not in the list, and the page gives no date for the renaming. [01-F3]

G3. DO NOT SAY JOHN McCREA BUILT 1822 PINE. Stated bluntly in the source: the 1983 nomination places McCrea on the 1700 block of Pine and the 1800 block of DELANCEY. It does not place him on the 1800 block of Pine. It is sourced that he built the block immediately east and the block immediately north-and-around-the-corner. "That is not permission to say he built 1822 Pine." PAB attributes no builder and no architect to the 1800 block. [01-F18, 01-N6]

G4. DO NOT PUBLISH THE OPA "1800" AS A BUILD DATE. The City itself flags it year_built_estimate = "Y". Publishing the fact that the record is flagged as an estimate is fine; publishing the number as a date is not. [07-§4, 04-F8]

G5. DO NOT PUBLISH THE TRAILING "DATE" COLUMN VALUES FROM THE OPA-COMPLIANT REGISTER LIST (1800 PINE ST → 1840, 1834 → 1845, 1836 → 1845) AS CONSTRUCTION DATES. The column's source could not be established. [C] [01-F11]

G6. DO NOT CITE OPA's "number_stories" FIELD. It says 2 for 1822 and 3 for 1824 while both carry building code "ROW 4 STY MASONRY" — the field is unreliable here. [07-§4]

G7. GEOHISTORY MAP PLATES ARE NOT FREE TO REPUBLISH. Every plate page carries: "Greater Philadelphia GeoHistory Network / PhilaGeoHistory.org — A project of the Athenaeum of Philadelphia / Permission is required to duplicate or publish materials on this site." Underlying scans belong to the Free Library of Philadelphia Map Collection (Hexamer & Locher, Smedley, both Land Use Maps, the Dallin aerials) and the Athenaeum (the Bromleys). LINK to the plate pages and the viewer; clear any image you want to host. [07-§7]

G8. DO NOT PUBLISH AN INTERPRETATION OF THE HEXAMER & LOCHER PAIRED DOTS. Not confirmed against the atlas legend. The storey numerals ("4.", "3.") are unambiguous and safe. [07-§3]

G9. DO NOT USE THE 1862 SMEDLEY PLATE AS EVIDENCE THE REAR ELL WAS ABSENT. It is generalised — it hatches a band along the Pine frontage only. Not probative on footprint. Likewise 1875 Hopkins generalises building masses and must NOT be read as the rear building having been removed; treat it as low-detail. [07-§3]

G10. DO NOT PUBLISH A 1934 APPRAISAL-MAP GRADE FOR THIS BLOCK. The scan disintegrates at the needed magnification and no grade could be read reliably. [07 negatives]

G11. DO NOT CLAIM THE DALLIN c.1930 AERIAL SHOWS THIS BLOCK. The block was not confirmed; individual houses cannot be separated and no landmark fixes the frame. [07 negatives]

G12. DO NOT PUBLISH THE 1800-BLOCK ADDISON DIRECTORY HOUSEHOLDS AS BLACK HOUSEHOLDS. The directory does not state race. Publish the occupations verbatim, and let Du Bois's 1899 identification of Addison Street as the centre of "one of the best Negro residence sections of the city" stand as separately-sourced context. That is the honest construction. [04-F11]

G13. DO NOT WRITE THAT 1822 PINE ITSELF WAS SURVEYED BY ISABEL EATON. Labelled inference [B]: Eaton's canvass covered the residence streets of the ward, and the 1800 block of Pine is a residence street of that ward, so the block was inside the survey area. The house being surveyed is not established. [04-F12]

G14. IF YOU USE DU BOIS'S "WAVERLY STREET… FAIRLY SWARMS" QUOTATION, SAY EXPLICITLY THAT IT IS ABOUT THE 1100 BLOCK, NOT THE 1800 BLOCK. [04-F13]

G15. DO NOT CALL THIS BLOCK RAMCAT. 1822 Pine is at 18th Street; the Ramcat / Schuylkill Historic District (NRHP #86000055, listed January 8, 1986) begins at 23rd. [04-F15]

G16. DO NOT CLAIM A PERIOD SOURCE FOR "GOOSETOWN" OR "RAMCAT." Neither appears in Cohen 1922 nor in the 8.9 MB Gopsill's 1871. Treat Goosetown as [B] with the HSP/PhillyVoice attribution; treat Ramcat's root as Dennis Clark's 1975 essay, reached only through summaries. [04 negatives]

G17. DO NOT PUBLISH "the oldest continuously-operating antiques district in the country." [C] — appears only in travel/SEO content (Islands.com, Tripadvisor, shopping directories, AI-generated attraction pages). No historical, municipal, or trade-press source. [01-F31]

G18. IF THE SITE SAYS ANTIQUE ROW IS A "DISTRICT," IT SHOULD SAY "INFORMALLY KNOWN AS." No Historical Commission designation, no National Register listing, no City ordinance creating or bounding it. Boundaries are unsettled across sources (9th–12th, 9th–Broad, 9th–17th). [01-F32, 01-N7]

G19. DO NOT PUBLISH PINE STREET'S ONE-WAY DIRECTION FROM MEMORY. Not stated on any City page reached. Check the Streets Department. [01-F36, 01-N9]

G20. STATE THE FEDERAL DISTRICT RELATIONSHIP AS "WITHIN THE BOUNDARY AS DESCRIBED," NOT "FEDERALLY LISTED." 1822 Pine is not enumerated in the 1983 federal document. [01-F14]

G21. DO NOT PUBLISH "the house is in PAB as the Spencer Residence" WITHOUT A SPECIFIC pj_display.cfm PROJECT URL. And do not use ho_display.cfm/75343 — that is Spencer Ervin, Lower Merion, 1925–1927. [07-§6]

G22. DO NOT PUBLISH MARY CUTHBERT (1865, "Cuthbert Mary, 1822 Pine") AS A SECOND HOUSEHOLD. The line is genuine [A]; her status is unresolved [C]. [08-F3]

G23. DO NOT GUESS WHAT COMPANY HOWARD SPENCER WAS PRESIDENT OF. The 1871 directory says only "pres" with an office at 233 S 3d. [04-F23]

G24. THE 1863 McELROY'S ABSENCE IS NOT EVIDENCE OF VACANCY. The volume is ~25% smaller than its neighbours — treat as a volume/OCR gap. [08 negatives]

G25. FLAG THE WARD DISCREPANCY IN-LINE, DO NOT SILENTLY HARMONISE IT. The 1985/2013 deeds recite "Eighth Ward"; the census ward is the Seventh. [04-F9]

G26. THE 1808 / 1810 IN THE INVENTORY ENTRY ARE ADDRESSES, NOT YEARS. "1808, reduced one story, metal cornice; 1810, altered c. 1880 cornice and door." [01-F5]

G27. THE 1995 CITY INVENTORY TRANSCRIPTION IS A RECONSTRUCTED WORD ORDER. 01 states plainly that the PDF's text layer interleaves the two printed columns; every word is present verbatim but the ORDER is a reconstruction, independently confirmed by the 1983 NR wording. Say so if publishing the transcription. [01-F5]

G28. DO NOT ASSERT THE McCREA AT 1825 PINE IS THE BUILDER. "M'Crea James A., M.D., 1825 Pine" (1860) is a physician and is not evidence about the builder. [08-F5]

G29. DO NOT LET "Spencer Carriage House and Stable" INTO THE ARCHIVE. It surfaces on searches combining Spencer + carriage house; verbatim first line: "Spencer Carriage House and Stable is an historic structure located in the Dupont Circle neighborhood of Washington, D.C." Built 1905. Nothing to do with this house or family. [04 negatives]

G30. VERIFY BEFORE ASSERTING: St. Peter's Episcopal Church (Third and Pine) details are [B] from Wikipedia and were NOT checked against an NPS record. Old Pine Street Church's "only Colonial Presbyterian church in Philadelphia still on its original foundation" claim rests on partisan/tertiary sources ([B]) — verify before asserting the "only" claim. [01-F34, 01-F35]

G31. THE 1950 CENSUS "no one home" READING IS GRADE B, NOT A. Clear cellophane tape crosses columns 2–5 on sheet 38 and the house numbers sit slightly low in their rows; "1,822" and "no one home" are individually legible but their row alignment is the residual uncertainty. The call-back entry was not located. The 1950 census does NOT tell us whether 1822 was one dwelling unit or five. [08-F6]

G32. METHODOLOGICAL WARNING TO CARRY FORWARD: the 1850, 1860 and 1870 federal censuses have no street-address column. There is no address-based route into the Roset decades; find the household by name, and for 1859–1871 the names are the tenants (Broadhead / McNeill / Smith / Trimble / Spencer), not "Roset". [08 Part 3]

# EXTRACT C — Domestic life, twentieth century, medical practice, access notes

GRADE A FINDINGS
===================================================================

--- NINETEENTH-CENTURY DOMESTIC PRACTICE (period type, not this house) ---

• [A] Louis Dreka, *Card Etiquette*, Philadelphia, 1873 — a Philadelphia card engraver's own manual; establishes the printed "reception day" as an institution. URL: https://archive.org/details/drekascardetique00drek
  Quote: "Reception days are mentioned on Visiting Cards of those having stated reception days" — specimen card beneath reads "Thursdays. 1364 Walnut Street."

• [A] Dreka 1873, on why the card mattered at the door. Same URL.
  Quote: "You call on your friend, and if you have no Card you must trust your name to a servant, who, it is more than probable, will make a mistake…"

• [A] Dreka 1873, the four turned-down corners, engraved on the card reverse. Same URL.
  Quote: "on the upper left hand corner of the card is the word *Visite*; on the upper right corner, *Felicitation*; on the lower left corner, *Congé*; on the lower right corner, *Condolence*." Dreka adds: "Turning down corners is not now much used, and disfigures the card."

• [A] Dreka 1873, the P.P.C. card — the mechanical link between calling and seasonal migration. Same URL.
  Quote: "Cards sent to friends before leaving for a journey, are issued with simply P. P. C. in lower left corner"

• [A] Mrs. H. O. Ward, *Sensible Etiquette of the Best Society*, Philadelphia: Porter & Coates, 10th rev. ed., 1878, ch. "CALLS AND CARDS," p. 76 — the servant's role on an At Home day. URL: https://archive.org/details/sensibleetique00bloo
  Quote: "On reception days, the well-drilled servant in American cities receives the card of the caller on a small salver, and, of course, suppresses it…"

• [A] Ward 1878, pp. 76–77, colliding At Home days. Same URL.
  Quote: "Where a lady has one day in every week, and this day for some reason is an inconvenient one to some of her friends, whose day may be the same… the call [should be] made upon some other day."

• [A] Ward 1878, p. 101, the servant screening callers. Same URL.
  Quote: "a well-trained servant soon learns to discriminate between those ladies who are calling merely to leave their cards and those who are really desirous of being admitted."

• [A] Ward 1878, p. 76, the American/English contrast — the London footman "is trained to announce the name distinctly as he ushers the caller into the room where his mistress is receiving," whereas in America "the servant has not had previous instructions… in reference to his mistress being at home, or not at home, or engaged." Same URL.

• [A] Ward 1878, p. 57 — the seasonal migration in nine words, from a Philadelphia press. Same URL.
  Quote: "P. P. C. cards are no longer left when the absence from home is only for a few months, as for the summer… as residents are then supposed to be out of town."

• [A] Ward 1878, pp. 57–59, etiquette of the summer colony. Same URL.
  Quote: "At places of summer resort, those who own their cottages call first upon those who rent them; and those who rent, in turn, call upon each other, according to the priority of arrival."

--- THE PHILADELPHIA ROWHOUSE AS A MACHINE, 1859 (Sloan; type not this house) ---

All from [A] Samuel Sloan, *City and Suburban Architecture*, Philadelphia: J. B. Lippincott, 1859, Design II "A CITY RESIDENCE," pp. 23–27. URL: https://archive.org/details/gri_33125013609264

• [A] The double parlour, p. 25.
  Quote: "The drawing-room, which is divided by columns and an elliptical archway, is lighted by two windows in front and one on the rear; each division is about sixteen by twenty-three feet."

• [A] Marble and plaster, p. 25: "An oval centre-piece of three and a half by six feet will be placed in each division of the drawing-room" and "Marble mantles of approved pattern will be provided and set one in each division of the drawing-room."

• [A] The service stair, p. 25.
  Quote: "Rear of this vestibule is a flight of stairs for the use of servants, under which are the stairs leading to the cellar."

• [A] Servants' hall and dumbwaiter, p. 25.
  Quote: "Accessible from the kitchen, through the servants' hall, is the butler's pantry, in which is a sink, and a dumb-waiter, which communicates with the pantry above, adjoining the principal dining-room."

• [A] Bells and speaking tube, p. 41 ("IRON-WORK," of another design).
  Quote: "The front door-bell will require a pull to correspond with the knob of the door, and four inside bells are necessary; also, a speaking-tube from the pantry to the kitchen…"

• [A] Hierarchy of finish encoded in hardware, same spec.
  Quote: "The knobs of the front and vestibule doors, and all the hinges… will be silver-plated; the other knobs for the first story, and all in the second, will be the best quality of white porcelain; the knobs for the basement and attic will be mineral."

• [A] The furnace displacing the hearth, p. 74.
  Quote: "Heating by furnaces in the cellar has become quite common, and in view of its many advantages it seems probable that a few more years will make it almost universal in city residences."

• [A] Heating/ventilating mechanism, p. 74: "A flue warming each room by a register near the floor, ventilates it by one placed near the ceiling."

• [A] Plumbing spec, p. 27.
  Quote: "The bath-tubs and sinks lined with copper, tinned and planished… A galvanized iron circulating boiler and water-back will be attached to the kitchen-range, to supply hot water…"

• [A] Gas spec, p. 27.
  Quote: "Each division of the drawing-room will have a chandelier with twelve burners, and two side-lights… All other rooms, including baths and butler's pantry, will have one burner each."

• [A] Philadelphia hook, p. 87: the furnace Sloan recommends "is the invention of Mr. Thomas T. Tasker, of Philadelphia."

--- SERVANTS ON THIS STREET: DU BOIS AND EATON, 1899 ---

• [A] W. E. B. Du Bois, *The Philadelphia Negro: A Social Study*, Philadelphia, 1899, p. 1 — the canvass district. URL: https://archive.org/details/philadelphianegr00dubo
  Quote: "The work commenced with a house-to-house canvass of the Seventh Ward. This long narrow ward, extending from South Seventh street to the Schuylkill River and from Spruce street to South street…"

• [A] Isabel Eaton, "Special Report on Negro Domestic Service in the Seventh Ward, Philadelphia," bound as pp. 425–509 of the same volume — Table IX, "Distribution of Colored Service with Reference to the Fashionable Quarter," p. 451, counts Pine Street servants block-group by block-group: "99 domestics, or 58 per cent of all colored domestic servants on Pine Street" east of Broad, against "71 domestics, or 42 per cent…" west. Same URL.
  Eaton's reading, p. 451: "The smaller number of colored domestics employed in the fashionable section is noticeable both on Pine and Spruce streets."

• [A] Eaton, p. 450, a Philadelphia butler's explanation. Same URL.
  Quote: "You see they (the employers) go to Europe and bring home Englishmen, and that knocks out 'the Negro.'" And: "it seems probable that the fashion of importing English and French service has an appreciable effect."

• [A] Eaton, p. 444, the staffing grades of a Philadelphia house. Same URL.
  Quote: "If another grade be supplied between the second and third given here — a grade found in well-to-do Philadelphia families, where two women servants are employed — this grading of London service applies very fairly…"

• [A] Eaton, p. 444, what a single servant actually did, in her own words. Same URL.
  Quote: the one woman does the "cooking, washing, ironing, and drags up all the ashes, tends furnace, cleans the front, and does every single thing."

• [A] Eaton, Table XI, p. 454, living in. Same URL.
  Quote: "only 50 per cent of the colored women in service and only 24 per cent of the colored men lodge at their employers' establishments." Among single women, "only 38.7 per cent of them going home at night."

• [A] Eaton, Table V, p. 445, sub-occupations: Butler 109, Coachman 76, Waiter 387, Cook 47, Valet 4 (male); Chambermaid 114, Waitress 44, Child's nurse 21 (female). Same URL.

• [A] Du Bois, p. 205 — Thursday was the day out. Same URL.
  Quote: "Thursday afternoons and evenings, when the servant girls are free, are always sure to have some sort of entertainment."

• [A] Du Bois, p. 231 footnote. Same URL.
  Quote: "The members of the Berean Union have opened a 'Y' parlor, where young colored girls employed as domestics can spend their Thursday afternoon… The parlor is open from 4 until 10 p. m., every Thursday."

• [A] Eaton, p. 458, the shape of the working day. Same URL.
  Quote: "Household work is a ceaseless round, which like woman's work, is 'never done.' And the private domestic… must always hold himself in readiness to answer any call at a moment's notice."

--- NEWSPAPER PAGES NAMING 1822 PINE (Pennsylvania Newspaper Archive, Penn State) ---

• [A] SERVANT-WANTED ADVERTISEMENT PLACED FROM 1822 PINE STREET. *Evening Public Ledger*, Monday, May 7, 1917, p. 16, "Help Wanted — Female." URL: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1917-05-07/ed-1/seq-16/
  OCR verbatim: `CHAXIIU'llXlAlD. while, v p ." vv allied until / .lime 1" prlv fum , no wash ; ref ren , car / fare pild Apil Xlnndiv n m., 1822 Pine st`
  Reading: "CHAMBERMAID, white, w[anted] until June 1[st]; priv[ate] fam[ily], no wash; ref[e]ren[ces], carfare paid. Appl[y] Monday a.m., 1822 Pine st." (OCR is poor; page image must be checked before publication.)

• [A for the printed line / B for the identification] A DEATH AT 1822 PINE STREET, MAY 1918. *Evening Public Ledger*, Wednesday, May 22, 1918, p. 18, death-notice column. URL: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1918-05-22/ed-1/seq-18/
  OCR verbatim (left column edge cropped in the scan): `LguddenlT, at 1822 Pine st May / Bfl LAWTQN. xrtdow of Henry a / ri, pervices ana int. private.`
  Reading: "[S]uddenly, at 1822 Pine st., May [21], … LAWTON, widow of Henry [C.] …; services and int[erment] private." Grade A for "a sudden death at 1822 Pine Street published May 22, 1918"; grade B for naming her Naomi Lawton Davis — the surname is lost in the scan gutter.

• [A] A SECOND 1915 SUFFRAGE TICKET NOTICE, five days after the one already in the archive. *Evening Public Ledger*, Wednesday, January 13, 1915, p. 11. URL: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1915-01-13/ed-1/seq-11/
  Verbatim: "Several hundred tickets have been sold. They are $1.50 apiece and may be bought from Mrs. Horatio Gates Lloyd, of Haverford; Miss Martha Davis, 1822 Pine street, and at the Equal Franchise Society headquarters, 33 South 9th street."
  Column also names "Caroline Katzenstein," "Miss Sophia H. Dulles, Miss Harriet W. Dulles, Dr. Frances Van Gasken." Gives the EFS headquarters address (33 South 9th Street), which the site does not currently carry.

• [A] NOVEMBER 1866: A HOUSEHOLD NAMED AT 1822 PINE IN A SEWING-MACHINE TESTIMONIAL. *The Evening Telegraph* (Philadelphia), Saturday, November 17, 1866, p. 8. URL: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1866-11-17/ed-1/seq-8/
  Advertisement heading verbatim: "REFERENCE. To some of the First Families in Philadelphia who have had the Willcox & Gibbs Machines in Use for Five Years and Upwards."
  List entry verbatim: "smith. 8. Decatur. o 1822 Pine street," — i.e. "Smith, S. Decatur, No. 1822 Pine street." Two consequences: a Willcox & Gibbs sewing machine in use at 1822 Pine since roughly 1861; and in November 1866 the printed occupant is S. Decatur Smith, not a Roset.

• [A] A dated Philadelphia gas price on that same 1866 page: the Cresson Gas Regulator advertisement prints an office's meter readings and the rate — gas "at 3 30 per thousand (including United States tax, 80 per M.)". Same URL as above.

• [A] Designation-document narrative, the post-WWI apartment wave (PDF pp. 6–7, 9, 12; OCR'd from re-rendered pages, since pp.1–20 carry no embedded text). Source URL as above.
  Verbatim: "The post-World War I era is represented by a significant group of apartment buildings that replaced many of the first generation mansions, especially around Rittenhouse Square."

• [A] Same document, the 1920s renovation turn.
  Verbatim: "Instead of replacing the tiny older houses on the back streets, families of the post-war years found charm in the narrow streets, small windows, and traditional forms." Architect William Koelle "specialized in such adaptations for [the] cream of old Philadelphia society."

• [A] Same document, on the district's survival.
  Verbatim: "The fact that Rittenhouse remained continuously fashionable has meant that its buildings have suffered few of the indignities of other neighborhoods. No large tracts have been emptied and reconstructed within the boundaries of the district…"

• [A] Same document, Statement of Significance: "The Rittenhouse/Fitler Residential district is a cohesive and essentially intact Victorian and twentieth century neighborhood reflecting the architectural and social history of Philadelphia."

--- THE MEDICAL HOUSE, 1919–1937 ---

• [A] Dr. Henry Paul Brown, Jr. at 1822 Pine, 1921 — visually verified on the page image. *American Medical Directory*, 7th ed. (AMA, 1921), Philadelphia listing. URL: https://archive.org/details/americanmedicald1921ameruoft/page/n1399/mode/1up
  Verbatim: "BROWN, HENRY PAUL, JR., b'88; Pa.1,'12; l'12; ⊕ S★ D4—1822 Pine St.; until 10"
  Decoded from the volume's own key pages: capitals = County and State society member; ⊕ = AMA member; b'88 = born 1888; Pa.1 = "Univ. of Pa. Sch. of Med., Philadelphia," class of 1912; l'12 = licensed 1912; S = "S.—Surgeon"; D4 = "Philadelphia Pediatric Society." "until 10" is his morning consulting hour at that address, with no separate office given.

  – vol. 42 (1920): identical line. https://archive.org/details/transactionsofco3421coll
  – vol. 43 (1921): "1919. Brown, Henry P., Jr., M.D., Assistant Surgeon, Presbyterian and Pennsylvania Hospitals; Dispensary Surgeon, Children's Hospital. 1822 Pine St." https://archive.org/details/transactionsofco3431coll

  Verbatim: "HENRY PAUL BROWN, Jr. b. Feb. 13, 1888; B. S., 1909; M. D., 1912; Psi Upsilon; Physician; mem. Medical Corps, U. S. A., June, 1917-Jan., 1919; mem. University Club. 1822 Pine St., Phila., Pa."

  Verbatim: "1915. PFEIFFER, DAMON B., A.B., M.D., Assistant Professor of Surgery, Graduate School of Medicine, University of Pennsylvania; Surgeon, Methodist and Abington Memorial Hospitals; Assistant Surgeon, Presbyterian and Lankenau Hospitals. 1822 Pine St."

Sheet header: "d. E. D. NUMBER 51-158", "DATE SHEET STARTED May 10, 1950", enumerator signature reading as "Margaret Hoenem[e]r".

The "1822" reading was verified at 5000px.

• [A] THE NEIGHBOURING HOUSES WERE CUT INTO APARTMENTS BY 1950, floor by floor, with doctors' offices at street level. Same ED 51-158, verbatim from the house-number/street and name columns:
  – Image 34: "1836" with a stacked run — "1st fl 491", "1st fl R — 492", "2nd fl R 493", "3rd fl rear 494 — Schnell Harriet", "3rd fl 495 — Newman Harold", "4th fl 496 — Graff Walter", "3rd fl 497"; then "1834 / 498 / Doctors Offices"; "2nd fl 499 — Scarf Maxwell". Also at 1809 Pine: "4th fl 478 — Basement Apt".
  – Image 35: "1st f. | 1832 Pine | 501 — Goldberg Bessie, Head"; "2nd f 502", "2nd 504", "3rd f 505", "1st fl 506"; "1830 | 507 — 1st Floor 1 Dent- Frank", "508 — 1st fl. Rear vacant", "509 — Haig Daniel W / Katherine / John W"; "2nd 510", "3rd 511", "512 — Brown Alfred Head; Herbert James partner; Morgenstern Marvin partner; Rosenberg Larry partner" (four unrelated men, ages ~20-23, one unit).
  – Image 36: "1828 Pine | 513 — Sokatz Harry A / Rosalie"; "2nd fl 514"; "1826 | 516 — 1st Floor Vacant"; "1826 3rd fl | 517 — Rose Naomi"; "2nd fl 518"; "4th Rear | 522 — McHugh John E / Helen M"; "525 — Office (Doctor)".
  – Image 37: line 9 note "vacant apt 1819 2nd F".
  – Image 38: "182[6] … Pine St. | 1st fl 546 — Apt 1st Floor Doctors"; "3rd 547 — Lee, John Paul / Marie Craig"; "2nd fl 548 — Salzmann Harry M" (six-person household).
  Citable bottom line: in April–May 1950 the south side of the 1800 block of Pine was a floor-by-floor apartment street with ground-floor medical offices; 1836, 1834, 1832, 1830, 1828, 1826 and 1824 all show multiple dwelling-unit serials or explicit floor designations. 1822 alone shows one serial and "no one home."

  – Appendix p.436 (PDF p.21), title: "AN ORDINANCE Regulating and restricting the height, location, size, bulk and use of buildings and/or land in Philadelphia… and providing for the creation of a Bureau of Zoning… designated as the Bureau of Engineering, Surveys and Zoning…" — the exact agency that produced every deed-registry card in the archive.
  – Appendix p.437 (PDF p.23), definitions: "(f) A multiple dwelling shall be a dwelling occupied by three (3) or more families, each living independently of the other."
  – Appendix p.440 (PDF p.26), "Section 4. NON-CONFORMING USE.": "Any building or the use of any building existing at the time of the passage of this ordinance that does not conform… shall be considered a non-conforming building or use, and may continue such use in its present location…"
  – Appendix pp.503–504 (PDF pp.89–90), "PERMITS AND CERTIFICATES": "Use registration permits shall be required for every use commenced after the passage of this ordinance… provided, however, that single-family dwellings, duplex dwellings, churches, chapels or other places of worship are exempt from this regulation."

CORRECTIONS
===================================================================

1. "Victorian mansion" → late Greek Revival townhouse.
   WRONG (site): describes the house as a Victorian mansion.
   CORRECT: one of "Nineteen, 4-story, 2 bay, brick, late Greek Revival townhouses, marble trim and surrounds."
   EVIDENCE: Philadelphia Register designation inventory p.54 = PDF p.88, https://www.phila.gov/media/20190213131150/Historic-District-Rittenhouse-Fitler.pdf ; corroborated by the 1983 federal nomination inventory ("1800-36 4-story brick, late Greek Revival townhouses…"), https://npgallery.nps.gov/NRHP/GetAsset/NRHP/83002277_text . Closes repo Open Question #10. (Agent 5 and Agent 2 independently reach the same correction.)

2. National Register reference number for the Rittenhouse district.
   WRONG (repo): #92001878 — that is RittenhouseTown.
   CORRECTION PER AGENT 2: use NRIS 83002277, "Rittenhouse Historic District," Philadelphia County, listed 8/25/1983 (from the Supplementary Listing Record bound at the front of the nomination PDF).
   CONFLICTING GUIDANCE PER AGENT 5: publish NO National Register reference number for the *Rittenhouse-Fitler Residential Historic District* — that district's number was NOT FOUND; 81000557 is "Rittenhouse Square" the park (Four Public Squares of Philadelphia TR, listed 9/14/1981, https://npgallery.nps.gov/AssetDetail/NRIS/81000557), and Wikipedia's "Rittenhouse–Fitler Historic District … 81000557 … September 14, 1981" conflates the two. Agent 5 adds 81000557 to the do-not-publish list alongside 92001878.
   NET: 83002277 is the *federal* "Rittenhouse Historic District" nomination Agent 2 actually opened and quoted; it is not established that it is the same entity as the City's Rittenhouse-Fitler district. Do not publish either number as the Rittenhouse-Fitler NR number.

3. "Goosetown" → "Ramcat."
   EVIDENCE [B]: Preservation Alliance for Greater Philadelphia, "Rittenhouse West" walking-tour brochure, https://preservationalliance.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/01/RW.pdf — "Once known as 'Ramcat,' the neighborhood remained for decades an Irish enclave whose men did backbreaking labor and whose women served as maids and housekeepers in the fashionable households further east." Closes repo Open Question #11 with a second independent source.
   CAVEAT [Agent 11]: no 19th-century source for "Goosetown" OR "Ramcat" — zero occurrences of either in Cohen's *Rittenhouse Square, Past and Present* (1922, https://archive.org/details/rittenhousesquar00cohe) or in the 1871 directory. The oldest citable authority on Ramcat is Dennis Clark's essay in *The Divided Metropolis* (1975), reached only through summaries.

4. Dr. Damon B. Pfeiffer's dates.
   WRONG (repo): "Dr. Damon B. Pfeiffer — 1822 Pine St, College of Physicians roll, by 1915."
   CORRECT: Pfeiffer is at 1822 Pine from 1922, not 1915. The "1915" is his year of *election as a Fellow*, printed at the head of every one of his entries.
   EVIDENCE: College of Physicians roll for 1915, 1916, 1917, 1918 and 1921 all give 2028 Pine St. — e.g. *Trans.* vol. 37 (1915): "1915. Pfeiffer, Damon B., A.B., M.D., Instructor in Surgery, University of Pennsylvania; Assistant Surgeon, University Hospital; Pathologist to the German Hospital; Director of the Clinical Laboratory, Presbyterian Hospital. 2028 Pine St." https://archive.org/details/transactionsofco3371coll . *American Medical Directory* 1912 puts him at "1953 Locust St.", 1921 at "2028 Pine St."

5. Stillwell Corson Burns was never at 1822 Pine.
   WRONG (repo): "Stillwell Corson Burns — Surgery, 1822 Pine St, 1916 register."
   CORRECT: not supported, and contradicted. Delete.
   EVIDENCE: College of Physicians roll gives him 1326 Spring Garden St. in the 1914–1918 volumes — *Trans.* vol. 38 (1916): "1906. Burns, Stillwell C, M.D., Lecturer on Surgery in the Medico-Chirurgical College; Assistant Surgeon to the Medico-Chirurgical Hospital. 1326 Spring Garden St." https://archive.org/details/transactionsofco3381coll — and 1925 Spring Garden St. in every volume 1919 through 1932. *American Medical Directory* 1921: "Burns, Stillwell Corson (28) 1925 Spring Garden St." He never appears at 1822 Pine in any source searched.

6. "Roset family home, 1854–1893."
   WRONG (site framing): implies continuous Roset residence.
   CORRECT: temper it — the ownership was a tenancy succession. In November 1866 the printed occupant of 1822 Pine is S. Decatur Smith.
   EVIDENCE: *The Evening Telegraph*, Nov 17, 1866, p. 8, Willcox & Gibbs testimonial list, "Smith, S. Decatur, No. 1822 Pine street" — https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1866-11-17/ed-1/seq-8/ . Corroborates the repo's own bench item 20. Agent 11 adds: for 1859–1871 the names to search in census are the TENANTS — Broadhead, McNeill, Smith, Trimble, Spencer — not "Roset."

7. William McLean is no longer an unsourced [C] name.
   WRONG (repo): carries William McLean as an unsourced [C] name for a Spencer estate executor.
   CORRECT: he is the grantor of record on the Apr 3, 1899 conveyance. Agent 11 upgrades him to [B].
   EVIDENCE: *Builders' Guide* v.14 (1899): "Pine st No 1822, Wm McLean to Agnes M Spencer, Apr 3 99, 22 ft x 90 ft . . 17500" — https://archive.org/details/PhilaBuildersGuide_v14_1899

8. "Henry S. Pleasants" as executor of Graham Spencer's estate.
   No source found. That name stays [C]. (Agent 11, 8.3.)

9. The 2013 deed's date and price, and the 2002 price.
   The six orphaned facts on /history/provenance can be re-cited to a live, free, linkable source: document 52683090, 7/18/2013, recorded 8/19/2013, $1,555,000. NEW sourced fact: the 2002 deed price, $1,200,000 (document 50510515). EVIDENCE: `rtt_summary` via https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql .

10. "Five dwelling units" vs. RM-1 vs. the rental licence.
    CORRECT FRAMING TO PUBLISH: RM-1 is the zoning district; "five (5) dwelling units" on the 2022 property certificate is the registered legal *use*, not a description of the building today; the City's 2020 rental licence records ONE unit and OPA classifies the parcel SINGLE FAMILY. Closes repo Open Question #16. EVIDENCE: `business_licenses` rental licence 851061 (numberofunits: 1) and OPA record 081079100, both via https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql .

11. "Parlor-floor doctor's office."
    WRONG (as a stated fact): no source says "parlor floor," "front room," or describes any office fit-out; no building permit, alteration notice or plan for a medical conversion at 1822 Pine was found. Write it as inference or drop it. [C]

12. The 1899 *Builders' Guide* volume number.
    PAB cites "v. 24, n. 31, p. 489, 8/2/1899." That volume number appears to be an error; Internet Archive's series numbers 1899 as v.14 (consistent with v.13=1898, v.17=1902). The text is in `PhilaBuildersGuide_v14_1899`.

13. "Guy Bryan Schott, 1822 Pine street" — RETRACT.
    Agent 2 reported this as [A] (Section 5e): *Daily Evening Bulletin*, July 9, 1870, p. 4, legal notices, "…the Estate of THOMAS J. BRYAN, deceased… GUY BRYAN SCHOTT, Administrator, 1822 Pine street," running through July–August 1870.
    Agent 11 (8.4, "False leads killed at the image") KILLS IT: "IT IS 1522 PINE. Both images read directly: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn84026016/1870-07-09/ed-1/seq-4/ and https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn84026016/1870-05-25/ed-1/seq-1/". The image reading supersedes the OCR reading. DO NOT PUBLISH Schott at 1822 Pine. Agent 11: "This is the strongest argument in the entire pass for the rule that no address digit goes on the site without someone looking at the page image."

14. The archive's own 1867-04-08 clipping, "M. Errickson, No. 1822 Pine street" — RETRACT. IT IS 1322 PINE, confirmed on the 17 April rerun. It is a medical-electricity advertisement and does not concern this house. (Agent 11, 8.4.)

15. "Meeting at the residence of Miss Martha Davis, 1822 Pine street," April 1919 — RETRACT. IT IS 1823 PINE. (Agent 11, 8.4.)

16. Census ward. 1822 Pine is in census WARD 7, not the "Eighth Ward" the 1985 deed recites. Do not search Ward 8 on the strength of the deed. (Agent 11, 8.2.)

17. "The oldest continuously-operating antiques district in the country" (Antique Row) — ADD TO DO-NOT-PUBLISH. No historical, municipal or trade-press source exists for that phrase. If the site uses the word "district" for Antique Row it must say "informally known as." (Agent 11, 8.3.)

18. Naming convention for the 1918 death. The notices print it as "NAOMI LAWTON, widow of Henry C. Davis" — "Naomi Lawton Davis" as a three-word string returns zero hits anywhere. Search and cite the components. (Agent 11, 8.3.)


RESOLVED QUESTIONS
===================================================================

• Open Question #10, "Victorian mansion" vs late Greek Revival — CLOSED. Both the City's 1995 inventory (PDF p.88) and the 1983 federal nomination call the row "late Greek Revival townhouses." (Agents 2 and 5.)

• Open Question #11, "Goosetown" vs "Ramcat" — CLOSED to the extent a second source can close it: Preservation Alliance "Rittenhouse West" brochure says "Ramcat." (Agent 5, [B].) See caveat in Corrections #3.

• Open Question #16, the "five dwelling units" contradiction — CLOSED. Rental licence 851061 records one unit; OPA classifies SINGLE FAMILY; the certificate records legally permitted use, not present configuration. (Agent 5.)

• Open Question #18 / Finding 3, the 2013 deed — CLOSED without opening a PDF: `rtt_summary` document 52683090, 7/18/2013, recorded 8/19/2013, $1,555,000, Bullard → Price. (Agent 5.)

• Open Question #8, "Who is G. C. Purviss?" — PARTIALLY CLOSED, negatively: he was not a physician (no Purvis/Purviss in the AMA directories for 1912, 1921 or 1929 anywhere in Pennsylvania), and he does not appear near 1822 Pine in the 1905 *Builders' Guide* (`PhilaBuildersGuide_v20_1905`, which carries only Chas B. Purvis of 4236 Market St and an E. Russell Purvis judgment). Still unidentified. (Agents 2 and 6.)

• Repo cheapest-task #6, the 1899 contract award text — CLOSED. Full text recovered from *Builders' Guide* v.14 (1899), including "A new story on the front and rear, plumbing, cement work and considerable interior detail work will be done." Contractor spelling confirmed as J. S. Rodgers. (Agent 2.)

• The 1893 "$14,000" headline — EXPLAINED. It was an auction at the Philadelphia Exchange on Wednesday May 10, 1893, by James A. Freeman & Co.; "four-story residence, lot 22 x 90 feet, $14,000." (Agent 2.)

• The 1899 deed date and price — RESOLVED: Apr 3, 1899, $17,500, from Wm McLean. (Agent 2.)

• The PAB claim about 1822 Pine — VERIFIED AS WRITTEN. PAB project 14707, "Spencer Residence," 1822 PINE ST, Philadelphia Register 2/8/1995. (Agent 2. Note Agent 5 could not re-verify PAB — 403 to WebFetch — and Agent 11 documents the working route.)

• Where "c. 1845" comes from — RESOLVED [B]. It is a PHMC datum propagated through the Athenaeum's database, not a listing-agent invention: PAB's untitled project for "1808-1836 PINE ST" reads "c. 1845 / BUILT / source: PHMC." https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display_alldates.cfm/277174 . The repo's directory argument for 1854 must engage with PHMC. (Agent 2.)

• The designation-form authorship and the 1987→1995 gap — CLOSED. "George E. Thomas/Center City Foundation/edited and submitted by the P.H.C." — "Philadelphia Historical Commission 9 Feb. 1987 for P.H.C." Designated 8 February 1995. (Agent 5.)

• Whether 1822 Pine has an individual Philadelphia landmark designation — CLOSED, negatively. It carries a *district* date only (Rittenhouse Fitler, 2/8/1995), per the Historical Commission's own OPA address list, p.158. (Agent 5.)

• The zoning stamp on the 1941 deed card — the agency is identified with certainty: the Bureau of Engineering, Surveys and Zoning, created by the 1933 ordinance. The deed-registry cards in the archive are artefacts of that ordinance. (Agent 5, [A] for the text.)

• The John McCrea attribution — CLOSED, and now citable rather than inferred. The City designation document names McCrea's blocks (1700 Pine and Addison; both sides of the 1800 and 2000 blocks of Delancey) and never the 1800 block of Pine. Agent 11 confirms from the federal nomination and from PAB, which "attributes NO builder and NO architect to the 1800 block of Pine." Attribute McCrea to the neighborhood, never to 1822 Pine. Do not go looking for a McCrea attribution. Also note several McCreas must be disambiguated, and McElroy's 1860 shows a "M'Crea James A., M.D., 1825 Pine" — a physician on this block who is NOT evidence about the builder.

• Was 1822 Pine ever a rooming house or boarding house — CLOSED, negatively. No evidence found anywhere; the 1950 census gives it one dwelling-unit serial. Say "divided into apartments," never "rooming house" or "boarding house." (Agent 5.)

• Was 1822 Pine a doctor's house — CLOSED and dated. Two Penn-connected surgeons in succession, 1919–1937+, each with 1822 Pine as primary address, stated consulting hours, and no separate office address. Fills the front half of GAP 1. (Agent 6.)

• Whether the pre-1858 number "756 Pine" is findable in free newspapers — CLOSED, negatively and permanently. Zero hits across the entire Penn State PA archive and all of Chronicling America (the three LOC hits are railroad timetables: "leave Auburn at 756 A.M. for Pine grove"). "DO NOT RUN THIS SEARCH AGAIN." The reason is structural coverage, not absence. (Agent 11.)

• Whether a pre-1855 name exists for Waverly at 17th–19th — CLOSED, as a positive finding of absence. The DOR index records no 1858 and no 1897 change for those two blocks; the street is absent from the 1854 directory index and present in 1855. "Stop hunting for a former name." (Agent 11.)

• Whether maps can decide the build date — CLOSED, negatively. "NO PROPERTY-LEVEL MAP EXISTS BETWEEN 1843 AND 1858… MAPS CANNOT DECIDE THE BUILD DATE. Stop looking." (Agent 11.)

• Whether the 1800 block's apartment conversion is in the *Builders' Guide* — CLOSED, negatively for 1901–1940: a full-run full-text search returns exactly four hits, all 1899–1900. (Agent 11. Note Agent 2 states the opposite recommendation — see STILL OPEN.)


STILL OPEN
===================================================================

• The storey count. OPEN CONTRADICTION, explicitly flagged "do not resolve without more evidence." The house is a "four-story residence" in 1893 (*Builders' Guide* v.8), yet in 1899 the architects add "a new story on the front and rear," and the 1983 National Register inventory describes the row as 4-story. Three readings are possible: the 1899 story went on the rear building only; the 1899 work raised the roof in a way the 1983 survey read as four storeys; or one source is wrong. SETTLED BY: the 1899 permit itself, or a dated elevation/plan. Do not publish a storey count as settled.

• Whether the death at 1822 Pine on ~May 21, 1918 is Naomi Lawton Davis. SETTLED BY: reading the page image of *Evening Public Ledger*, May 22, 1918, p. 18 (https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1918-05-22/ed-1/seq-18/) at the cropped left column edge, where the surname sits in the scan gutter. Agent 11 notes no Naomi Davis estate, will, or letters-testamentary notice appears in the EPL after May 1918.

• Whether James Curtis Booth is corroborated as a block neighbour. The 1866 Willcox & Gibbs list carries an entry OCR'd as `Biotn.i (rrolessor of Chemistry in U. S. Mint I, No. 1813 Pine sireet.` — "Bo[o]th (Professor of Chemistry in U. S. Mint), No. 1813 Pine street." [B — OCR garbled]. SETTLED BY: cropping that entry on the page image, https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83025925/1866-11-17/ed-1/seq-8/ .

• Whether the *Builders' Guide* holds the apartment-conversion record. DIRECT DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN AGENTS. Agent 2: no "1822 Pine" hits in 1905, 1922, 1923 or 1924, and recommends "a systematic sweep of all 40 free IA volumes… would very likely close GAP 1." Agent 11: a full-run full-text search of 1901–1940 returns exactly four hits, all 1899–1900 — "The apartment conversion is not in the city's richest building-trade source," while still calling an unblocked systematic sweep "the single most likely thing to close Gap 1 from the trade press." SETTLED BY: a volume-by-volume, whitespace-tolerant sweep of `PhilaBuildersGuide_v{N}_{year}` for v.15 (1900), v.36 (1921) and v.43 (1928), the missing volumes neither agent could search.

• The Roset-era occupancy, 1859–1871. SETTLED BY: name-based census searching on the tenants — Broadhead, McNeill, Smith, Trimble, Spencer — since the 1850, 1860 and 1870 censuses have no street-address column.

• The 1880 census entry for 1822 Pine. A bounded ~50-page window remains: on NARA reel 1170 (Philadelphia Wards 6–7), the ED↔page map established was n409=ED122, n454=124, n499=126, n544=127, n589=129, n634=130, n679=132; streets positively identified were Delancey 1700-1800 at ~n600, Lombard 1900 at ~n655, Naudain at ~n675, Pine 2000 block at ~n710. "THE 1800 BLOCK OF PINE IS SOMEWHERE BETWEEN n648 AND n700 AND WAS NOT FOUND." SETTLED BY: paging https://archive.org/download/populationsc18801170unit/page/n<N>_w2000.jpg through that window.

• The 1900, 1910, 1920, 1930 and 1940 censuses for this address. Blocked by FamilySearch's login gate and by the absence of a free IIIF endpoint for 1940 (NARA's 1940 site is retired; the Catalog API returns only the SPA shell and v2 needs a key). The 1910 census "is one of the two records that would close the Davis/Mott question." SETTLED BY: a FamilySearch login, or a free 1940 IIIF endpoint if one exists — the 1950 method in Section 5 works and is documented.

• Whether 1822 Pine was already a medical address before Brown arrived in 1919. *American Medical Directory* editions for 1916 and 1918 are not freely available anywhere reached; the 1914 IA scan (`americanmedicald1914ameruoft`) is a partial with no 1822 Pine entry; the 1956 edition (`bwb_T3-BRL-425`) is lending-restricted. SETTLED BY: the 1916 or 1918 AMD volume.

• Which floor or room the doctor's office occupied. No source reached says. SETTLED BY: a building permit, alteration notice, or plan for a medical conversion at 1822 Pine — none found in any source.

• What the 1904 "At Home" notice implies about a stated reception day at this house. The ritual is A-grade and Philadelphia-sourced; the connection to the Dec 11, 1904 Inquirer line is labelled [B] inference. SETTLED BY: Boyd's Blue Book (society directory) volumes for 1887-88, 1889, 1890, 1896, 1898, 1900, 1906 — street-arranged, and society blue books of this era print reception days and country residences. HathiTrust IDs: hvd.hxnyj7, hvd.hxisbs, hvd.hxisbm, hvd.hxisbp, hvd.hxisbn, mdp.39015035098444, hvd.hxisbr. All 403 to fetchers. "A human with a browser closes this in an hour."

• Servant wages in a comparable Philadelphia household. HSP Collection 1899, Horstmann–Lippincott family papers; secondary descriptions state the papers include "account books of household expenses, 1864; servant's wages, 1856–1896." UNVERIFIED — the finding aid PDF (https://hsp.org/sites/default/files/legacy_files/migrated/1899horstmannlippincottfindingaid.pdf) returned HTTP 403 to both curl and WebFetch. [C/LEAD] SETTLED BY: ten minutes from a browser.

• The 1987–1995 designation fight. SETTLED BY: Temple SCRC finding aid for the Center City Residents Association records, https://scrcarchivesspace.temple.edu/repositories/4/resources/997 (403 to fetchers). "Likely the best source."

• Whether any photograph of 1822 Pine or the 1800 block exists. "STILL UNKNOWN." phillyhistory.org's Search.aspx is an ASP.NET postback app returning an empty shell to every fetcher. "Ninety seconds in a browser answers it."

• The Hexamer & Locher plate number and its legend. Free Library of Philadelphia item IDs appear to run 11737 + plate number: 11770 = Plate 33, 11771 = Plate 34, 11775 = Plate 38; vol. 3 Index and Legend at https://libwww.freelibrary.org/digital/item/11766 . Cloudflare-blocked. "Ten minutes in a browser would pin the H&L plate number and capture the legend, which is needed before anyone interprets the paired dots drawn in each lot."

• The 1934 Appraisal Map letter grade for this block. JMB1934.Phila covers the block and carries red letter grades, but maxZoom is 17 against 19–20 for other layers and the scan disintegrates at the needed magnification. "NO GRADE WAS READ AND NONE SHOULD BE PUBLISHED without the plate in hand."

• The 1942 and 1962 Philadelphia land-use maps (Greater Philadelphia GeoHistory Network, served through atlas.phila.gov) would show this parcel's recorded use in both years — "the cleanest possible confirmation of the multi-family period." No parcel-level reading extracted; "reading a colour off a raster tile is not a citable claim on its own." Flagged as the highest-value remaining cheap task for GAP 1.

• The Dallin c.1930 aerial, Plate 9 (16,800 × 10,829). Fetched at full resolution around the computed position; rows, rear yards and party walls visible, individual houses not separable, no landmark in frame. "THE BLOCK WAS NOT CONFIRMED." SETTLED BY: georeferencing against Rittenhouse Square first. "It remains a real asset sitting inside the 1922-1941 gap."

• Whether a free Philadelphia Sanborn sheet for Pine between 18th and 19th exists. Penn's guide (https://guides.library.upenn.edu/sanborn) states the Philadelphia Sanborns are microfilm with online access only through subscription products. "Do not assume a free Philadelphia Sanborn sheet… exists until you have seen it."

• The 1899 $6,000 permit, the 1900 $1,200 Myres permit, and the 1905 fireplace permit. Historic building permits are not online; the City's dataset begins ~2007. Must be pursued at the City Archives or the Athenaeum in person.

• *Historic Rittenhouse: A Philadelphia Neighborhood* (Burke, McCauley, Sperr, Vaux; Penn Press, 1985; ISBN 9780812212020), pp. 44–45, cited by PAB FOR THIS HOUSE. Not on Internet Archive, no free full text. "THIS IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE UNCOLLECTED SECONDARY SOURCE FOR THE WHOLE PROJECT and it must be bought or borrowed."

• Chronicling America / LOC full-text for "1822 Pine" and for Brown, 1914–1922. UNTESTED, not exhausted — hard-blocked from this environment (308 redirect to www.loc.gov, then 403/429 behind a Cloudflare challenge to both curl and WebFetch). "Use a real browser." Likewise HathiTrust full-text search (403 to everything).

• The identity of the hospital at 1827-1831 Pine. Unresolved; targeted searches return only Pennsylvania Hospital at 8th and Pine.

• The archive's 1891-11-28 clipping. Three mutually incompatible descriptions exist in the repo (Spencer purchase / Ellen Rozet Drexel's death / the scarlet-fever report). All three are guesses, and the first cannot be true — the Spencer purchase is 1893. SETTLED BY: reading the clipping.

• The archive's 1871-01-14 clipping. It cannot be Annie McQueen Graham Spencer's death notice — she died 15 January 1871, the day AFTER the paper. Either the genealogical day is off or the clipping is unrelated. "Read it before assuming either."

• Whether the practice, and not just the house, passed from Brown to Pfeiffer in 1922. [C] — the sources show only the two addresses swapping in the same volume, with both men listed as Assistant Surgeon at Presbyterian Hospital. SETTLED BY: a memoir or practice record. Memoir citations exist but are not open-access: Duncan GG, "Memoir of Henry Paul Brown, Jr., 1888-1955," *Trans. & Stud. Coll. Phys. Phila.* 23(4):165–166 (1956), https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/13324950/ ; Deaver JM, "Memoir of Damon Beckett Pfeiffer. 1878-1966," *Trans. Stud. Coll. Phys. Phila.* 35:200 (1968), https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/4914878/ . Also cpparchives.org holds a "Bequest of Damon B. Pfeiffer" record — Cloudflare-blocked.

• Corroborating the firm "Roset & Bicking." It "appears nowhere on the open web except the phila.gov nomination quoting the 1870 Evening Bulletin obituary." SETTLED BY: HSP's Roset Family Papers or period business directories.

• Lucy Davis's Penn School trusteeship. Attested only by the 1914 *Woman's Who's Who*; the Penn School Papers finding aid at UNC (https://finding-aids.lib.unc.edu/catalog/03615) names no Davis at all. "Do not expect UNC to corroborate it cheaply."

• Martha Davis's Equal Franchise Society treasurership. Rests on the 1915 EPL notices. The Ann Lewis Suffrage Collection's 1912 EFS constitution is metadata plus page images only, no transcription — so no officer list without reading the images by hand.

• Unresolved directory lines flagged as not worth re-chasing: McElroy's 1857 "FRENCH WM. H., plasterer … h 192 Waverly" (number fits neither numbering on 17th-to-19th Waverly); 1857 "Smith Russel, landsc. painter, Waverly" (no number; tempting to connect to the painter Russell Smith, NOT confirmed); 1866 "Moore Marmaduke, 319 Walnut, h Waverly" (no number).

• Two Henry C. Davises in one Boyd's volume, neither at 1822 Pine: "Davis Henry C (John Davis & Sons), h Devon Pa" and "Davis Henry C, pres Barclay RR Co, Slate Line & Sullivan RR and Long Valley Coal Co, h Hotel Metropole." The anthracite-railroad one is tempting but nothing connects him to Henry Corbit Davis. "Open lead only; do not assert."


FREELY REPUBLISHABLE ASSETS
===================================================================
Everything below is public domain or an open government/institutional record, reachable free, with a direct URL. URLs are exact as given in the source files.

--- A. NEWSPAPER PAGES NAMING 1822 PINE (Pennsylvania Newspaper Archive, Penn State — Open ONI, public domain, no login, permalinkable) ---
Base: https://panewsarchive.psu.edu — carries *Evening Public Ledger* (Philadelphia), *The Evening Telegraph* (Philadelphia), *Daily Evening Bulletin* (Philadelphia). Working JSON search API: `/search/pages/results/?phrasetext=...&format=json` ; per-page OCR at `/lccn/…/seq-N/ocr.txt`. A phrase search on "1822 Pine" across the corpus returns exactly five pages.

1. *Evening Public Ledger*, Mon. May 7, 1917, p. 16 — chambermaid want-ad placed from 1822 Pine.
   https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/lccn/sn83045211/1917-05-07/ed-1/seq-16/

Free Philadelphia newspaper coverage map (the binding constraint — reproduce it, it is itself publishable context): *The Press* 1857-08-01 to 1865-10-14; *Daily Evening Bulletin* 1864-02-02 to 1870-10-08; *The Evening Telegraph* 1864-07-01 to 1871-06-30; *The American Presbyterian* 1860-1869; *Our Daily Fare* 1864-1865; *Evening Public Ledger* 1914-09-14 to 1922-12-30; *Gazette of the United States* and successors 1789-1801; three Italian-language papers 1917-1922. That is all. There is NO free full-text Philadelphia daily for 1802–1856, 1872–1913, or 1923 onward.

Method note that unlocks all of the above: Internet Archive has no working *global* full-text search endpoint. The method that works is per-item — download `archive.org/download/<id>/<id>_djvu.txt` and grep it. The per-item search-inside endpoint `https://{server}/fulltext/inside.php?item_id=…&q=…` also works and is how leaf/page numbers were resolved.

GUARDRAILS
===================================================================

DO NOT PUBLISH — FACTUAL

• Do not publish Sloan's room dimensions, or any Sloan specification, as this house's. Sloan's Design II is 25 ft front × 50 ft deep; 1822 Pine is 22 ft × 90 ft to Waverly. Sloan documents "the *type* and the *period expectation*, not this house." Frame any Sloan material explicitly as "the type, not this house."

• Do not publish a storey count as settled. The 1893/1899/1983 sources contradict each other. Flag the contradiction to the reader.

• Do not publish a National Register reference number for the Rittenhouse-Fitler Residential Historic District. Do-not-publish list: 92001878 (that is RittenhouseTown) and 81000557 (that is Rittenhouse Square the park). Agent 5's recommendation: publish "listed on the National Register in 1983 (per Hidden City Philadelphia)" or omit the NR claim entirely.

• Do not say "rooming house" or "boarding house" for 1822 Pine or the 1800 block of Pine. Nothing supports it; the 1950 census gives the house one dwelling-unit serial. Say "divided into apartments," which the 1950 census supports for this row.

• Do not publish "the oldest continuously-operating antiques district in the country" for Antique Row. No historical, municipal or trade-press source exists for that phrase. And if the site uses the word "district" for Antique Row it must say "informally known as" — there is no Historical Commission designation, no National Register listing and no ordinance creating or bounding it.

• Do not publish Pine Street's one-way direction. It "is not stated on any City page reached. Do not publish it from memory."

• Do not publish a 1934 Appraisal Map letter grade for this block. "NO GRADE WAS READ AND NONE SHOULD BE PUBLISHED without the plate in hand."

• Do not publish "parlor-floor doctor's office" as a fact. It "is a period-plausible inference from Philadelphia rowhouse practice, not a sourced fact about this house. It should be written as inference or dropped."

• Do not assert Guy Bryan Schott lived at, or is connected to, 1822 Pine. Agent 2's [A] grade on this is superseded by Agent 11's direct image read: it is 1522 Pine.

• Do not name the woman who died at 1822 Pine in May 1918 until the page image is checked — the surname is lost in the scan gutter.

• Do not conflate PAB's "Spencer Residence" (project 14707, 1822 Pine) with "Ervin, Spencer, Residence Alterations" (ho_display.cfm/75343), which is Spencer Ervin's house in Bala Cynwyd, 1925–27.

• Do not treat OPA as authoritative on physical description — its own record is internally inconsistent ("number_stories 2" against "ROW 4 STY MASONRY").

• Do not conclude from a null GeoHistory address search that a map does not exist.

• Absence from the Social Register "is never proof of anything on its own."

DO NOT PUBLISH — PRIVACY

• The 2025 transaction. "A further DEED, document 54492590, display date 2025-11-14, was recorded 2025-11-20 for a consideration of $1 — a routine intra-owner transfer adding a co-owner. FLAG: this is current-owner personal/financial information, and the mortgage rows alongside it are too… I would not publish the 2025 transaction, the mortgagees, or the co-owner's name without the owner's explicit say-so."

REQUIRED WORDING / FRAMING

• The 1950 census entry: "'no one home' means the enumerator failed to make contact. It does NOT prove the house was vacant, and it does NOT prove single occupancy — it only proves he assigned it one serial number and moved on. State it exactly that way."

• The 1941 zoning stamp inference: publish it as inference, grade B — "publish it as 'the ordinance that created the stamp, and what the stamp most likely means,'" not as fact.

• Eaton's Pine Street counts: "the 1800 block falls inside the seven blocks west of Broad, [but] Eaton does not enumerate the blocks, so say 'within the western count,' not 'on this block.'"

• Du Bois's canvass: 1822 Pine sits inside the stated Seventh Ward boundaries, but that is "a geographic inference from the stated boundaries, not a statement that this house was canvassed." Label it [B].

• The 1904 "At Home" clipping: the ritual is A-grade and Philadelphia-sourced; "the connection between the clipping and the ritual is inference, clearly labelled."

• The Waverly Street framing (Pine for callers, Waverly for coal, ice, ash and deliveries): "That framing is inference from the nomination's own statement plus the deed; label it."

• The 1917 chambermaid advertisement: "It also names race as a condition — which is precisely the 'importation'/exclusion pattern Eaton documented twenty years earlier on this street. Handle with care and context; do not sand it off." Be "honest about the ad's racial restriction."

• The 1912/1921/1929 physicians'-row counts: "publish with the numbers: these are counts from OCR'd text, hand-checked at the margins, so treat them as 'at least N,' not exact."

• The 1899 *Builders' Guide* text and the 1917/1918 EPL OCR: OCR is poor; "the page image must be checked before publication."

• On the Brown/Pfeiffer succession: that the *practice* passed with the house is [C]; the sources show only the two addresses swapping in the same year.

• On the Martha Tracy and Ellen Culver Potter identifications: [B], each resting on a single directory listing. Agent 6 notes this women's-history material "is *sound* in a way the suffrage-in-the-parlour claims are not" — it "does not require claiming anything about meetings inside 1822."

• On the "c. 1845" build date: it is a PHMC datum propagated through PAB, "a genuine competing date, not a listing-agent invention. The repo's directory argument for 1854 must engage with PHMC, not dismiss the figure as real-estate copy."

• On the designation-authority contradiction (Encyclopedia says district-designation power arrived only with the 1986 overhaul; the form is dated 9 Feb. 1987): "worth saying plainly" rather than resolving — the nomination was filed within months of the city first acquiring the power, then took eight years and two trips to the state Supreme Court.

• On the City's competing descriptions of the house: "Publishing the contradictions, with the query URLs, is more trustworthy than picking one."

METHOD GUARDRAIL

• "CROP EVERY ADDRESS BEFORE YOU BELIEVE IT. Three of nine strongest OCR leads changed meaning at the image step in one lens alone, and two of those three would otherwise have been published as facts about this house." No address digit goes on the site without someone looking at the page image.

• "NOTHING WAS WRITTEN INTO THE REPO. These will not survive the session — copy anything worth keeping into the repo now, before acting on any of the above." ## ARTICLE BRIEFS
Ranked by publish-value ÷ effort. Every one is buildable from assets listed above.

**1. "756 Becomes 1822: The Day Philadelphia Renumbered Pine Street"**
Material: Roset's own line in two consecutive volumes ("Roset John, mer., 756 Pine" 1857 → "Roset John, nier., 1822 Pine" 1858); "1822 Pine" occurs exactly once in the entire 1858 volume; nine same-name pairs on a constant +1066, flanked by Hone 754→1820 and Barnes 758→1824; the directory publisher's own house moving 740→1806 in his own book; the +1000/+1002/+1006 counter-examples from the 1700 block; the 1856 "Decimal"/Mascher ordinance name [B]. Two publishable scans a year apart.
Queries: "philadelphia street renumbering 1857", "756 pine street philadelphia", "philadelphia house numbers changed", "philadelphia decimal system street numbers"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Low.** *The single best-evidenced story the project owns, and it is currently unwritten.*

**2. "Who Actually Lived at 1822 Pine, 1855–1871"**
Material: the complete volume-by-volume occupant table with verbatim lines (Roset → Broadhead → the McNeills → S. Decatur Smith → Mary Cuthbert → Trimble → Howard Spencer), plus S. Decatur Smith printed at the address in a November 1866 newspaper, plus John Roset dying in West Philadelphia in 1870, plus the corroborating negative (zero Roset/Rozet near "Pine" in any Philadelphia paper 1857–1871).
Queries: "1822 pine street history", "who lived at 1822 pine street philadelphia", "rittenhouse rowhouse tenants 1860s"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Low–medium.** *Replaces the site's single largest factual error with something better.*

**3. "Not a Victorian Mansion: Nineteen Late Greek Revival Townhouses"**
Material: the two government inventory entries verbatim, side by side; the 1700-block entry that actually carries "c. 1845"; the real architectural distinction (paired doorways/modillioned cornice vs marble trim only); "1808, reduced one story" corroborated in today's OPA record; the arithmetic of nineteen.
Queries: "greek revival rowhouse philadelphia", "1800 block pine street architecture", "rittenhouse fitler historic district contributing"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Low.** *Blocker: nothing about architecture can publish until this ships.*

**4. "Suddenly, at 1822 Pine Street: 21 May 1918"**
Material: the Evening Public Ledger notice with its IIIF crop; the independent New-York Tribune notice; the Fair Hill register's "Died 5/21/18"; Naomi Lawton Davis's parentage and PA Death Certificate No. 52962; and the extraordinary register line showing her ashes interred 16 January 1945, twenty-six and a half years later.
Queries: "naomi lawton davis", "1822 pine street death 1918", "fair hill burial ground davis"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Low.** *Guardrail: strongly-indicated wording on the Mott descent, never "Lucretia Mott's grandson lived at 1822 Pine."*

**5. "Two Spencers: Father, Son, and a Naming Error That Swapped Two Obituaries"**
Material: the Wilmington obituary with its LOC IIIF crop; Gopsill's 1871 leaf 1354 vs McElroy's 1867 (same office, different house); the Find a Grave structured records for Howard 1821–1896, Graham 1852–1896, Agnes 1862–1921; the surname surviving as "Anne **Heward** Spencer" in 1921; and the consequence — two clippings in the archive assigned to the wrong man.
Queries: "graham spencer kaolin hockessin", "howard spencer feldspar delaware", "1822 pine street spencer family"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Low.** *Also a self-correcting-archive piece: the site publishes its own mistake and how it was caught.*

**6. "1823, Not 1822: How One Digit Almost Put a Suffrage Meeting in This House"**
Material: the April 1919 meeting notice with its crop (printed digits unmistakably 1823, OCR reads "1123"); the Guy Bryan Schott retraction (1522, two page images); the M. Errickson retraction (1322); and the rule that came out of it. Publishable negative-evidence crops for all three.
Queries: "ocr errors newspaper archive research", "house history research methods", "verifying historical addresses"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Medium.** *The most distinctive piece on the list — nobody else's house-history site publishes its own near-misses.*

**7. "Tickets, Not Meetings: What the Suffrage Record Actually Shows"**
Material: both January 1915 ticket notices (Martha Davis as EFS treasurer, 1822 Pine); the booth committee naming Caroline Katzenstein and the Dulles sisters; the EFS headquarters address printed two ways five days apart; the 1919 meeting at 1823; and next door — Dora Lewis of 1820 Pine funding the 1915 canvass, picketing, jailed, force-fed, and publicly disowned by Mrs. Lawrence Lewis Smith of Strafford. Plus the LOC portrait of her.
Queries: "philadelphia equal franchise society 1915", "dora lewis suffragist philadelphia", "rittenhouse square suffrage history"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Medium.** *Guardrail-heavy. The honest version is a better story than the fabricated one.*

**8. "The Doctor's House, 1919–1937"**
Material: Brown's 1921 AMD entry ("until 10", no separate office) and his Penn alumni line (Medical Corps June 1917–Jan 1919 — a demobilised Army surgeon took the house the year he came home); Pfeiffer 1922–1937 across fifteen Fellows-roll volumes; the one-year handover, surgeon-to-surgeon at Presbyterian Hospital, visible as two addresses swapping between v.43 and v.44; and the physicians'-row curve — 10 entries in 1912, 15 in 1921, 49 in 1929.
Queries: "1822 pine street doctor", "damon pfeiffer surgeon philadelphia", "henry paul brown jr philadelphia surgeon", "pine street doctors row philadelphia"
Grade: **A** (counts with an "at least N" caveat; the practice passing with the house is [C]). Effort: **Medium.** *Fills an era the site does not currently narrate at all.*

**9. "$14,000, $17,500, $6,000: The House's Money, 1893–1900"**
Material: the James A. Freeman auction of 10 May 1893 at the Philadelphia Exchange, "four-story residence, lot 22 x 90 feet, $14,000" — which explains the archive's own 1893 headline; the Wm McLean → Agnes M Spencer conveyance of 3 Apr 1899, $17,500; the July 1899 plans notice; the 2 August 1899 award to J. S. Rodgers of Stanwick, N.J. with the full scope line ("A new story on the front and rear, plumbing, cement work and considerable interior detail work"); "Cost about $6,000"; the September 1900 second campaign and the $1,200 Jacob Myres & Son permit. Plus the hard negative: four hits in the entire 1886–1940 run, all 1899–1900.
Queries: "duhring okie ziegler 1822 pine", "philadelphia builders guide 1899", "1822 pine street renovation history"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Low–medium.** *Kills the unsourced $25,000 and the "Rogers" spelling in one page.*

**10. "The Servants' House: What a Philadelphia Rowhouse Expected of the People Who Ran It"**
Material: Sloan 1859 on the service stair, the servants' hall, the dumbwaiter, four inside bells, a speaking tube from pantry to kitchen, and the hardware hierarchy (silver-plated at the front door, white porcelain on the first and second storeys, mineral in the basement and attic); Dreka 1873 and Ward 1878 on the reception day, the salver, the servant who screens callers, the P.P.C. card; Eaton 1899 on Pine Street's servant counts, the one woman who "does every single thing," and the butler's line about importing Englishmen; Du Bois on Thursday afternoons. Anchored to this house by exactly one document: the May 1917 chambermaid advertisement placed from 1822 Pine.
Queries: "victorian rowhouse servants philadelphia", "reception day calling cards 1870s", "philadelphia domestic service 1899"
Grade: **A for the type; the connection to this house is inference, clearly labelled.** Effort: **Medium–high.** *Guardrail: the 1917 ad names race as a condition — handle with care and context, do not sand it off.*

**11. "The Block in 1858"**
Material: the pre/post-renumbering roster of all nineteen houses verbatim; then the identifications the directory prints for itself — James Curtis Booth "melter & refiner, U. S. Mint" at 1813; William Parker Foulke at 1827 in the year of the Hadrosaurus dig, printed in capitals; Henry Coppée "prof. Penn. University" two doors west; Charles Short of Lewis-and-Short at 1808, in the house Commodore Geisinger had just left; Chapman Biddle at 1831; Joseph W. Drexel arriving at 1829 c. 1867; Meade one block north at 1836 Delancey. Plus the side-of-street correction: all but Coppée were across the road.
Queries: "1800 block pine street philadelphia residents", "james curtis booth us mint home", "william parker foulke hadrosaurus philadelphia", "charles short lewis and short philadelphia"
Grade: **A** (Biddle's Gettysburg identification and Duane/Allibone at [B]). Effort: **Medium.** *Charles Short is called "a genuinely new find and probably the most publishable of the extensions."*

**12. "1820 Pine Goes to Apartments, 1919"**
Material: the September 1919 "HOUSEKEEPING APARTMENTS / FINEST IN PHILADELPHIA" campaign with its open fireplace "to burn logs furnished by the owner," the 1921 $125/month follow-up, the fifteen dated 1820 Pine hits 1915–1922; then 1818 Pine's 1941-42 conversion under Louis Magaziner with drawings surviving at the Athenaeum; then the 1942 and 1962 land-use codes for 1822 itself, and 1824 next door which was **not** converted; then the 1950 census sheet showing the whole south side floor-by-floor with ground-floor doctors' offices, and 1822 alone with one serial and "no one home."
Queries: "1820 pine street apartments 1919", "philadelphia rowhouse apartment conversion 1920s", "rittenhouse square mansions divided apartments"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Medium.** *Reopens the "Apartment House Years" chapter 22 years earlier than it currently begins.*

**13. "The Street With No Front Doors: Waverly Behind 1822 Pine"**
Material: Waverly absent from the 1854 street index and present in 1855, the same year the row fills; the 1897 consolidation table with its decisive gap at 17th-to-18th and 18th-to-19th; the 1871 street index printing dashes where house numbers should be, against Addison's numbers one block south; six occurrences of "Waverly" in an 8.9 MB directory, all in indexes, zero residents; eight AIS address points in 2026, all at 1800 and 1802; zero assessed properties; the City's own 1995 line, "Houses on both sides of the street face onto Pine or Addison Streets." Plus the correction it forces: no stables behind this house.
Queries: "waverly street philadelphia history", "philadelphia alley streets renamed 1897", "rittenhouse carriage house waverly"
Grade: **A.** Effort: **Medium.** *A whole page built out of well-documented absence — and it retires a piece of site copy.*

**14. "Addison Street, One Block South, in 1871"**
Material: the twenty-five verbatim 1871 directory lines — ten waiters, seven drivers, two barbers, two porters, a steward, a coachmaker; the modern OPA fabric (32 parcels, uniformly ROW 3 STY MASONRY, 14–16 ft frontage); then Du Bois in 1899 identifying Addison as the centre of "one of the best Negro residence sections of the city," and his passage on 16th-to-18th; then Eaton's Pine Street split, 42 per cent of the street's colored domestics in the seven blocks west of Broad.
Queries: "addison street philadelphia history", "seventh ward du bois philadelphia negro", "philadelphia servant streets 19th century"
Grade: **A** for every directory line and every Du Bois/Eaton quote; **[B]** for anything connecting them to this house. Effort: **Medium.** *Guardrail: the directory does not state race. Publish the occupations verbatim and let Du Bois supply the identification separately. That is the honest construction.*

**15. "What the Plates Show: 1858 to 1962, Lot 22 × 90"**
Material: Hexamer & Locher c.1858–60 — a four-story front block, a three-story back building, open ground to Waverly, and the whole row drawn identically, i.e. a purpose-built speculative row; 1885 Bromley — built to the rear line with a narrow light court on the western party line; 1895 and 1910 showing the same, which puts the rear-built condition at least fourteen years before Duhring, Okie & Ziegler; 1922 with "22", "90", "4"; and the finding that 1885 frontages match today's OPA frontages house for house, no lot subdivided or merged in 141 years.
Queries: "1822 pine street floor plan history", "bromley atlas philadelphia 1885 pine street", "philadelphia rowhouse back building"
Grade: **A** for the plate readings; **[B]** for the 1860–1885 bracket. Effort: **High** — and note the plates are **not** free to republish; this piece must run on description, links, and cleared images only.
