## GUARDRAILS
Consolidated. Every one of these is a rule, not a suggestion.

### The suffrage rules
**G1. NO SUFFRAGE MEETINGS IN THIS HOUSE.** The record shows tickets SOLD from the address and tax resistance BY a resident. Four unread archive synopses and two marketing lines assert meetings here. "Those must not propagate."
**G2. THE APRIL 1919 NOTICE IS ADDRESSED 1823 PINE.** "**This document must not be used to claim a suffrage meeting inside 1822 Pine.**" It is nevertheless the strongest thing yet found on Martha Davis's own political life "and it belongs on the site with the address printed exactly as it appears."
**G3. THE DECEMBER 1913 TAX-RESISTANCE QUOTE IS ON THE DO-NOT-PUBLISH LIST** while /history/suffrage renders it as a full pull quote. Resolve the contradiction before anything else on that page changes.
**G4. LUCY DAVIS IS NOT A SUFFRAGIST.** Her Woman's Who's Who entry does not carry the volume's "Favors woman suffrage" tag; every other DAVIS entry on that page and the next was checked, and several do. "She is a Quaker reformer at this address, not a suffragist."

### The naming and attribution rules
**G5. NEVER "DREXEL LIVED HERE."** Required phrasing: "documented family connection."
**G6. QUOTE THE GRAHAM SPENCER OBITUARY AS PRINTED.** "Careful: the phrase is 'the residence of his relatives,' not 'his residence.' Quote it as printed; do not upgrade it to 'his home.'"
**G7. DO NOT PUBLISH "LUCRETIA MOTT'S GRANDSON LIVED AT 1822 PINE."** The join is circumstantial and partly circular — no document names *this* Henry C. Davis at 1822 Pine, and the load-bearing strand leans on a repo date the repo itself flags as poorly sourced. Publish as "strongly indicated, not yet confirmed."
**G8. DO NOT SAY JOHN McCREA BUILT 1822 PINE.** The nomination places him on the 1700 block of Pine and Addison and the 1800/2000 blocks of Delancey. "That is not permission to say he built 1822 Pine." PAB attributes no builder and no architect to the 1800 block. And "M'Crea James A., M.D., 1825 Pine" (1860) is a physician, not evidence about the builder.
**G9. DO NOT CITE PAB FOR THE ARCHITECTS**, and do not publish "the house is in PAB as the Spencer Residence" without a specific `pj_display.cfm` project URL. Never use `ho_display.cfm/75343`.
**G10. DO NOT GUESS WHAT COMPANY HOWARD SPENCER WAS PRESIDENT OF.** The 1871 directory says only "pres" with an office at 233 S 3d.
**G11. DO NOT PUBLISH A YEAR FOR THE BOYD'S HOWARD SPENCER CITATION** until the title page is read.
**G12. DO NOT PUBLISH THE AGNES-SPENCER-OWNED-IT-TO-1921 HYPOTHESIS AS FACT.** "It is the hypothesis to test first" — nothing more, until the rotated 1922 card is read.
**G13. ELLEN ROZET: PUBLISH 1832, FOOTNOTE 1829, AND DELETE "TWIN."** Flagging the year in-line is explicitly **not** sufficient.
**G14. TRUST FIND A GRAVE'S STRUCTURED FIELDS, NOT ITS BIO PROSE.** "Find a Grave's free-text bios contradict its own structured fields on this family… Trust the structured fields; treat the bio prose as a lead only."
**G15. DO NOT PUBLISH A DAY FOR CHAPMAN BIDDLE'S DEATH** (Dec 9 vs Dec 29, 1880) **OR GEORGE GORDON MEADE'S** (Nov 6 vs Nov 7, 1872).
**G16. DO NOT PUBLISH "GENERAL BIRNEY LIVED ON THE 1800 BLOCK OF DELANCEY."** The 1871 listing is his widow.
**G17. DO NOT PUBLISH EITHER ROSENBACH 2008-DELANCEY DATE** (Jan 1993 vs "since 2003") without checking.
**G18. THE 1905 FIREPLACE ATTRIBUTION IS UNSAFE AS PUBLISHED.** "The site attributes this fireplace to the Davis era without accounting for Purviss at all" — and it is the site's most-repeated physical detail, invoked in six chapters.

### The address and OCR rules
**G19. NO ADDRESS DIGIT GOES ON THE SITE WITHOUT SOMEONE LOOKING AT THE PAGE IMAGE.** "Three of my nine strongest OCR leads changed meaning at this step, and two of those three would have been published as facts about this house."
**G20. DO NOT LET "GUY BRYAN SCHOTT" INTO THE NARRATIVE.** Both page images read **1522**. "Recording this here so the next researcher does not re-find it and publish it."
**G21. DO NOT CONVERT THE 1866 SEWING-MACHINE TESTIMONIAL INTO A TENANCY DATE.** "'in Use for Five Years and Upwards' describes the machine, not the residence."
**G22. DO NOT NAME THE WOMAN WHO DIED AT 1822 PINE IN MAY 1918 FROM OCR ALONE** — the surname sits in the PSU scan gutter. (Lens 03's crop and the New-York Tribune notice satisfy this; cite them, not the PSU OCR.)
**G23. FLAG DISCREPANCIES IN-LINE RATHER THAN SILENTLY PICKING ONE:** the EFS headquarters ("33 South 9th" vs "35 South 8th"); John Roset's death (Aug 8 vs "died yesterday" Aug 9, 1870); the ward (deeds recite Eighth, census is Seventh); Dr. Jopson's middle initial (H. vs R.).

### The building and data rules
**G24. DO NOT PUBLISH A STOREY COUNT AS SETTLED.** The house is a "four-story residence" in 1893, yet in 1899 the architects add "a new story on the front and rear," and the 1983 NR describes the row as 4-story. Three readings are possible. **Flag the contradiction to the reader.**
**G25. DO NOT PUBLISH THE OPA "1800" AS A BUILD DATE.** The City itself flags it `year_built_estimate = "Y"`. Publishing the fact that the record is flagged as an estimate is fine; publishing the number as a date is not. The Redfin "circa 1845" figure is barred on the same grounds.
**G26. DO NOT PUBLISH THE TRAILING "DATE" COLUMN VALUES** from the OPA-compliant Register list (1800 → 1840, 1834 → 1845, 1836 → 1845) as construction dates. Source unestablished. [C]
**G27. DO NOT CITE OPA's `number_stories` FIELD.** It says 2 for 1822 and 3 for 1824 while both carry "ROW 4 STY MASONRY." More generally, do not treat OPA as authoritative on physical description — its own record is internally inconsistent.
**G28. GEOHISTORY MAP PLATES ARE NOT FREE TO REPUBLISH.** Link to the plate pages and the viewer; clear any image you want to host. Locally rendered derivatives inherit the restriction.
**G29. DO NOT PUBLISH AN INTERPRETATION OF THE HEXAMER & LOCHER PAIRED DOTS.** Not confirmed against the atlas legend. The storey numerals ("4.", "3.") are unambiguous and safe.
**G30. DO NOT USE THE 1862 SMEDLEY PLATE AS EVIDENCE THE REAR ELL WAS ABSENT** — it hatches a band along the Pine frontage only. Likewise 1875 Hopkins generalises building masses and must NOT be read as the rear building having been removed.
**G31. DO NOT PUBLISH A 1934 APPRAISAL-MAP GRADE FOR THIS BLOCK.** "NO GRADE WAS READ AND NONE SHOULD BE PUBLISHED without the plate in hand."
**G32. DO NOT CLAIM THE DALLIN c.1930 AERIAL SHOWS THIS BLOCK.** Not confirmed; no landmark fixes the frame.
**G33. DO NOT CONCLUDE FROM A NULL GEOHISTORY ADDRESS SEARCH THAT A MAP DOES NOT EXIST.** Its address search is demonstrably incomplete — it does not return Hexamer & Locher, Smedley, or the 1895 Bromley for this address even though all three cover it in the viewer.
**G34. THE 1808 / 1810 IN THE INVENTORY ENTRY ARE ADDRESSES, NOT YEARS.**
**G35. THE 1995 CITY INVENTORY TRANSCRIPTION IS A RECONSTRUCTED WORD ORDER.** The PDF's text layer interleaves the two printed columns; every word is present verbatim but the ORDER is a reconstruction, independently confirmed against the 1983 NR wording. **Say so if publishing the transcription.**
**G36. THE 1863 McELROY'S ABSENCE IS NOT EVIDENCE OF VACANCY.** The volume is ~25% smaller than its neighbours — a volume/OCR gap.
**G37. DO NOT PUBLISH MARY CUTHBERT AS A SECOND HOUSEHOLD.** The line is genuine [A]; her status is unresolved [C].
**G38. BLOCK-NEIGHBOUR DIRECTORY LINES vs IDENTIFICATIONS.** A line carrying its own occupational identifier IS the identification. Where the directory gives only a name, grade lower and say so — Allibone and Duane are "probable, not proven."
**G39. SAY "DIVIDED INTO APARTMENTS," NEVER "ROOMING HOUSE" OR "BOARDING HOUSE."**
**G40. THE 1950 CENSUS "no one home" WORDING IS PRESCRIBED:** "'no one home' means the enumerator failed to make contact. It does NOT prove the house was vacant, and it does NOT prove single occupancy — it only proves he assigned it one serial number and moved on. **State it exactly that way.**" The reading itself is Grade B: clear cellophane tape crosses columns 2–5 on sheet 38 and the house numbers sit slightly low in their rows.
**G41. THE 1941 ZONING STAMP INFERENCE IS GRADE B.** Publish it as "the ordinance that created the stamp, and what the stamp most likely means," not as fact.
**G42. ABSENCE FROM THE SOCIAL REGISTER "is never proof of anything on its own."**

### The neighbourhood-context rules
**G43. DO NOT SAY "Penn named it Pine Street in 1683" AND CITE THE 1683 LETTER.** The word "Pine" does not occur anywhere in Penn's pamphlet. The naming attribution is secondary-source only.
**G44. THE "UN-QUAKERLY RENAMING" ANECDOTE MUST BE TOLD AS A STORY ABOUT THE STREET GRID, NOT A SOURCED STATEMENT ABOUT PINE.** The PHMC marker text lists "Chestnut, Cranberry, Locust, Mulberry, Strawberry, 'Wallnut,' and Vine" — Pine is not in the list, and no date is given.
**G45. DO NOT PUBLISH THE 1800-BLOCK ADDISON DIRECTORY HOUSEHOLDS AS BLACK HOUSEHOLDS.** The directory does not state race. Publish the occupations verbatim; let Du Bois supply the identification separately.
**G46. DO NOT WRITE THAT 1822 PINE ITSELF WAS SURVEYED BY ISABEL EATON.** [B] — the block was inside the survey area; the house being surveyed is not established. Say "within the western count," not "on this block." Likewise Du Bois's canvass: 1822 Pine sits inside the stated ward boundaries, "a geographic inference from the stated boundaries, not a statement that this house was canvassed."
**G47. IF YOU USE DU BOIS'S "WAVERLY STREET… FAIRLY SWARMS" QUOTATION, SAY EXPLICITLY THAT IT IS ABOUT THE 1100 BLOCK, NOT THE 1800 BLOCK.**
**G48. DO NOT CALL THIS BLOCK RAMCAT.** 1822 Pine is at 18th; Ramcat/Schuylkill (NRHP #86000055, listed January 8, 1986) begins at 23rd.
**G49. DO NOT CLAIM A PERIOD SOURCE FOR "GOOSETOWN" OR "RAMCAT."** Neither appears in Cohen 1922 nor in the 8.9 MB Gopsill's 1871.
**G50. DO NOT PUBLISH "the oldest continuously-operating antiques district in the country."** No historical, municipal, or trade-press source. And if the site says "district" for Antique Row it must say "informally known as."
**G51. DO NOT PUBLISH PINE STREET'S ONE-WAY DIRECTION FROM MEMORY.** Not stated on any City page reached.
**G52. STATE THE FEDERAL DISTRICT RELATIONSHIP AS "WITHIN THE BOUNDARY AS DESCRIBED," NOT "FEDERALLY LISTED."** 1822 Pine is not enumerated in the 1983 federal document.
**G53. NR-NUMBER DO-NOT-PUBLISH LIST:** 92001878 (RittenhouseTown) and 81000557 (Rittenhouse Square the park). Use the Philadelphia Register date, 2/8/1995.
**G54. VERIFY BEFORE ASSERTING:** St. Peter's Episcopal Church (Third and Pine) details are [B] from Wikipedia, unchecked against NPS. Old Pine Street Church's "only Colonial Presbyterian church in Philadelphia still on its original foundation" rests on partisan/tertiary sources [B].
**G55. THE NICHOLSON MARKER AT 1805 PINE IS FLAGGED "Missing."** Do not send a reader to look at it. And Nicholson is commemorated there for the museum he founded, not because he lived there.

### The type-vs-this-house rule
**G56. DO NOT PUBLISH SLOAN'S ROOM DIMENSIONS, OR ANY SLOAN SPECIFICATION, AS THIS HOUSE'S.** Sloan's Design II is 25 ft front × 50 ft deep; 1822 Pine is 22 ft × 90 ft. Sloan documents "the *type* and the *period expectation*, not this house." Frame every use explicitly.
**G57. THE 1904 "At Home" CONNECTION IS INFERENCE.** The calling ritual is A-grade and Philadelphia-sourced; the link to the 11 Dec 1904 Inquirer line is [B], clearly labelled.
**G58. THE WAVERLY FRAMING (Pine for callers, Waverly for coal, ice, ash and deliveries) IS INFERENCE** from the nomination's own statement plus the deed. Label it.
**G59. "PARLOR-FLOOR DOCTOR'S OFFICE" IS NOT A SOURCED FACT.** Write as inference or drop. [C]
**G60. THE BROWN→PFEIFFER PRACTICE SUCCESSION IS [C].** The sources show only two addresses swapping in the same volume.
**G61. THE MARTHA TRACY AND ELLEN CULVER POTTER IDENTIFICATIONS ARE [B]**, each resting on a single directory listing. This women's-history material "is *sound* in a way the suffrage-in-the-parlour claims are not" — it "does not require claiming anything about meetings inside 1822."
**G62. THE PHYSICIANS'-ROW COUNTS ARE "AT LEAST N," NOT EXACT.** OCR-derived, hand-checked at the margins. Publish with the caveat.
**G63. THE 1917 CHAMBERMAID ADVERTISEMENT NAMES RACE AS A CONDITION** — "precisely the 'importation'/exclusion pattern Eaton documented twenty years earlier on this street. Handle with care and context; do not sand it off."
**G64. ON "c. 1845": IT IS A GENUINE COMPETING DATE, NOT A LISTING-AGENT INVENTION.** "The repo's directory argument for 1854 must engage with PHMC, not dismiss the figure as real-estate copy."
**G65. PUBLISH THE CONTRADICTIONS, WITH THE QUERY URLS.** Where the City's own records disagree about this house, "publishing the contradictions, with the query URLs, is more trustworthy than picking one."

### The invention rule
**G66. THE 1913-02-09 SYNOPSIS IS AN INVENTION.** "Do not cite it, do not repeat it, and do not treat [it] as a finding." The same applies to the one-line synopsis on every one of the 41 never-read documents: "It is generated prose."

### The do-not-confuse list
**G67.** "Spencer Ervin" of Bala Cynwyd (PAB holding 75343) is a man named Spencer Ervin. · "Spencer Carriage House and Stable" is "an historic structure located in the Dupont Circle neighborhood of Washington, D.C.," built 1905 — nothing to do with this house or family. · The Boyd's directory contains two Henry C. Davises, neither at 1822 Pine — "Davis Henry C (John Davis & Sons), h Devon Pa" and "Davis Henry C, pres Barclay RR Co, Slate Line & Sullivan RR and Long Valley Coal Co, h Hotel Metropole"; the anthracite-railroad one is tempting — "I am not asserting it. Treat as an open lead only." · Mary W. [wife of —], Anthony Tarter and Margaret Thackray appear in the 1891 clipping only because they are adjacent death notices in the same column — they are **not** residents.

### Privacy
**G68. THE 2022 PROPERTY CERTIFICATE** (raw_documents/1822 Pine - Property Cert.pdf, PC-2022-009391, owner PRICE ROBERTSON) is intentionally excluded from the public archive, and the site says so.
**G69. DO NOT PUBLISH THE 2025 TRANSACTION.** "A further DEED, document 54492590, display date 2025-11-14, was recorded 2025-11-20 for a consideration of $1 — a routine intra-owner transfer adding a co-owner. **FLAG:** this is current-owner personal/financial information, and the mortgage rows alongside it are too… I would not publish the 2025 transaction, the mortgagees, or the co-owner's name without the owner's explicit say-so."

## STILL OPEN, AND WHAT WOULD SETTLE IT

### Cheapest, highest value — do these first

**S-1. WHO OWNED THE HOUSE 1922–1967, AND WHO SIGNED THE 1854 DEED.**
Settled by: **rotating the ten Philadelphia Bureau of Engineering deed-registry card JPGs 180° and re-OCRing them.** The handwritten owner names, dates and deed-book references "are physically present in the scans and have simply never been read." Files already in `raw_documents/`. Named as the single cheapest high-value task available. Closes 1922–1952 in one pass and gives the 1854 McCrea→Roset conveyance a readable source.

**S-2. DID AGNES SPENCER OWN THE HOUSE CONTINUOUSLY 1899–1921, MAKING THE DAVISES HER TENANTS?** [B, labelled inference]
Agnes took title 3 Apr 1899 and died 24 Dec 1921; the 1922 card carries "Spencer," a husband-and-wife pair, and a date fragment. "The obvious reading is an estate conveyance following her death." If true it also dissolves the G. C. Purviss problem into an ordinary owner-of-record-vs-occupant situation.
Settled by: **reading the rotated `1922-deed-abstract-1` card.**

**S-3. THE LOAD-BEARING JOIN — IS THE HENRY C. DAVIS OF 1822 PINE LUCRETIA MOTT'S GRANDSON?**
Stated honestly: no document names *this* Henry C. Davis at 1822 Pine. Three circumstantial strands, and the load-bearing one leans on a date the repo itself flags — "so the argument is partly circular."
Settled by, **in one step: Philadelphia Inquirer, 22 May 1918, page 17** — the exact citation from Naomi Davis's Find a Grave memorial. Same paper, same Newspapers.com workflow as the rest of the archive. **It should also name the surviving children and settle which Davis woman did what outright.**

**S-4. THE 24 UNREAD CLIPPINGS.** All retrievable by Newspapers.com image ID. Highest-value single item: **1896-11-23 Philadelphia Times p.11, npcom 53387473** — almost certainly Graham Spencer's death notice. Read 1896-06-29 (npcom 53320902's neighbour, Howard Sr.) first.

**S-5. IS THE 1891-11-28 CLIPPING ANYTHING?** Three incompatible repo descriptions, all guesses, and the first cannot be true. Settled by: reading npcom 168413629.

**S-6. THE 1871-01-14 CLIPPING.** Tempting as Annie McQueen Graham Spencer's death notice — but it is dated the day **before** her recorded death of 15 Jan 1871. "Either Find a Grave's day is off, or the clipping is unrelated. Read it before assuming either." Settled by: npcom 169198203.

**S-7. "IS22" vs "1822" — THE 1901 WEDDING.** Until the digits are confirmed against the page image, the wedding is not proven to have happened at this address. Settled by: cropping npcom 168316181.

**S-8. THE 1900 SECOND CONSTRUCTION CAMPAIGN.** The archive holds an unread Inquirer clipping dated 1900-10-03 (npcom 168086532) sitting exactly on that window. Settled by: transcribing it, and reading the Builders' Guide page image for 2 Aug 1899 p.489 — the unread remainder after "A new story" may also carry a settled cost. Ten minutes, free.

**S-9. ARE THE FIVE FLOOR PLANS HISTORIC OR MODERN MARKETING DOCUMENTS?** No survey date, draughtsman or provenance recorded. "A five-minute question that nobody has asked."

**S-10. THE DATE OF THE BOYD'S VOLUME PLACING HOWARD SPENCER AT 1822 PINE.** IA dates it 1858; internal evidence brackets it c. 1894–1900. Settled by: reading the title page from the page images of `boydscopartnersh01boyd` (imagecount 1682). *Note: Gopsill's 1871 leaf 1354 independently supplies a firmly dated Howard Spencer occupancy, so this is a nice-to-have, not a blocker. The two lenses disagree on whether the repo's "in 1871" date was ever sourced; leaf 1354 decides it.*

**S-11. THE BUILDERS' GUIDE VOLUME NUMBER for the 8/2/1899 notice.** PAB reads "v. 24, n. 31"; the IA series reads v.14. Settle from the volume title page before citing.

**S-12. DOES PAB PROJECT 14707 RESOLVE, AND WHAT IS ON ITS ARCHITECTS TAB?** Two lenses disagree. Re-check https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/pj_display.cfm/14707 directly.

**S-13. THE PORTRAIT SITTER.** The nomination's caption pairs "John Jacob Roset" with "Mary Ann Lanning Roset" — "the sitter is either John Roset or his father Jacob." Needs one check.

### Needs a browser — a human with Chrome closes these in an hour

**S-14. DOES A PHOTOGRAPH OF 1822 PINE OR THE 1800 BLOCK EXIST?** "STILL UNKNOWN." phillyhistory.org's Search.aspx is an ASP.NET postback app returning an empty shell to every fetcher. "**Ninety seconds in a browser answers it.**"
https://www.phillyhistory.org/PhotoArchive/Search.aspx?action=link&type=address&address=1822+PINE+ST

**S-15. WHO OCCUPIED 1822 PINE BETWEEN 1872 AND 1935?** No free full-text Philadelphia city directory exists for any year after 1871 on Internet Archive — the run is McElroy's 1837–1867 plus Gopsill's 1871, then nothing. **Named as the highest-value single unblocked task remaining.**
Settled by: HathiTrust Full View Boyd's Philadelphia combined city and business directories — **1887 (hvd.hx6aaz), 1893 (hvd.hx6aax), 1894 (hvd.hx6ab9), 1895 (hvd.hx6aaj), 1900 (hvd.hwrff5)** — blocked by Cloudflare to tooling, readable by a human in minutes. Or Ancestry, FamilySearch, or the Free Library's digital collections.

**S-16. BOYD'S BLUE BOOK — the society directories that print reception days and country residences.** HathiTrust IDs for 1887-88, 1889, 1890, 1896, 1898, 1900, 1906: **hvd.hxnyj7, hvd.hxisbs, hvd.hxisbm, hvd.hxisbp, hvd.hxisbn, mdp.39015035098444, hvd.hxisbr.** All 403 to fetchers. "A human with a browser closes this in an hour." Would settle what the 1904 "At Home" notice implies about a stated reception day at this house.

**S-17. THE HEXAMER & LOCHER PLATE NUMBER AND ITS LEGEND.** Free Library item IDs appear to run 11737 + plate number (11770 = Plate 33, 11771 = Plate 34, 11775 = Plate 38); vol. 3 Index and Legend at https://libwww.freelibrary.org/digital/item/11766. Cloudflare-blocked. "**Ten minutes in a browser would pin the H&L plate number and capture the legend, which is needed before anyone interprets the paired dots.**" The atlas is Volume 3 (1858), plates 30–42.

**S-18. HMDB MARKER INSCRIPTIONS.** hmdb.org returned Cloudflare 403 to every approach. "Someone should open the marker pages in a browser and transcribe them."

**S-19. THE 1987–1995 DESIGNATION FIGHT.** Temple SCRC finding aid for the Center City Residents Association records, https://scrcarchivesspace.temple.edu/repositories/4/resources/997 (403 to fetchers). "Likely the best source." Context worth publishing plainly: the nomination was filed within months of the city first acquiring district-designation power, then took eight years and two trips to the state Supreme Court.

**S-20. SERVANT WAGES IN A COMPARABLE PHILADELPHIA HOUSEHOLD.** HSP Collection 1899, Horstmann–Lippincott family papers; secondary descriptions state the papers include "account books of household expenses, 1864; servant's wages, 1856–1896." UNVERIFIED — the finding aid PDF (https://hsp.org/sites/default/files/legacy_files/migrated/1899horstmannlippincottfindingaid.pdf) returned 403 to both curl and WebFetch. [C/LEAD] "Ten minutes from a browser."

**S-21. CHRONICLING AMERICA / LOC FULL TEXT — UNTESTED, NOT EXHAUSTED** by one lens (308→403/429 behind Cloudflare). Another lens reached LOC fine and should be the model. Death notices still sitting free and unread: **Foulke (June 1865), Meade (November 1872), Chapman Biddle (December 1880)** — all likely carrying street addresses. Called "the highest-value unblocked lead I am leaving behind."

### Needs a login, a purchase, or a trip

**S-22. THE 1900, 1910, 1920, 1930 AND 1940 CENSUSES FOR THIS ADDRESS.** Blocked by FamilySearch's login gate and by the absence of a free 1940 IIIF endpoint (NARA's 1940 site is retired; the Catalog API returns only the SPA shell and v2 needs a key). "**The 1910 census is one of the two records that would close the Davis/Mott question.**" The 1950 method works and is documented.

**S-23. THE 1880 CENSUS ENTRY FOR 1822 PINE.** A bounded ~50-page window remains: reel 1170, between **n648 and n700**. Settled by paging `https://archive.org/download/populationsc18801170unit/page/n<N>_w2000.jpg` through that window. Method warning to carry forward: **the 1850, 1860 and 1870 federal censuses have no street-address column.** There is no address-based route into the Roset decades — find the household by name, and for 1859–1871 the names are the **tenants** (Broadhead / McNeill / Smith / Trimble / Spencer), not "Roset."

**S-24. WHO WAS AT 1822 PINE IN 1950?** The enumerator wrote "no one home" with a partly legible cross-reference "See S[heet] __ Line __", and the call-back entry was not located among the other 61 sheets. Settled by: reading the remaining sheets of ED 51-158.

**S-25. DID WAVERLY BETWEEN 18TH AND 19TH EVER ACQUIRE RESIDENTS AFTER 1871?** Cheapest route: the 1950 census EDs **51-158** (north side) and **51-164** (south side).

**S-26. *HISTORIC RITTENHOUSE: A PHILADELPHIA NEIGHBORHOOD*** (Burke, McCauley, Sperr, Vaux; Penn Press, 1985; ISBN 9780812212020), **pp. 44–45, cited by PAB FOR THIS HOUSE.** Not on Internet Archive, no free full text. "**THIS IS THE HIGHEST-VALUE UNCOLLECTED SECONDARY SOURCE FOR THE WHOLE PROJECT and it must be bought or borrowed.**"

**S-27. A PUBLISHED BOOK ON THIS FAMILY, UNPURSUED.** "Grace Mather-Smith and Kelley, Russell P., III, *My Darlings: A Memoir* (Rowman & Littlefield, 2018), 144," cited by the phila.gov nomination for the Rozets' Vienna years. "An obvious next stop and would likely settle several of the above."

**S-28. HISTORIC BUILDING PERMITS ARE NOT ONLINE.** The City's open dataset begins ~2007. The 1899 $6,000 permit, the 1900 $1,200 Jacob Myres & Son permit and the 1905 J. N. Long fireplace permit must be pursued in person at the City Archives or through the Athenaeum. This is also the only route left to **G. C. Purviss** and to **which floor or room the doctor's office occupied**.

**S-29. THE DECEMBER 1913 TAX-RESISTANCE QUOTE.** Cannot be closed with free newspapers (the EPL run starts 1914-09-14). Settled by: Newspapers.com, or the NAWSA / Caroline Katzenstein papers.

**S-30. WAS 1822 PINE ALREADY A MEDICAL ADDRESS BEFORE BROWN ARRIVED IN 1919?** *American Medical Directory* 1916 and 1918 are not freely available anywhere reached; the 1914 IA scan is a partial with no 1822 Pine entry; the 1956 edition (`bwb_T3-BRL-425`) is lending-restricted. Settled by: the 1916 or 1918 volume.

**S-31. WHETHER THE PRACTICE, AND NOT JUST THE HOUSE, PASSED FROM BROWN TO PFEIFFER.** [C]. Memoir citations exist but are not open-access: Duncan GG, "Memoir of Henry Paul Brown, Jr., 1888-1955," *Trans. & Stud. Coll. Phys. Phila.* 23(4):165–166 (1956), https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/13324950/ ; Deaver JM, "Memoir of Damon Beckett Pfeiffer. 1878-1966," *Trans. Stud. Coll. Phys. Phila.* 35:200 (1968), https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/4914878/ . cpparchives.org holds a "Bequest of Damon B. Pfeiffer" record — Cloudflare-blocked.

**S-32. "ROSET & BICKING" AS A FIRM.** Appears nowhere on the open web except the phila.gov nomination quoting the 1870 obituary. Settled by: **HSP's Roset Family Papers (Collection 1897)** or period business directories. The [B] inference that Ellen **Bicking** Rozet's middle name is the partner's name is not independently corroborated.

**S-33. LUCY'S AND MARTHA'S PARENTAGE.** [C — explicitly speculation]. Neither appears in the Fair Hill register (all 3,573 rows searched; 26 Davis burials, none of them), in Woman's Who's Who beyond Lucy's own entry, or as children on either of Henry's Find a Grave marriages. The testable inference: Henry married Martha Mellor in 1862 and she died 1874; a Lucy who took a Vassar A.B. in 1885 was born c. 1863–65, inside the first marriage — **which would make Naomi Lawton Davis their STEPmother.** Settled by: the 22 May 1918 Inquirer obituary, or a census.

**S-34. LUCY DAVIS'S PENN SCHOOL TRUSTEESHIP.** Attested only by the 1914 *Woman's Who's Who*; the Penn School Papers finding aid at UNC (https://finding-aids.lib.unc.edu/catalog/03615) names no Davis at all. "Do not expect UNC to corroborate it cheaply."

**S-35. MARTHA DAVIS'S EFS TREASURERSHIP** rests on the 1915 EPL notices. The Ann Lewis Suffrage Collection's 1912 EFS constitution is metadata plus page images only, no transcription — so no officer list without reading the images by hand.

**S-36. DENNIS CLARK, "Ramcat and Rittenhouse Square," in *The Divided Metropolis: Social and Spatial Dimensions of Philadelphia, 1800–1975*** — the root of everything published about Ramcat — was reached only through Ken Finkel's summaries. Someone with library access should get Clark's text itself.

### Open questions with no cheap route

**S-37. WHO BUILT THE 1800 BLOCK OF PINE?** PAB attributes NO builder and NO architect to the three block-level records (1800-1806 = 277172, 1808-1836 = 277174, 1803-1825 = 277170); all are "(untitled)" with a PHMC date and nothing else. Settled by: the deed chain behind the April 1854 conveyance, or the City Archives / Athenaeum pre-1889 building records.

**S-38. WHAT WAS THE HOSPITAL AT 1827-1831 PINE?** Both inventories say "altered c. 1950 for hospital" and PAB types the record "dwelling, hospital," but no source names the institution. Targeted searches return only Pennsylvania Hospital at 8th and Pine. Settled by: 1950s city directories, L&I records, or the Inquirer.

**S-39. DOES A FREE PHILADELPHIA SANBORN SHEET FOR PINE BETWEEN 18TH AND 19TH EXIST?** loc.gov was unreachable (403 / Cloudflare 429). Penn's guide states the Philadelphia Sanborns are microfilm with online access only through subscription products (ProQuest Sanborn Maps Geo Edition; Digital Sanborn Maps 1867–1970 Pennsylvania). "**Do not assume a free Philadelphia Sanborn sheet exists until you have seen it.**" If retrying: look for the `sanborn07905` series and its index map/street index.

**S-40. WHETHER THE BUILDERS' GUIDE HOLDS THE APARTMENT-CONVERSION RECORD — DIRECT DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN LENSES.** One reports no "1822 Pine" hits in 1905, 1922, 1923 or 1924 and recommends "a systematic sweep of all 40 free IA volumes… would very likely close GAP 1." Another reports a full-run full-text search of 1901–1940 returning exactly four hits, all 1899–1900 — "The apartment conversion is not in the city's richest building-trade source" — while still calling an unblocked systematic sweep "the single most likely thing to close Gap 1 from the trade press."
Settled by: **a volume-by-volume, whitespace-tolerant sweep of `PhilaBuildersGuide_v{N}_{year}` for v.15 (1900), v.36 (1921) and v.43 (1928)** — the missing volumes neither lens could search.

**S-41. THE STOREY COUNT.** Open contradiction, explicitly flagged. Settled by: the 1899 permit itself, or a dated elevation/plan.

**S-42. THE 1934 APPRAISAL MAP GRADE** (`JMB1934.Phila`). Covers the block and carries red letter grades, but maxZoom is 17 against 19–20 for other layers and the scan disintegrates. Settled by: the source scan in hand.

**S-43. THE c.1930 DALLIN AERIAL, PLATE 9** (16,800 × 10,829). Rows, rear yards and party walls visible; individual houses not separable; no landmark in frame. Settled by: georeferencing against Rittenhouse Square first. "It remains a real asset sitting inside the 1922-1941 gap."

**S-44. THE 1995 CITY NOMINATION'S NARRATIVE SECTIONS (pp. 1-33)** are image-only with no text layer. The 1983 federal nomination supplies the equivalent narrative in searchable text.

**S-45. IS HENRY COPPÉE AT 1826 PINE THE SAME MAN AS LEHIGH'S FIRST PRESIDENT?** The directory says only "prof. Penn. University." Needs an independent source.

**S-46. WAS MARY CUTHBERT A LODGER, A RELATIVE, OR A DIRECTORY ERROR?** [C]

**S-47. MARTHA MELLOR DAVIS** is listed by Find a Grave as buried at Fair Hill but does not appear in the Fair Hill register — though her parents do ("Mellor | Martha | 1880, 02/23 | 67 | Sec E | Lot 123" and "Mellor | Thomas | 1882, 03/28 | 74 | Sec E | Lot 123"). Discrepancy logged, unresolved.

**S-48. THE ROSENBACH 2008 DELANCEY DATE.** A search summary of the Hidden City piece said the foundation purchased 2008 Delancey in January 1993; rosenbach.org says "since 2003." Irreconcilable from what was reached.

**S-49. WHY THE 1824-03-26 CLIPPING IS IN THE ARCHIVE AT ALL.** Predates the house by ~30 years; content entirely unknown; "why it was clipped is unrecorded."

**S-50. UNRESOLVED DIRECTORY LINES — LISTED SO NOBODY RE-CHASES THEM.** McElroy's 1857 "FRENCH WM. H., plasterer & plaster paris ornament manuf., 18th and Chestnut, h 192 Waverly" (the number 192 fits neither the old nor new numbering on 17th-to-19th Waverly) · McElroy's 1857 "Smith Russel, landsc. painter, Waverly" (no number; tempting to connect to the painter Russell Smith, **not confirmed**) · McElroy's 1866 "Moore Marmaduke, 319 Walnut, h Waverly" (no number).

### Site-governance questions, not research

**S-51. DUPLICATE DOMAINS.** 1822pine.com and rittenhouseresidence.com both indexed with near-identical titles; canonical unresolved.
**S-52. THE EVENTS POLICY CONTRADICTION.** The "Events & Filming" story chapter is a full micro-wedding and film-shoot playbook; site policy in llms.txt and the FAQ is no parties/events.
**S-53. PINE STREET'S ONE-WAY DIRECTION.** Check the Streets Department.

---

## RESEARCH METHOD NOTES
So the next pass does not repeat dead ends.

### What is reachable, and how

**Pennsylvania Newspaper Archive (Penn State), https://panewsarchive.psu.edu — fully open, no login, permalinkable, public domain.** The workhorse of the whole pass.
- Search API: `/search/pages/results/?phrasetext=…&format=json` and `?proxtext=…&proxdistance=N&format=json`
- Page OCR: `/lccn/…/seq-N/ocr.txt`
- Word bounding boxes: `/lccn/…/seq-N/coordinates/` (gzipped JSON; **coordinate space is 4× the JP2**)
- IIIF crop pattern: `https://panewsarchive.psu.edu/iiif/<batch-path>.jp2/<x>,<y>,<w>,<h>/1720,/0/default.jpg`

**Library of Congress / Chronicling America.**
- Working search: `https://www.loc.gov/collections/chronicling-america/?q=…&fo=json`
- **The legacy `chroniclingamerica.loc.gov/search/pages/results/?format=json` API is DEAD — it 308s to a www.loc.gov 404. Do not use it.**
- Page OCR: `resource.fulltext_file` in the LOC JSON
- Word coordinates: `word-coordinates-service` with `format=alto_xml` and **no** `full_text` flag
- IIIF crop: `https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/<service-id>/<x>,<y>,<w>,<h>/full/0/default.jpg` — **request size `full`, not an upscale, or you get a blurred low-res tile**
- **LOC was hard-blocked for one lens (308 → 403/429 behind a Cloudflare challenge to both curl and WebFetch) and worked fine for another. Retry it; do not conclude it is down.**

**RUN BOTH SEARCH ENGINES.** LOC's search is exact-token against raw OCR and "a single garbled character kills the hit." PSU's `proxtext=…&proxdistance=N` is far more forgiving and **found three EPL pages LOC missed entirely.**
**Corollary — a count conflict to be aware of:** one lens reports "a phrase search on '1822 Pine' across the corpus returns exactly five pages." Another lens surfaced at least six PSU pages naming the address, including the 1915-01-08 page. **Treat "exactly five" as a floor produced by phrase search, not a ceiling.**

**Internet Archive — the single most productive source in the pass.**
- **The method that works is per-item, not global:** download `https://archive.org/download/<identifier>/<identifier>_djvu.txt` and grep it. **Any digitised directory can be searched this way.**
- The per-item search-inside endpoint `https://{server}/fulltext/inside.php?item_id=…&q=…` also works and is how leaf/page numbers were resolved.
- Page images fetch directly at `https://archive.org/download/<id>/page/n<N>_w2000.jpg` (follow redirects).
- **The global full-text search API `https://ia-pub-fts-api.archive.org/search/v1/scrape` returned nothing. Do not use it.**
- `advancedsearch` API is the right tool for checking what volumes exist (used to establish that the free Philadelphia directory run is McElroy's 1837–1867 plus Gopsill's 1871 and nothing else).
- **md5-verify downloaded text files against fresh archive.org downloads** before quoting line numbers. Two lenses did this and it is why the Roset renumbering pair is unassailable.

**Sites that reject WebFetch or bare curl but serve fine to curl with a Chrome User-Agent:** phila.gov, npgallery.nps.gov, philadelphiabuildings.org, philageohistory.org. **philageohistory.org via WebFetch returns 403 — don't conclude the site is down.** The big phila.gov / npgallery PDFs also exceed WebFetch's 10 MB limit but download fine with a UA.

**pypdf is installed**, so any nomination PDF with an OCR layer becomes a greppable text file in one command. **Caveat:** the 1995 City PDF's text layer interleaves its two printed columns — words arrive out of order; and its embedded OCR is badly garbled in places ("1822" renders as "L822"), so **verify by rendering the page image** with pdfium.

**PAB (philadelphiabuildings.org):**
- `search_location.cfm` is **behind a login**.
- `pj_search_results.cfm` **no longer exists**.
- Project pages are public once you have an id. Pine Street ids cluster around **75400–75900**.
- **The route that works:** the browse-by-block view — `https://www.philadelphiabuildings.org/pab/app/view_street.cfm/64820?PJ_GRID_BLOCKNUMBER=18` — "39 records displayed."

**GeoHistory:**
- **Its address search is INCOMPLETE.** It does not return the 1858-60 Hexamer & Locher, the 1862 Smedley, or the 1895 Bromley for this address **even though all three demonstrably cover it in the viewer.** Check the viewer layer list too.
- The viewer parses `?layers=`, `?c=lat,lng`, `?z=` — deep links are reproducible.
- Raw-image retrieval at survey resolution works through the WMS `GetMap` endpoint by swapping `LAYERS=`; BBOX in World-Mercator metres.
- The 1933 zoning layer (`PRD1933.Phila`) returns a **pure white image** for this block (mean RGB 255,255,255, one unique colour). Skip it.
- The 1962 Land Use sheet is georeferenced about **half a lot off** from the 1942 sheet in the mosaic; a **+0.000043° longitude correction** lands the lot lines on the strip edges.

**City of Philadelphia open data — all reachable, all quotable, all directly linkable.** CARTO SQL (`https://phl.carto.com/api/v2/sql`) for `opa_properties_public`, `permits`, `business_licenses`, `rtt_summary`; ArcGIS FeatureServer for AIS address points and PWD parcel polygons. **Publish the query URLs alongside the claims** — it is more trustworthy than paraphrasing, especially where the City's own records disagree.

**1950 census:** the IIIF endpoint and the JSON search API both work and are free. The API searches **by name and enumeration district, not by street address** — which is why the ED-description route (bounded by street names) is the way in.

**phillyhistory.org Historic Street Index** is an ASP.NET form, **queryable by POST**. The **PhotoArchive** Search.aspx is a different animal — an ASP.NET postback app returning an empty shell to every fetcher.

### What is hard-blocked

| Source | Failure mode |
|---|---|
| **HathiTrust** (full-text search and all Boyd's/Blue Book volumes) | 403 to everything |
| **hmdb.org** (marker inscriptions) | Cloudflare 403 to every approach |
| **Free Library of Philadelphia digital collections** | Cloudflare-blocked; a browser gets in |
| **FamilySearch** (1900–1940 censuses) | login gate |
| **NARA 1940 census** | site retired; Catalog API returns only the SPA shell; v2 needs a key |
| **Temple SCRC finding aid** (CCRA records) | 403 |
| **HSP finding aid PDF** (Horstmann–Lippincott) | 403 to curl and WebFetch |
| **cpparchives.org** (Pfeiffer bequest) | Cloudflare-blocked |
| **phillyhistory.org PhotoArchive** | JS-rendered ASP.NET postback; empty shell to fetchers |
| **Newspapers.com** | paywall; the Inquirer and the Times are in no free full-text archive |
| **loc.gov** (for one lens) | 308 → 403/429 Cloudflare; worked for another lens |
| **philageohistory.org via WebFetch** | 403; works via curl with a browser UA |

### Dead ends — proven, do not repeat

1. **"756 Pine" in any newspaper.** Zero hits across the entire PSU archive and all of Chronicling America. Structural coverage gap, not absence. **"DO NOT RUN THIS SEARCH AGAIN."**
2. **Maps for the build date.** "NO PROPERTY-LEVEL MAP EXISTS BETWEEN 1843 AND 1858… MAPS CANNOT DECIDE THE BUILD DATE. Stop looking."
3. **A former name for Waverly at 17th–19th.** There isn't one. "Stop hunting."
4. **G. C. Purviss in free newspapers.** "Purviss returns only unrelated 1845/1857/1889 items."
5. **G. C. Purviss in the medical directories.** No Purvis/Purviss physician anywhere in Pennsylvania in 1912, 1921 or 1929.
6. **1913 Philadelphia suffrage coverage, free.** The EPL run begins 1914-09-14 and no other free Philadelphia daily covers 1913.
7. **Internet Archive's global full-text search API.** Returns nothing.
8. **The legacy Chronicling America JSON API.** Dead.
9. **The 1933 zoning map layer for this block.** Pure white.
10. **A period source for "Goosetown" or "Ramcat."** Zero in Cohen 1922, zero in the 8.9 MB Gopsill's 1871.
11. **Address-based census searching before 1880.** The 1850, 1860 and 1870 schedules have no street-address column.
12. **PAB's location search and `pj_search_results.cfm`.** Login-gated and gone, respectively.

### The one method rule that matters most

**CROP EVERY ADDRESS BEFORE YOU BELIEVE IT.** Three of nine strongest OCR leads changed meaning at the image step in a single lens, and two of those three would otherwise have been published as facts about this house: Guy Bryan Schott (1522, not 1822 — including a funeral supposedly held here), M. Errickson (1322, not 1822), and the Martha Davis suffrage meeting (1823, not 1822). **"This is the strongest argument in the entire pass for the rule that no address digit goes on the site without someone looking at the page image."**

### And one housekeeping warning carried forward from the extracts

**NOTHING FROM THIS PASS WAS WRITTEN INTO THE REPO.** The scratchpad files listed in Assets §13 will not survive the session. Copy anything worth keeping into the repo **before** acting on any of the above.
